# Code & AI-instruction review — 2026-08-21

Multi-agent review (workflow `wf_e2f2eb8a-11f`): 10 reviewers (5 instruction-doc groups, 5 code dimensions), one adversarial verifier per findings batch, coverage critic. 74 raw findings.

## Executive summary

**Verdict:** the port is in good shape — no showstoppers found in the graft itself — but the review surfaced a handful of real, previously unknown code bugs on the active 1014D build, one systematic seam class (pecostm32 `int16` fields kept as Atlan4 `uint16`), and heavy staleness in the instruction docs, whose newest "Known issues" state is six weeks behind the code.

**Numbers:** 74 raw findings; 71 survived adversarial verification, and the coverage critic overturned one of the 3 refutations (the F25-guard finding at `fpga_control.c:631` is in fact correct), so the working set is **72 confirmed**. Both variants were build-tested after the review: 1013D and 1014D compile clean, 1014D rebuilt last (`bootloader_1014d_base.bin` at 0x8000).

**Top code findings (new, on the current 1014D build):**

1. **`uint16` wrap class** (imported pecostm32 code assumes `int16` fields; this tree keeps Atlan4 `uint16`): `traceposition` wraps on rotary-down at the bottom with fast move speed — trace jumps bottom→top (`sm_1014d.c:1571`, high); time-cursor positions wrap the same way (`sm_1014d.c:292/309`, medium). Fix pattern is one signed local before the clamp.
2. **Roll-mode half of F25 still dead**: `fpga_set_long_timebase()` never sends `0x28/0x01`, and the stock capture wants it (plus `0x0D`) per read cycle — long timebases still run the FPGA in fast-mode select (`fpga_control.c:599`, high). Both FPGA_NOTES and PORT_AUDIT misattribute the commented write to `fpga_set_time_base`, which contains no 0x28 at all.
3. **Two more F16-class missing pushes**: v/div change never re-pushes the trigger level although the FPGA level is a function of volt/div (`sm_1014d.c:1553`); channel-enable toggle leaves the FPGA trigger source on a disabled channel — `fpga_swap_trigger_channel()` has no 1014D caller (`sm_1014d.c:1511`). Both freeze normal/single-mode acquisition until an unrelated key pokes the FPGA.
4. **Buffer seams**: `MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE` 16382 vs 16380-byte `uint32[4095]` arrays — 2-byte overruns in the math/reference paths (`variables.h:529`); USB CDC debug command `f<number>` sets `totalsamples` unbounded → huge acquisition-buffer overrun from the host side (`usb_interface.c:1542`).
5. **Wave-view seam**: `sm_set_time_base` dropped `!waveviewmode` from the Atlan4 condition it mirrors — timebase turns in wave view corrupt the viewed capture's sample rate and re-arm (`sm_1014d.c:1480`).
6. **F25 restore is `#if PORT_1014D`-guarded** — the 1013D variant keeps the sawtooth regression its own upstream never had (`fpga_control.c:631`).
7. Smaller: 1014D `get_fattime` returns 0 (invalid FAT timestamps on every save), unguarded RTC repaint block reachable via restored config, X-Y mode negative-index reads, capture-geometry numbers off 2× (per-ADC vs interleaved) in FPGA_NOTES/ROADMAP/analyzer, acqprobe reads CH1 unconditionally while the analyzer divides by enabled channels.

**Instruction-file rot (the "AI instruction review" half):** CLAUDE.md's Known-issues section is frozen at 2026-07-12 — F26–F35, both open bugs, and the entire capture-geometry thread are missing (high). FPGA_NOTES' command table still calls 0x28 dead, contradicting its own SOLVED section; its claim that the 1014D build never sends generator commands 0x50–0x52 is false (they go out every boot from `main()`, which also weakens the ROADMAP-14 hang hypothesis). Both FPGA_NOTES and the bring-up memory claimed `fpga/` is tracked — it is entirely untracked, a real data-loss hazard for the only copy of the built bitstream. CLAUDE.md tells the reader to rebuild `mksunxi`/`flashfilepacker` "from the checked-in .c sources" — no such sources exist in the tree (critic finding). AGENTS.md's display-geometry rules predate F17's 705-wide blit. ROADMAP still queues the solved sawtooth A/B (1b) and calls the built FPGA retarget "real remaining work" (16).

**Critic's next-pass list (what this review did NOT cover):** `test.c` (the actual roll-mode implementation, ~1400 lines, essentially unread — most relevant file to the open roll defect); USB mass-storage runs raw SD writes in IRQ context against the same card FatFs uses (unaudited); the CPU-infrastructure layer (D-cache enable is inert without an MMU on ARM926 — the data side runs uncached, a silent performance loss; hard-wired stacks with no guard); three tracked loader source trees at repo root nobody assessed; FatFs/SD/display_lib only byte-diffed vs Atlan4, not audited.

### Roll-mode 0x28/0x01 mode-select still missing (F25's other half), and FPGA_NOTES points the fix at the wrong function

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:599`
- **Severity:** high

F25 restored the 0x28/0x00 mode-select for short timebases in fpga_do_conversion, but the roll-mode half is still Atlan4-commented-out: fpga_set_long_timebase() only sends 0x0F/0x01 (trigger disable) while the 0x28/0x01 write remains dead code at lines 598-600 ('0X28 not support in FPGA???'). The stock-FPGA reference shows roll mode needs 0x28=0x01, and needs it (plus a 0x0D rate write) before EVERY 0x24/0x26 read cycle, not just once at mode entry — the current long-mode read loop (scope_get_long_timebase_data -> fpga_average_trace_data) sends neither, so on stock fw_FPGA==1 the FPGA stays in the fast-mode select (0x00, last written by fpga_do_conversion) for all long timebases. Bench already calls roll rendering 'glitchy on this unit' (sm_1014d.c:1449-1450 comment). Additionally FPGA_NOTES.md documents this follow-up but names the wrong function twice ('restore roll-mode 0x28/0x01 in fpga_set_time_base') — fpga_set_time_base is the 0x0E function sent on every short-timebase change; adding 0x28/0x01 there would actively break short mode.

**Evidence:** fpga_control.c:595-600: 'fpga_write_cmd(0x0F); fpga_write_byte(0x01); ... //fpga_write_cmd(0x28); //0X28 not support in FPGA??? //fpga_write_byte(0x01);'. pecostm32-RE/'FPGA explained/FPGA explained.txt':76 '0x28 ... 0x00 for all time base settings below 100mS. 0x01 for all from 100mS and up' and :202 '0x28 is written 0x01 after or before every read sequence. 0x0D is also written'; '100mS_50S-div_signal_data_read_sequence.txt':64-82 shows the repeating cycle 0x0D(0x07D0) -> 0x28/0x01 -> 0x24 read 10 -> 0x26 read 10. FPGA_NOTES.md:193 and :201 both say the commented write is 'in fpga_set_time_base'; it is actually in fpga_set_long_timebase (fpga_control.c:576-601). fpga_do_conversion writes 0x28/0x00 (fpga_control.c:639-640) but is only called on the short-mode path (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:391-414 routes long_mode to scope_get_long_timebase_data in test.c:824, which never writes 0x28/0x0D).

**Suggested action:** Fix FPGA_NOTES.md:193/201 to name fpga_set_long_timebase. When implementing the follow-up, mirror the stock capture: send 0x0D + 0x28/0x01 per roll-mode read cycle (e.g. in scope_get_long_timebase_data or fpga_average_trace_data), not only once in fpga_set_long_timebase; bench-verify against the 100mS_50S capture.

**Verifier:** Every cited fact reproduced. fpga_control.c:595-600: fpga_set_long_timebase sends only 0x0F/0x01 with the 0x28/0x01 write dead-commented ('0X28 not support in FPGA???'). pecostm32-RE 'FPGA explained.txt':76 and :202 say exactly what is quoted, and '100mS_50S-div_signal_data_read_sequence.txt':61-82 shows the repeating stock cycle 0x0D(0x07D0) -> 0x28/0x01 -> 0x24 x10 -> 0x26 x10. The current long-mode path never sends either per cycle: fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:391-393 routes long_mode to scope_get_long_timebase_data (test.c:824), whose read loop (test.c:950-957) calls only fpga_average_trace_data, which writes just settings->averagecommand (fpga_control.c:1440); 0x0D is pushed once at mode entry (sm_1014d.c:1486) but 0x28/0x01 never, so the stock FPGA's last mode-select remains the 0x28/0x00 from fpga_do_conversion (fpga_control.c:639-640). The doc error is real: FPGA_NOTES.md:193 and :201-202 both name fpga_set_time_base, but that is the 0x0E short-timebase function (fpga_control.c:552-572) called on every short-timebase change (sm_1014d.c:1475) and in the fw_FPGA>1 reset window (fpga_control.c:688) — adding 0x28/0x01 there would select roll mode during short timebases. The dead code actually lives in fpga_set_long_timebase (fpga_control.c:576-601). PORT_AUDIT F25's own follow-up note also flags the roll-mode restore as pending (and repeats the same wrong function name). The 'glitchy on this unit' bench comment is at sm_1014d.c:1449-1450 as cited. Whether the FPGA strictly requires 0x28/0x01 per cycle is a hardware question, but the divergence from the silicon-tied reference protocol is proven and the repo's own doctrine (match reference, never invent) makes the conclusion follow.

*(from code-gotchas)*

### uint16 traceposition wraps on rotary-down: channel trace jumps bottom-to-top

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/sm_1014d.c:1571`
- **Severity:** high

sm_set_channel_position() does `settings->traceposition += speedvalue;` then clamps with `< VERTICAL_POINTER_POS_MIN` / `> VERTICAL_POINTER_POS_MAX`. The code was imported from pecostm32's 1014D statemachine.c where traceposition is int16 (reference FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/variables.h:358), but this tree keeps Atlan4's `uint16 traceposition` (variables.h:605). With the position clamped at the bottom (VERTICAL_POINTER_POS_MIN=1, variables.h:176) and fast move speed (speedvalue=-10, set at sm_1014d.c:122 from movespeed, MOVE_SPEED_FAST=10 in statemachine.h:102), 1+(-10) stores as 65527; the `<1` test is false, the `>399` test (VERTICAL_POINTER_POS_MAX=TRACE_MAX_HEIGHT=399, variables.h:149/177) is true, and the position is clamped to 399. Concrete failure: hold the channel-position rotary down at fast speed — when the trace reaches the bottom, the next detent slams it to the top of the screen instead of stopping. Repeats cyclically. Port-introduced seam (Atlan4's touch UI only ever assigned absolute on-screen values to this field, so uint16 was safe there).

**Evidence:** sm_1014d.c:1571 `settings->traceposition += speedvalue;`, guards at 1574 `if(settings->traceposition < VERTICAL_POINTER_POS_MIN)` and 1579 `else if(... > VERTICAL_POINTER_POS_MAX)`; variables.h:605 `uint16 traceposition;`; pecostm32 reference variables.h:358 `int16 traceposition;` with byte-identical handler logic (reference statemachine.c:1463-1480); variables.h:176-177 POS_MIN=1, POS_MAX=TRACE_MAX_HEIGHT; variables.h:146-149 TRACE_MAX_HEIGHT=399; speedvalue int8 set negative at sm_1014d.c:121-122.

**Suggested action:** Compute in a signed local (int32 pos = settings->traceposition + speedvalue; clamp; store back), or change tagChannelSettings.traceposition to int16 as in pecostm32 after auditing all consumers (display code like scope_preset_values 442-traceposition, config save/restore).

**Verifier:** Independently reproduced every link: sm_1014d.c:1571 '+= speedvalue' with wrap-then-clamp guards at 1574/1579; traceposition is uint16 in this tree (variables.h:605) but int16 in the pecostm32 source of the handler (reference variables.h:358; handler at reference statemachine.c:1463 with the same +=/clamp logic); VERTICAL_POINTER_POS_MIN=1/MAX=TRACE_MAX_HEIGHT=399 (variables.h:176-177, 146/149); speedvalue is int8 (variables.h:1518) set to -10 at fast speed (sm_1014d.c:121-122, MOVE_SPEED_FAST=10 statemachine.h:102). Holding the rotary down at fast speed always lands the position in 1..10, and the next detent computes 1..9 + (-10) which wraps to >=65526, fails the <1 test, passes >399, and clamps to 399 — the opposite end. Handler is wired to the CH1/CH2 position rotaries (sm_1014d.c:907/912/1022/1027). Atlan4's own uint16 (Atlan4 variables.h:450) was safe because its touch UI assigns absolute values (e.g. statemachine.c:6124), so this is a genuine port seam.

*(from doc-fpga-boot)*

### Generator commands ARE sent by the 1014D build every boot

- **Where:** `FPGA_NOTES.md:73`
- **Severity:** high

The doc claims the generator commands 0x50-0x52 are 'reachable only from the 1013D touch UI paths (statemachine.c ~3600, generator.c); the 1014D build never sends them today', and builds the ROADMAP-14 hypothesis on it ('the boot hang is consistent with an unknown command wedging the stock FPGA's parser'). In reality main() calls fpga_on_off_generator() unconditionally in both variants, and that function sends 0x51 (freq), 0x52 (duty), and 0x50 (on/off) in BOTH the enable and disable branches — so the 1014D build sends all three generator commands to the stock FPGA at every boot, and boots fine on the bench (per the docs' own hardware-working status). The claim was already wrong when written: the call has been in main() since the pristine Atlan4 vendor commit 9daa91f. This also directly undercuts the parser-wedge hypothesis for the scope_generator_settings() hang (that function is pure UI drawing, no FPGA writes — menu.c:2538+).

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:183 `fpga_on_off_generator();` (unguarded, in main() before the PORT_1014D-conditional blocks); fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:241-297 — both branches of fpga_on_off_generator() call fpga_set_generator_freq (cmd 0x51, line 278), fpga_set_generator_duty (cmd 0x52, line 293), then `fpga_write_cmd(0x50)` (lines 249/260); `git log -L183,183:fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c` shows the line introduced by vendor commit 9daa91f; Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:161 has the same call. scope_generator_settings (menu.c:2530+) contains only display_* calls, no fpga_* writes.

**Suggested action:** Rewrite the paragraph at FPGA_NOTES.md:72-78: 0x50-0x52 are sent once at every boot from main() on both variants (with gen_enable from config, default 1); only the interactive generator UI is 1013D-only. Re-examine the ROADMAP-14 'unknown command wedges the stock FPGA parser' hang hypothesis, since the commands demonstrably go out each boot without wedging.

**Verifier:** Independently reproduced: fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:183 calls fpga_on_off_generator() unguarded in main() (surrounding PORT_1014D guards close at line 132 and reopen at 197), and fpga_control.c:241-264 sends 0x51 (line 278), 0x52 (line 293), and 0x50 (lines 249/260) in BOTH the enable and disable branches — so the 1014D build sends all three generator commands to the stock FPGA at every boot. git log -L183 traces the call to vendor commit 9daa91f (pristine Atlan4 has it at line 161), so FPGA_NOTES.md:72-73's 'the 1014D build never sends them today' was wrong when written. scope_generator_settings() (menu.c:2530-2721) contains only display_* calls; its fpga_set_generator_* calls were already commented in the vendor commit (git log -L2630,2640 shows them born commented), so the parser-wedge hypothesis for its hang (lines 77-78) has no mechanism — and the scope demonstrably boots and runs on bench while sending 0x50-0x52 with disable values.

### fpga/ tree (built 1014D-retarget bitstream + TD toolchain) is absent from CLAUDE.md

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/CLAUDE.md:47`
- **Severity:** medium

CLAUDE.md enumerates every other repo-root directory (six vendor trees, bench/, tools/) but never mentions fpga/ — the 2026-07-18 FPGA thread's own work product: a complete self-contained Anlogic TD project (fpga/AL3_1014D/) that retargets Atlan4's AL3 design to the stock 1014D board, builds headless on this server, and has a BUILT-but-NEVER-FLASHED bitstream in out/. Its README also carries a safety-critical warning (Atlan4's own bitstream must never be flashed unmodified — pin functions differ between boards). A fresh session would not know the retarget project or the working headless TD flow exists and could redo the work or treat Atlan4-FPGA/ as the only TD project.

**Evidence:** ls repo root shows fpga/ containing AL3_1014D and README.md; git status header shows '?? fpga/'. fpga/README.md:1–11: '# fpga/ — 1014D retarget of Atlan4's AL3 replacement-FPGA design… **Status 2026-07-18: BUILT, NEVER FLASHED.**… Atlan4's own bitstream must never be flashed unmodified'. CLAUDE.md sections 'Reference trees' (47–71) and 'Local bench data and host tools' (73–83) list every other root dir but not fpga/.

**Suggested action:** Add an fpga/ bullet (e.g. under the local-tools section): untracked local TD project, status built-never-flashed, pointer to fpga/README.md and FPGA_NOTES.md §TD rebuild, and the never-flash-Atlan4's-bitstream warning.

### AGENTS.md display-geometry rules stale after F17 (705-wide 1014D blit, per-frame sidebar redraw)

- **Where:** `AGENTS.md:157`
- **Severity:** medium

AGENTS.md teaches: the cleared rect (2,48,728,432) is what gets blitted, and 'Chrome at y < 48 ... or x >= 730 (right measurement column) persists without per-frame redraw.' On the current 1014D build this is wrong twice: (1) the per-frame blit was narrowed to (2,48,705,432) by F17 (commit 77af34b, 2026-07-10), so anything drawn into displaybuffertmp at x 707..729 never reaches the screen — a developer following AGENTS.md's 'must be redrawn INSIDE this function if x < 730' rule and placing chrome at x=710 would get invisible chrome; conversely on-screen content at x >= 707 (not 730) is what survives the blit; (2) the right measurement column does NOT persist without per-frame redraw — ui_update_measurements() is now called every frame after the blit (F12). The adjacent chrome-call list (AGENTS.md:159-164) also omits ui_draw_outline() (drawn first) and ui_update_measurements(). AGENTS.md was last committed 2026-07-10 23:59 (204201d), same day as the code change, and never caught up.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/scope_functions.c:3578 'display_fill_rect(2, 48, 728, 432);' (clear, still 728-wide); scope_functions.c:3780-3788 '#if PORT_1014D ... display_copy_rect_to_screen(2, 48, 705, 432); ... ui_update_measurements();' with comment 'the trace window ends at x=706 ... Copying Atlan4's full 728-wide trace buffer wiped the sidebar edge'; scope_functions.c:3768 'ui_draw_outline();' opens the 1014D chrome block; 1013D path keeps 728: scope_functions.c:3794. git log -S"705, 432" → 77af34b 2026-07-10 'GUI seam fixes F10-F17'; AGENTS.md last commit 204201d 2026-07-10.

**Suggested action:** Rewrite the 'clears rect' paragraph to state: clear is (2,48,728,432) into displaybuffertmp, but the 1014D blit is (2,48,705,432) — visible trace area ends at x=706, chrome for x>=707 must be drawn to the visible screen (or via ui_update_measurements), and the sidebar IS redrawn every frame. Add ui_draw_outline() and ui_update_measurements() to the chrome-call list.

**Verifier:** Independently reproduced all evidence: scope_functions.c:3578 still clears (2,48,728,432) but the 1014D blit at :3785 is display_copy_rect_to_screen(2,48,705,432) (1013D path at :3794 keeps 728), so chrome drawn into displaybuffertmp at x 707..729 never reaches the screen — AGENTS.md:157's 'x < 730 must be redrawn / x >= 730 persists' rule is wrong for the current 1014D build. ui_update_measurements() is called every frame at :3788 and ui_draw_outline() opens the chrome block at :3768; AGENTS.md:159-164's chrome list omits both (grep: 0 hits). Git confirms the timeline: the 705 blit landed in 77af34b (2026-07-10 23:58:59, 'GUI seam fixes F10-F17'); AGENTS.md was last committed in 204201d 16 seconds later and never caught up. One phrasing nuance does not change the verdict: sidebar content at x>=707 still mechanically survives the blit (nothing wipes it), so 'persists' is not strictly false — but the boundary is 707 not 730, and the column is in fact redrawn every frame, so the doc's geometry teaching is stale exactly as claimed.

### PORT_AUDIT §4 checklist item 1 prescribes the pre-F21 timebase behavior (8↔11 skip) that F21 replaced with 6↔11

- **Where:** `PORT_AUDIT.md:223`
- **Severity:** medium

Hardware-verify checklist item 1 tells the bench tester to exercise 'the long/short boundary (the 20/50 ms skip at indices 8↔11)'. F21 (2026-07-11, commit 87cf94b) deliberately changed that boundary: roll is kept for ≤500 ms only and 'crossing jumps 6↔11, making those four settings sweep-only' — i.e. 200/100/50/20 ms are no longer long-timebase and there is no 8↔11 skip any more. The checklist was never updated, so following it verbatim would make a tester report the intended F21 behavior as a regression. (The checklist item is also largely de facto exercised: the F21 bench session stepped the dial through this exact region.)

**Evidence:** PORT_AUDIT.md:221-224 vs PORT_AUDIT.md:444-448 ('the sm_set_time_base() boundary now keeps roll for ≤500ms only (crossing jumps 6↔11), making those four settings sweep-only'); code confirms: fnirsi_1013d_scope/sm_1014d.c:1456-1457 'if(timeperdiv == 7) { timeperdiv = 11; long_mode = 0; } / if(timeperdiv == 10) { timeperdiv = 6; long_mode = 1; }'; commit 87cf94b 'cal at run level, honest residual readout, symmetric trim, roll-mode overlap'.

**Suggested action:** Update §4 item 1 to describe the F21 boundary (roll ≤500 ms, crossing jumps 6↔11; 200/100/50/20 ms sweep-only), or strike it with a pointer to F21's bench session.

**Verifier:** PORT_AUDIT.md:222-224 (checklist item 1) tells the tester to exercise 'the 20/50 ms skip at indices 8↔11'. Commit 87cf94b's diff shows exactly that boundary being replaced: 'timeperdiv == 9 → 11 / == 10 → 8' became '== 7 → 11 / == 10 → 6', and the current sm_1014d.c:1456-1457 matches the finding's quote verbatim (6↔11 jump, indices 7..10 unreachable, 200/100/50/20 ms sweep-only per the comment at 1447-1453). PORT_AUDIT.md:444-448 (F21) documents the new boundary. The checklist is still live guidance (CLAUDE.md and ROADMAP item 1 both point bench work at §4), so following it verbatim would flag intended F21 behavior as a regression. The 'de facto exercised' aside is consistent with F21's bench narrative (the dial was stepped through this region on 2026-07-11).

### Fill-geometry 'CLOSED' claims mix per-ADC ring addresses with interleaved samples (2x)

- **Where:** `FPGA_NOTES.md:131`
- **Severity:** medium

Commit ceeae17 records the post-trigger fill question as CLOSED, but three derived statements are off by exactly 2x from mixing per-ADC ring addresses with interleaved (2-ADC) screen samples: (1) FPGA_NOTES.md:131 'display window ring 161…3161 (= raw − 750)' — the standard readout fetches only nofsamples=1500 samples per ADC from the translated pointer, so the readout window spans 1500 ring addresses (161…1661), not 3000; (2) FPGA_NOTES.md:132-133 (and ROADMAP.md:200, tools/acqprobe_analyze.py:325-326, both commit messages) 'pre-trigger fresh = 910, of which 750 are on-screen → trigger sits at 25 % of the 3000-sample window' — 750 pre-trigger ring addresses = 1500 interleaved samples = 50 % of the 3000-sample window (750/3000 divides an address count by a sample count); this also contradicts the firmware's own naming: settriggerpoint == samplecount/2 is labeled 50 % (menu.c:6283) and the FPGA is written settriggerpoint/2 = 750 (fpga_control.c:681) — exactly the fw1 −750 constant; (3) FPGA_NOTES.md:140-141 / analyzer output 'the gap sits just past the display's right edge' — with the true window edge at ring 1661, the held-run gap (starts ring 3418) is ~1757 addresses past it and the noisy-run gap (2616) ~955 past; the region 1661…gap-start is fresh but off-window data, not gap. The same section's own earlier bullet (FPGA_NOTES.md:113-114: 'read pointer = trigger − 750 (per ADC), then 1500 samples per ADC × 2 ADCs → 3000 interleaved per channel centered on the trigger', 'readout uses only ~37 % of the ring' = 1500/4096) states the correct model, so the doc is internally contradictory. Related: analyzer line 196 prints '4096-sample ring spans 4096/rate per ADC' using the combined rate — half the true per-ADC span (bench 5 ms/div prints 204.8 ms; true 409.6 ms). The per-ADC-unit numbers themselves (910 pre / 2507 post / gap 679–1481 / fixed start ~0 / 'post-trigger ≈2500 per ADC' at ROADMAP.md:229) are correct.

**Evidence:** scope_functions.c:865 'scopesettings.nofsamples = fpgasettings.totalsamples/2' (=1500); fpga_control.c:1072 'count = scopesettings.nofsamples' clocks per ADC command; fpga_control.c:798/870 interleave the two 1500-sample ADC reads into the 3000-entry tracebuffer (stride 2, offsets 1/0) — so a capture window occupies samplecount/2 ring addresses. menu.c:6283 'settriggerpoint == scopesettings.samplecount / 2)//50%'; fpga_control.c:681 'fpga_write_short(fpgasettings.settriggerpoint/2)'. Empirical from the commit's own bench data: autocorrelating bench/acqprobe/2026-07-19-trigger-probe/ringdump_5ms.bin block 0 gives a 50 Hz hum period of 198–200 ring addresses — i.e. addresses tick at combined-rate/2 (20 KSa/s combined → 10 KSa/s per address), so 3000 interleaved samples = 1500 addresses. Analyzer re-run reproduces the doc numbers (trigger ring 911, pre 910, post 2507, gap 679) plus the wrong prints ('display window ring 161..3161', 'trigger at ~25% width').

**Suggested action:** Correct FPGA_NOTES.md:131-141 and ROADMAP.md:200 (window = raw−750 … raw+750 in ring addresses; trigger mid-window = 50 %; gap sits ~950–1750 addresses past the readout window with fresh off-window data in between), and fix tools/acqprobe_analyze.py: line 196 use rate/2 for the per-ADC span, line 325 use samplecount//2 for the window end, line 326 report 50 % (750 addresses = 1500 of 3000 interleaved samples). Re-check any future serial-reply/multi-sampling window math against the corrected geometry.

**Verifier:** Independently re-derived the unit model from the code and reproduced the empirical test from the bench data. Code: nofsamples = totalsamples/2 = 1500 (scope_functions.c:865, totalsamples=3000 at :7828); fpga_read_adc_data clocks nofsamples samples per ADC command (fpga_control.c:1072); fpga_read_sample_data writes the same 0x1F pointer before each of the two per-ADC reads and interleaves them stride-2 into the 3000-entry tracebuffer (fpga_control.c:787-798, 858-873) — so one capture window occupies 1500 ring addresses, matching the doc's own '~37 % of the ring' (FPGA_NOTES.md:113) and '3000 interleaved ... centered on the trigger' (line 114), and contradicting line 131 ('ring 161…3161') and line 133 ('25 %'). Firmware corroboration verified: menu.c:6283 labels settriggerpoint==samplecount/2 as //50%; fpga_control.c:681 writes settriggerpoint/2 = 750; fw1 translation is data<750 ? +3345 : −750 (scope_functions.c:810-822). Decisive empirical check reproduced from scratch: autocorrelating block 0 of both driven dumps in bench/acqprobe/2026-07-19-trigger-probe/ gives a 50 Hz hum period of 198-199 ring addresses (peak at lag 198/199, ac(200)=4535 vs ac(400)=3630 for 5ms; 1562 vs 1232 for tt5ms) — addresses tick at combined-rate/2 = per-ADC, so 750 pre-trigger addresses = 1500 interleaved samples = 50 % of the window, and analyzer line 196's '204.8 ms per ADC' at 20 KSa/s is half the true 409.6 ms per-ADC span. Re-running tools/acqprobe_analyze.py reproduces every disputed print (lines 324-331: 'display window ring 161..3161', 'trigger at ~25% width', 'gap sits past the display's right edge'). The 25 % claim confirmed present in ROADMAP.md:200 and commit ceeae17's message; gap distances check out (held gap start 3418 − true window end 1661 = 1757 addresses; noisy 2616 − 1661 = 955). Also verified the finding's concession that the per-ADC-unit numbers (910 pre / 2507 & 1705 post / gap 679 & 1481 / fresh start ring ~1 / ROADMAP.md:228-229 '≈2500 post-trigger samples per ADC') are correct as stated.

### sm_set_time_base samplerate clause dropped !waveviewmode — wave view + single-trigger corrupts the viewed capture's sample rate and re-arms

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/sm_1014d.c:1480`
- **Severity:** medium

Class (ii)/(iii) drift in the F1 rebuild. The F1 fix mirrored Atlan4's scope_set_timebase, whose sample-rate-update condition is '((previousytouch < 240)&&(!scopesettings.waveviewmode)&&(scopesettings.triggermode == 1))||(scopesettings.long_mode)||(scopesettings.runstate)'. The port correctly discarded the stale previousytouch term but also dropped '!waveviewmode' from the triggermode clause: ours reads '(runstate == RUN_STATE_RUNNING) || long_mode || (triggermode == 1)'. P14's original only updated on 'runstate == RUN_STATE_RUNNING'. The TIME rotary IS routed in wave view (sm_button_dial_wave_view_handling, sm_1014d.c:972-1052 routes UIC_ROTARY_TIME_ADD/SUB to sm_set_time_base; the function's own waveview regime clamp lines 1430-1440 permits movement within the current regime). So when viewing a saved waveform whose stored triggermode is 1 (single), every TIME detent: overwrites scopesettings.samplerate — which in wave view holds the viewed buffer's acquisition rate that the code's own comment says must be preserved ('Otherwise the sample rate of the acquired buffer still is valid') and which feeds freq_calc_data/time_calc_data readouts and scope_calculate_sample_range_properties — pushes fpga_set_sample_rate to the live FPGA, forces runstate = RUN_STATE_RUNNING with ui_display_run_stop_text(), and calls scope_preset_values(), all while a file is on screen. Both references avoid this: Atlan4 via !waveviewmode, P14 via runstate-only.

**Evidence:** sm_1014d.c:1480 'if((scopesettings.runstate == RUN_STATE_RUNNING) || scopesettings.long_mode || (scopesettings.triggermode == 1))' followed by samplerate = time_per_div_sample_rate[...], fpga_set_sample_rate, 'scopesettings.runstate = RUN_STATE_RUNNING;' and scope_preset_values() (lines 1482-1499). Atlan4 reference Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/scope_functions.c:204 'if(((previousytouch < 240)&&(!scopesettings.waveviewmode)&&(scopesettings.triggermode == 1))||(scopesettings.long_mode)||(scopesettings.runstate))'. P14 original FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/statemachine.c (sm_set_time_base): 'if(scopesettings.runstate == RUN_STATE_RUNNING)' guards the samplerate update. Wave-view routing: sm_1014d.c sm_button_dial_wave_view_handling handles 'case UIC_ROTARY_TIME_ADD/SUB: sm_set_time_base();'.

**Suggested action:** Gate the triggermode clause on scope mode: '(runstate == RUN_STATE_RUNNING) || long_mode || ((triggermode == 1) && !scopesettings.waveviewmode)' — and consider whether the single-mode re-arm block (runstate = RUNNING + preset) should also be skipped in waveviewmode, matching both references.

**Verifier:** Independently reproduced every element. (1) sm_1014d.c:1480 reads '(scopesettings.runstate == RUN_STATE_RUNNING) || scopesettings.long_mode || (scopesettings.triggermode == 1)' — no !waveviewmode — followed by samplerate = time_per_div_sample_rate[...] (1483), fpga_set_sample_rate (1486), runstate = RUN_STATE_RUNNING + ui_display_run_stop_text under triggermode==1 (1489-1495), and scope_preset_values (1497). (2) Atlan4's reference condition at Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/scope_functions.c:222 carries '(previousytouch < 240)&&(!scopesettings.waveviewmode)&&(scopesettings.triggermode == 1)' — the port dropped BOTH the touch term (correctly) and !waveviewmode (the regression). (3) P14's original guards the samplerate update with runstate-only (reference statemachine.c:1389-1393), explicitly preserving the viewed buffer's rate. (4) The TIME rotary IS live in wave view: sm_button_dial_wave_view_handling routes UIC_ROTARY_TIME_ADD/SUB to sm_set_time_base (sm_1014d.c:1045-1048), and the function's own wave-view clamp (1430-1440) only restricts the regime, not the update. (5) The scenario is concrete: loading a wave file restores timeperdiv/samplerate/triggermode/long_mode from the file (scope_functions.c:5502ff via scope_restore_setup_from_file) and forces runstate=0/waveviewmode=1 (scope_functions.c:6250-6251); with stored triggermode==1 each detent then overwrites the viewed capture's samplerate (contradicting the code's own comment at 1478-1479), pushes it to the live FPGA, and re-arms — and since scope_acquire_trace_data gates only on runstate (scope_functions.c:752), the forced RUN_STATE_RUNNING starts a real conversion from the main loop (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:412) that will overwrite the viewed trace buffers, arguably worse than the finding states (the long_mode clause additionally fires in wave view for long-timebase files even with triggermode!=1). git shows the condition was introduced by commit 77af34b replacing the P14 runstate-only guard, with no documented intent to drop wave-view protection.

*(from code-fpga)*

### V/div change never re-pushes the trigger level - the fifth F16-class handler

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/sm_1014d.c:1553`
- **Severity:** medium

sm_set_channel_sensitivity pushes fpga_set_channel_voltperdiv + fpga_set_channel_offset when running, but never fpga_set_trigger_level, even though the FPGA level is a function of samplevoltperdiv (level = ((trigverpos - dcoffset - traceposition) * 2097152) / signal_adjusters[voltperdiv] + 128, fpga_control.c:512). F16 established that in this tree's non-blocking acquire the level is only re-sent when arming, so any handler that changes the level mapping must push directly; the fix covered level rotary, trigger-channel toggle, 50% and origin (sm_1014d.c:1382, 867, 875, 1402) but not the sensitivity dial. Failure scenario: normal/single mode armed and waiting, turn the trigger channel's v/div toward less sensitive (e.g. 100mV->2V/div) — the waveform shrinks in ADC counts while the FPGA keeps the level computed for the old sensitivity, which now lies outside the signal; the conversion never completes and the scope sits frozen until the user happens to turn the level dial. pecostm32's identical handler was safe only because his acquire is blocking and re-arms (re-pushing level) after every user input; Atlan4's touch flow (match_volt_per_div_settings, statemachine.c:6578) has the same latent gap. PORT_AUDIT.md:698's sweep claim 'volt/div + trigger settings all push to the FPGA on change' is true for the v/div command itself but misses this derived-level staleness. (Smaller same-class residue: the F31 coupling fix at sm_1014d.c:2485-2493 zeroes dcoffset, which also shifts the level mapping without a level push; dcoffset is nonzero after AUTO on a DC-offset signal, scope_functions.c:3159/3218.)

**Evidence:** sm_1014d.c:1547-1560: 'if(scopesettings.runstate == RUN_STATE_RUNNING) { settings->samplevoltperdiv = settings->displayvoltperdiv; fpga_set_channel_voltperdiv(settings); fpga_set_channel_offset(settings); timer0_delay(50); }' — no fpga_set_trigger_level. fpga_control.c:489/499 use samplevoltperdiv in the level calc. Arming-time-only push: scope_functions.c:752-765 (fpga_set_trigger_level only inside the runstate&&display_data_done arm block). F16 precedent: PORT_AUDIT.md:347 and the pushes at sm_1014d.c:865-867, 874-875, 1378-1382, 1401-1402.

**Suggested action:** In sm_set_channel_sensitivity, after the FPGA v/div/offset push, re-push fpga_set_trigger_level() when the trigger is on this channel (the condition at sm_1014d.c:1539-1543 already exists); consider the same one-liner in the coupling case after dcoffset is zeroed.

**Verifier:** sm_1014d.c:1522-1562: sm_set_channel_sensitivity pushes fpga_set_channel_voltperdiv + fpga_set_channel_offset in the RUN_STATE_RUNNING branch and never fpga_set_trigger_level. fpga_set_trigger_level derives the FPGA level byte from samplevoltperdiv (fpga_control.c:489/499, formula at :512), so a v/div change invalidates the level last written to the FPGA. The arming-only push is verified at scope_functions.c:752-756 (fpga_set_trigger_level only inside the runstate && display_data_done arm block; fpga_done_conversion sends no level), matching PORT_AUDIT F16's own description (~line 347). The four F16 fixes exist exactly where cited (sm_1014d.c:865-867, 874-875, 1378-1382, 1401-1402) and the sensitivity dial is the one level-mapping-changing handler without a push — so in normal/single mode, armed and waiting, a v/div turn toward less sensitivity leaves the FPGA holding a level outside the shrunken signal span and the conversion never completes until another handler pushes the level. pecostm32's handler is byte-identical (his statemachine.c:1420-1459, no level push) but his blocking acquire re-pushes fpga_set_trigger_level at the top of every acquisition (his scope_functions.c:~35), so his gap self-heals; Atlan4's touch flow match_volt_per_div_settings (statemachine.c:6579) has the same latent gap. The PORT_AUDIT sweep-note quote ('volt/div + trigger settings all push to the FPGA on change') and the F31-coupling residue (sm_1014d.c:2485-2493 zeroes dcoffset, which enters the level formula at fpga_control.c:493/503, with dcoffset nonzero after AUTO per scope_functions.c:3159/3218) also check out.

*(from code-fpga)*

### Channel enable toggle leaves the FPGA trigger source on a disabled channel (fpga_swap_trigger_channel unreachable on 1014D)

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/sm_1014d.c:1511`
- **Severity:** medium

sm_toggle_channel_enable only flips settings->enable and repaints. Atlan4's proven 1013D touch flow calls fpga_swap_trigger_channel() + scope_calculate_trigger_vertical_position() on exactly this event, so the FPGA trigger source is moved off a channel being disabled; on the 1014D build fpga_swap_trigger_channel (fpga_control.c:440) has no callers at all (only statemachine.c:1868, 1013D touch path). Failure scenario: trigger on CH1, both channels on, user disables CH1 — FPGA 0x15 still selects CH1, whose front end no longer produces meaningful crossings; in normal/single mode the armed conversion never completes (F16-style freeze until the trigger-channel button is pressed), in auto mode the trigger never locks and software trigger processing (scope_process_trigger, scope_functions.c:924-932) keeps searching the stale channel1tracebuffer that fpga_read_sample_data no longer refreshes (enable guard at scope_functions.c:869/883). This is distinct from the already-documented decision not to push fpga_set_channel_enable at runtime (PORT_AUDIT F31 note / ROADMAP 24): that note covers the enable command only, not the trigger-source swap that Atlan4's reference flow performs.

**Evidence:** sm_1014d.c:1511-1518 (toggle + ui_display_channel_settings only). Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/statemachine.c:1864-1871: 'settings->enable ^= 1; //Update the trigger channel selection in the FPGA as needed fpga_swap_trigger_channel(); scope_calculate_trigger_vertical_position();'. Current-tree callers of fpga_swap_trigger_channel: only fnirsi_1013d_scope/statemachine.c:1868 (1013D touch). PORT_AUDIT.md:686 documents only the fpga_set_channel_enable aspect as a checked non-issue.

**Suggested action:** Mirror the Atlan4 touch flow in sm_toggle_channel_enable: call fpga_swap_trigger_channel() and scope_calculate_trigger_vertical_position() (plus a trigger display refresh) after toggling enable.

**Verifier:** The structural claims all verify: sm_1014d.c:1511-1518 only flips settings->enable and repaints; Atlan4's touch flow at Atlan4-1.00o5/statemachine.c:1864-1871 calls fpga_swap_trigger_channel() + scope_calculate_trigger_vertical_position() on exactly this event; the only caller of fpga_swap_trigger_channel (fpga_control.c:440) in this tree is statemachine.c:1868, reachable solely through the 1013D touch flow, so on the 1014D the FPGA 0x15 trigger source is never moved off a channel being disabled. The stale-buffer consequence is real: scope_functions.c:869/883 skip fpga_read_sample_data for a disabled channel while scope_process_trigger (scope_functions.c:924-932) keeps selecting that channel's frozen tracebuffer for display alignment. The distinction from the documented F31/ROADMAP-24 enable-command non-issue (PORT_AUDIT.md line 686) is also accurate. However the freeze half of the failure scenario is overstated — see correction.

**Correction:** Because fpga_set_channel_enable is pushed only at boot and in the clock search (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:153-154, scope_functions.c:1595/1735 — never from the toggle, per the F31 note and stock-capture behavior), the FPGA-side CH1 front end and trigger comparator are unaffected by the UI disable: with a signal still on the disabled channel's BNC, hardware crossings continue and normal/single conversions still complete — no immediate F16-style freeze, and auto mode still locks. The demonstrable code-level consequences are (a) the trigger source silently stays on a now-invisible channel (Atlan4's flow would auto-move it), so pulling that probe later stalls normal/single with no visual cue, and (b) software trigger alignment runs against the stale tracebuffer, misaligning the visible channel's display. The never-completes/never-locks wording as an immediate result of the toggle is wrong; the after-reboot state (config saved with enable=0 and pushed at boot) is where a genuinely dead trigger source could materialize.

*(from code-fpga)*

### FPGA_NOTES claim '1014D build never sends 0x50-0x52' is false - fpga_on_off_generator sends them to the stock FPGA at every boot

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:183`
- **Severity:** medium

main() unconditionally calls fpga_on_off_generator() at boot on both variants, and that function sends 0x51 (freq), 0x52 (duty) and 0x50 (output) in BOTH branches — even with gen_enable==0 it sends 0x51/100Hz, 0x52/0%, 0x50/0 (fpga_control.c:243-264). FPGA_NOTES.md states the generator commands are 'reachable only from the 1013D touch UI paths ... the 1014D build never sends them today', and builds the ROADMAP-14 boot-hang theory ('unknown command wedging the stock FPGA's parser') partly on that. The claim is stale: every 1014D boot already feeds the stock 0x1432 FPGA these three un-REd command bytes plus 4-byte payloads, and the scope demonstrably boots — which is itself useful evidence that 0x50-0x52 do NOT wedge the stock parser at boot, redirecting the scope_generator_settings() hang investigation. Inherited verbatim from Atlan4 main (Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:161) where it is harmless because Atlan4 targets replacement FPGAs that implement 0x50-0x52.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:183 'fpga_on_off_generator();' with no PORT_1014D or fw_FPGA guard (context lines 153-191 all run on 1014D). fpga_control.c:254-264 else-branch: 'fpga_set_generator_freq(100); fpga_set_generator_duty(0); fpga_write_cmd(0x50); fpga_write_int(0);'. FPGA_NOTES.md:71-73: 'The generator commands are reachable only from the 1013D touch UI paths ... the 1014D build never sends them today'. pecostm32-RE command inventory ('FPGA explained.txt') has no 0x50-0x52; pecostm32's 1014D fpga_control.c never writes them.

**Suggested action:** Correct FPGA_NOTES.md (and the ROADMAP 14 hang hypothesis) to record that 0x50-0x52 are sent at every boot and tolerated; decide explicitly whether to guard fpga_on_off_generator() with fw_FPGA > 1 for stock-FPGA protocol purity.

**Verifier:** fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:183 calls fpga_on_off_generator() with no PORT_1014D or fw_FPGA guard (the surrounding init sequence 140-195 is unconditional; the first #if is PORT_A_KEYDEBUG at 197), and fpga_control.c:241-265 shows both branches send 0x51 (fpga_set_generator_freq, cmd at :278), 0x52 (fpga_set_generator_duty, cmd at :293) and 0x50 — the gen_enable==0 branch sends 0x51/100Hz, 0x52/0, 0x50/0. FPGA_NOTES.md:71-73 states verbatim that the generator commands are 'reachable only from the 1013D touch UI paths ... the 1014D build never sends them today' and lines 74-76 tie the scope_generator_settings boot hang to 'an unknown command wedging the stock FPGA's parser' — so the doc claim is factually false for the boot path. Inheritance verified: Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:161 has the same call, present since the vendor commit 9daa91f. pecostm32's 1014D fpga_control.c contains no 0x50-0x52 writes and the pecostm32-RE command inventory lists none. Since 1014D SD boot with traces is bench-confirmed working (CLAUDE.md known-issues section), the corollary — three un-REd commands hit the stock 0x1432 parser every boot without wedging it — follows and legitimately redirects the ROADMAP-14 hang theory.

*(from code-guards)*

### F25 FPGA 0x28 mode-select restore is 1014D-only; 1013D branch keeps the regression vs its own upstream

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:631`
- **Severity:** medium

The F25 fix (restoring the FPGA 0x29 dual-ADC + 0x28/0x00 mode-select in fpga_do_conversion for fw_FPGA==1) is wrapped in #if PORT_1014D, so the 1013D variant still omits the 0x28 write that Atlan4 commented out on the (audit-proven wrong) "0x28 not support" guess. pecostm32's 1013D upstream sends 0x28/0x00 unconditionally at exactly this point, so a 1013D-variant build on a stock FPGA retains the omission that F25 demonstrated causes the <=200ns/div dual-ADC interleave sawtooth on the 1014D. The in-code comment justifies restricting to fw_FPGA==1 vs the PECO paths but never explains excluding the 1013D; the evidence for the 1013D (its own upstream source) is stronger than the bus captures that justified the 1014D fix.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:631-642: "#if PORT_1014D / if(fpgasettings.fw_FPGA == 1) { fpga_write_cmd(0x29); fpga_write_byte(0x01); fpga_write_cmd(0x28); fpga_write_byte(0x00); } #endif" with comment "Atlan4 commented these out on a wrong '0x28 not support' guess". Reference FNIRSI_1013D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:494-496: "//Set the FPGA for short time base mode / fpga_write_cmd(0x28); / fpga_write_byte(0x00);". Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:622-624 shows the same lines commented out ("//fpga_write_cmd(0x28);").

**Suggested action:** Extend the 0x28/0x00 restore to the 1013D branch for fw_FPGA==1 (matching pecostm32's 1013D upstream, which sends only 0x28/0x00 here — keep the 0x29 dual-ADC byte 1014D-only since it comes from 1014D bus captures), or explicitly record the deliberate exclusion and its rationale in PORT_AUDIT.md so the 1013D bring-up checklist knows to expect the sawtooth.

**Verifier:** Independently reproduced all three citations: fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:631-642 wraps the F25 restore (0x29/0x01 + 0x28/0x00 under fw_FPGA==1) in #if PORT_1014D, so a PORT_1014D=0 build compiles it out; the pecostm32 1013D upstream (FNIRSI_1013D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:494-496) sends 0x28/0x00 unconditionally at exactly this point in fpga_do_conversion (and sends no 0x29, matching the finding); Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:622-624 has the same writes commented out. So the 1013D variant genuinely retains Atlan4's omission that F25 proved wrong on the same stock-FPGA protocol (0x1432), against its own upstream's evidence. The in-code comment indeed only justifies fw_FPGA==1 vs the PECO paths and says nothing about the PORT_1014D guard.

**Correction:** One sub-claim needs tempering: the exclusion is not undocumented overall — PORT_AUDIT.md F25 (lines 638-639) explicitly records it as a deliberate choice ('#if PORT_1014D + fw_FPGA==1 so the untestable 1013D and the PECO fw_FPGA>1 paths are untouched'), i.e. conservatism about a never-hardware-tested variant, not an oversight. The 'never explains' statement is literally true only of the in-code comment, which does reference PORT_AUDIT.md F25. The retained-omission fact and the medium-severity latent hazard stand.

*(from code-guards)*

### CLAUDE.md module table misdocuments statemachine.c/PC_interface.c as 1013D-only; they are load-bearing, unguarded shared code in the 1014D build

- **Where:** `CLAUDE.md:179`
- **Severity:** medium

CLAUDE.md:179-180 lists menu.c, statemachine.c, touchpanel.c, DS3231.c, power_and_battery.c, generator.c, PC_interface.c as "1013D-only UI" and the imported modules as "1014D-only". In reality none of these files is compiled out in either variant (both variants build the identical object list), and two of the "1013D-only" files export utilities the 1014D build calls unconditionally: main() calls ini_SysParam() (PC_interface.c), the shared acquisition path calls match_volt_per_div_settings() (statemachine.c) from scope_functions.c, usb_interface.c calls usb_CDC_send_data() (PC_interface.c), and menu.c's export code uses my_strcpy/my_strcat/my_uint_to_str/usb_send_uint (PC_interface.c). Conversely uart.c, clock_synthesizer.c, and font_1.c ("1014D-only") carry no guards at all and are fully compiled and linked into 1013D images (the link at nbproject/Makefile-Debug.mk:107 has no -ffunction-sections/--gc-sections, so dead variant code ships in both images). Wrong-action risk: the repo's own documented remedy for variant files is the whole-file-guard-after-includes pattern (the F3/F4 lesson, applied to menu_1014d.c/sm_1014d.c/fonts_1014d.c) — applying it to statemachine.c or PC_interface.c per the table would break the 1014D link; the table also misleads audits like this one into treating those files as dead code on 1014D.

**Evidence:** CLAUDE.md:179-180: "1013D-only UI: `menu.c`, `statemachine.c`, ... `PC_interface.c`. 1014D-only: the imported table above." nbproject/Makefile-Debug.mk:38-82: single object list for both variants (statemachine.o, PC_interface.o, uart.o, clock_synthesizer.o, font_1.o all always built); line 107 link command has no --gc-sections. fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:279 "ini_SysParam();" (unguarded, in main) -> PC_interface.c:57 "void ini_SysParam(void)". scope_functions.c:99 "if(scopesettings.runstate) match_volt_per_div_settings(&scopesettings.channel1);" (unguarded; also 120, 158, 179, 2177-2178) -> defined in statemachine.c. usb_interface.c:1650 -> PC_interface.c usb_CDC_send_data. menu.c:9499-9501 my_uint_to_str/my_strcpy/my_strcat (unguarded). grep confirms statemachine.c, generator.c, PC_interface.c, uart.c, clock_synthesizer.c, font_1.c contain zero PORT_1014D occurrences.

**Suggested action:** Reword the CLAUDE.md module table to distinguish the three real mechanisms: (a) compiled-out whole-file guards (font_0/2/3/4.c vs fonts_1014d.c, menu_1014d.c, sm_1014d.c); (b) per-function-body stubs (touchpanel.c, DS3231.c, power_and_battery.c); (c) always-compiled shared files with variant-dead sections and shared utilities (menu.c, statemachine.c, generator.c, PC_interface.c, uart.c, clock_synthesizer.c, font_1.c) — and note explicitly that whole-file guards must NOT be added to group (c).

**Verifier:** Reproduced the CLAUDE.md:179-180 table text verbatim. nbproject/Makefile-Debug.mk has one variant-independent object list (PC_interface.o:39, font_1.o:50, generator.o:56, statemachine.o:75, clock_synthesizer.o:76, uart.o:77, touchpanel.o:80) and the link command (~line 107) has no --gc-sections (and CFLAGS no -ffunction-sections), so every object links fully into both images. Grep confirms zero PORT_1014D occurrences in statemachine.c, generator.c, PC_interface.c, uart.c, clock_synthesizer.c, font_1.c. Load-bearing cross-references verified: fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:279 calls ini_SysParam() unguarded in main (nearest guards end at line 227) -> PC_interface.c:57; scope_functions.c:99/120/158/179 (scope_adjust_timebase_and_voltdiv) and 2177-2178/2283 (scope_input_calibration) plus 3013+ (scope_do_auto_setup_new — both used on 1014D) call match_volt_per_div_settings -> statemachine.c:6579; usb_interface.c:1506-1516 calls usb_send_uint -> PC_interface.c:331; menu.c:9499-9501 (make_freq_to_text, unguarded — menu.c's PORT_1014D guards are all at/before line 2331) uses my_uint_to_str/my_strcpy/my_strcat -> PC_interface.c:510/409/425. Applying the repo's documented whole-file-guard remedy to statemachine.c or PC_interface.c per the table would therefore break the 1014D link — the wrong-action risk is real.

**Correction:** One evidence sub-item is inverted: usb_interface.c:1650 is the LIVE DEFINITION of usb_CDC_send_data; PC_interface.c's two copies (lines 196 and 235) are both inside /* */ comment blocks, so usb_interface.c does not call into PC_interface.c via that symbol. The same coupling exists anyway through usb_send_uint (usb_interface.c:1506-1516 -> PC_interface.c:331), so the conclusion is unaffected.

### uint16 time-cursor positions wrap: cursors jump to the opposite extreme

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/sm_1014d.c:292`
- **Severity:** medium

The time-cursor rotary handlers do `scopesettings.timecursor1position += speedvalue;` (line 292) and `timecursor2position += speedvalue;` (line 309) with wrap-then-clamp guards written for signed values. Both fields are uint16 in this tree (variables.h:804-805) but int16 in the pecostm32 source (reference variables.h:482). Left cursor at its minimum (6) with fast speed (-10) wraps to 65532, fails `< 6`, passes `>= timecursor2position`, and is set to timecursor2position-1 — it jumps rightward across the screen. Right cursor sitting just right of the left one wraps the same way, fails `<= timecursor1position` (65531 is not <=), passes `> 704`, and snaps to 704 — the far right edge. The volt-cursor handlers (326, 343) are safe because their minimum bounds (59) exceed the maximum step (10). Concrete failure: with time cursors enabled and fast move speed, dialing a cursor past its stop makes it leap to the opposite side instead of stopping. Port-introduced seam.

**Evidence:** sm_1014d.c:292-304 (left cursor: `< 6` then `>= timecursor2position` clamp to timecursor2position-1), 309-321 (right cursor: `<= timecursor1position` then `> 704` clamp to 704); variables.h:804-805 `uint16 timecursor1position; uint16 timecursor2position;`; pecostm32 reference variables.h:482 `int16 timecursor1position;`; speedvalue -10 via sm_1014d.c:121-122 with MOVE_SPEED_FAST=10.

**Suggested action:** Use a signed local for the adjusted value before clamping, or make the cursor position fields int16 as in pecostm32 (they are screen coordinates 6..704, well within int16).

**Verifier:** Reproduced from source: sm_1014d.c:292-304 (left cursor: '+= speedvalue', then '< 6' else '>= timecursor2position' -> set to timecursor2position-1) and 309-321 (right cursor: '<= timecursor1position' else '> 704' -> set to 704); both fields are uint16 here (variables.h:804-805) but int16 in the pecostm32 source (reference variables.h:482-483), and Atlan4's own uint16 (Atlan4 variables.h:633-634) shows the port kept Atlan4's types under pecostm32's signed-logic handlers. Left cursor at min 6 with speedvalue -10 wraps to 65532, fails <6, passes >=timecursor2position, and jumps to timecursor2position-1 (rightward across the screen). Right cursor wraps when both cursors sit near the left edge (timecursor2 in 7..9, reachable by dialing left cursor to 6 then right cursor down to 7): 7-10 wraps to 65533, fails <=timecursor1position, passes >704, snaps to 704. (The finding's example figure '65531' is off by 2 — the actual wrapped values are 65533..65535 — but the mechanism and failure are exactly as claimed.) The volt-cursor handlers at 326/343 are indeed wrap-safe: their reachable minimum values (>=59, and >=voltcursor1+1) minus the max step 10 never go below zero.

*(from code-gotchas)*

### USB CDC debug command 'f' sets totalsamples unbounded, causing a huge acquisition buffer overrun

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/usb_interface.c:1542`
- **Severity:** medium

The CDC debug parser accepts `f<number>` and assigns it raw: `case 'f': f = val; fpgasettings.totalsamples = f; break;`. totalsamples is uint16 (variables.h:844), so values up to 65535 stick. scope_functions.c:865-866 derive nofsamples = totalsamples/2, and fpga_read_adc_data (fpga_control.c:1072 `count = scopesettings.nofsamples;`) writes count samples at stride 2 (`settings->buffer += 2`, fpga_control.c:1389) starting at tracebuffer[1] then tracebuffer[0] (fpga_control.c:798/870) — touching totalsamples bytes of channelXtracebuffer, which is only 16380 bytes (uint32[4095], variables.c:207/212 with UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE=16382/4). A host sending 'f60000' over the CDC port makes every subsequent acquisition frame write ~44KB past each channel buffer through adjacent .bss. Worse, totalsamples is saved in the checksummed config sector and restored on boot (scope_functions.c:8269), so the corruption persists across power cycles until a factory reset. Pre-existing: usb_interface.c is byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5 (diff verified), so this is Atlan4's debug back door, not a port regression — but the 1014D build carries it.

**Evidence:** usb_interface.c:1542 raw assignment inside the a..k debug parser (Slovak comments, Atlan4 origin; `diff -q usb_interface.c ../Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/usb_interface.c` reports identical); fpga_control.c:1072/1076/1156/1389 read loop writing *buffer with += 2 per sample; scope_functions.c:865-866 nofsamples/samplecount from totalsamples; variables.c:207 `uint32 channel1tracebuffer[UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE]` = 16380 bytes; variables.h:844 `uint16 totalsamples;`; scope_functions.c:8269 restore from config sector.

**Suggested action:** Clamp the 'f' command (and the config-restore value at scope_functions.c:8269) to a safe maximum, e.g. totalsamples <= 16380 (or the FPGA-legal maximum), before it reaches nofsamples.

**Verifier:** Reproduced the full chain. usb_interface.c:1542 is verbatim `case 'f': f = val; fpgasettings.totalsamples = f; break;` with val from my_atoul and no bounds check, inside the a..k CDC parser that is wired to the EP2 RX interrupt path (usb_CDC_receive_callback called at usb_interface.c:1416); the file is byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5 (diff exit 0), confirming pre-existing Atlan4 origin. totalsamples is uint16 (variables.h:844) so 60000 sticks; scope_functions.c:865-866 recompute nofsamples = totalsamples/2 (uint32, variables.h:751) every acquisition; fpga_read_sample_data points settings->buffer (uint8*, variables.h:668-669) at tracebuffer[1] then [0] (fpga_control.c:798/870) and fpga_read_adc_data loops count = scopesettings.nofsamples (fpga_control.c:1072) storing one byte then 'settings->buffer += 2' per sample (~1386-1389) — two passes cover totalsamples bytes, i.e. 60000 bytes into channelXtracebuffer which is uint32[UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE=4095] = 16380 bytes (variables.h:528-529, variables.c:207/212, tracebuffer pointers assigned at scope_functions.c:7396/7425) — ~43.6KB past each buffer. Persistence confirmed: saved at scope_functions.c:8052 inside scope_save_config_data (checksum at 8060-8068), restored at 8269 inside scope_restore_config_data after only a checksum+version check (8088) with no range validation; nothing else writes totalsamples except the default 3000 at 7828.

*(from code-gotchas)*

### MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE (16382) exceeds the 16380-byte uint32 trace arrays: 2-byte overruns

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/variables.h:529`
- **Severity:** medium

UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE is defined as MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE/4 = 16382/4 = 4095 (floor), so every uint32[UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE] trace array holds 16380 bytes, while byte-view loops and I/O run to 16382 (SAMPLE_COUNT == MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE). Verified out-of-bounds users: ref_and_math.c:145-155 compute_all_math_ops loops i < SAMPLE_COUNT reading A[i]/B[i] and WRITING math_tracebuffer[i] (points at math_channel_tracebuffer, 16380 bytes, scope_functions.c:7471) — a 2-byte write past the array every math frame; ref_and_math.c:102 `memcpy(dest->tracebuffer, src->tracebuffer, SAMPLE_COUNT)` reads and writes 2 bytes past both; ref_and_math.c:247-310 f_read of SAMPLE_COUNT bytes into ref1..8_tracebuffer (uint32[4095], variables.c:217-218) on reference-waveform load — 2 bytes past each. The overrun lands in the adjacent .bss buffer (2 bytes of sample data corrupted, no crash observed-class), but it is a real OOB write. Pre-existing: macros and ref_and_math.c are byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5 (diff verified).

**Evidence:** variables.h:528-529 `#define MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE 16382` / `#define UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE (MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE / 4)` (integer floor -> 4095); variables.c:207/212/217-218/237 uint32[UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE] declarations; ref_and_math.c:102, 145-155, 247-310 (SAMPLE_COUNT-sized byte accesses); Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/variables.h:383-384 identical; `diff ref_and_math.c ../Atlan4-1.00o5/.../ref_and_math.c` empty.

**Suggested action:** Define UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE as ((MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE + 3) / 4) so the arrays round up to 16384 bytes; one-line fix, no behavior change for in-range data.

**Verifier:** Reproduced: variables.h:528-529 define MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE 16382 and UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE (16382/4) which floors to 4095, so every uint32[UINT32_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE] array holds 16380 bytes while SAMPLE_COUNT == 16382 (variables.h:531). Verified OOB users: ref_and_math.c copy_ch_to_ref does memcpy(dest->tracebuffer, src->tracebuffer, SAMPLE_COUNT) at line 102 (2-byte OOB read and write on 16380-byte buffers); compute_all_math_ops (ref_and_math.c:145-156) loops i < SAMPLE_COUNT writing scopesettings.math_tracebuffer[i], which points at math_channel_tracebuffer = uint32[4095] (scope_functions.c:7471, variables.c:237) — called from display_MATHx_data (ref_and_math.c:116) each math frame; the ref-waveform loads f_read(..., SAMPLE_COUNT, ...) into ref1..8_tracebuffer = uint32[4095] (ref_and_math.c:247-310, variables.c:217-226) — and the matching saves f_write SAMPLE_COUNT (416/431/446/461/475...) mean the files really do contain 16382 bytes, so the load writes the full 2 bytes past. Pre-existing confirmed: ref_and_math.c diffs empty against Atlan4-1.00o5, and Atlan4's variables.h:383-384 has the identical macros.

*(from doc-fpga-boot)*

### Command table still says 0x28 is dead — contradicts the doc's own SOLVED section and the code

- **Where:** `FPGA_NOTES.md:57`
- **Severity:** medium

The command-inventory row for 0x28 reads: '"short time base mode" — **dead**: Atlan4 comments it "0X28 not support in FPGA???" and never sends it'. That was true before 2026-07-12; the same document's SOLVED section (lines 190-204), PORT_AUDIT.md F25, and CLAUDE.md all record that 0x29/0x01 + 0x28/0x00 were restored on the stock fw_FPGA==1 path and bench-confirmed. The code sends 0x28 every conversion on the 1014D fw1 path. The table — the part a fresh session is most likely to consult as the protocol reference — was never updated, and 0x29 (dual-ADC mode) is missing from the inventory entirely.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:631-641: `#if PORT_1014D / if(fpgasettings.fw_FPGA == 1) { fpga_write_cmd(0x29); fpga_write_byte(0x01); fpga_write_cmd(0x28); fpga_write_byte(0x00); }` inside fpga_do_conversion; only the roll-mode copy remains commented (line 599). FPGA_NOTES.md:190-204 (SOLVED section) says 'Restoring 0x29/0x01 + 0x28/0x00 ... eliminated the sawtooth'; commit 1a18ce8 (2026-07-12) 'restore FPGA 0x29/0x28 dual-ADC mode-select in do_conversion (F25)'.

**Suggested action:** Update the 0x28 table row to: sent every conversion on the fw1/1014D path since F25 (0x00 fast, 0x01 roll — roll copy still commented out), and add a 0x29 (dual-ADC mode) row.

**Verifier:** FPGA_NOTES.md:57 still reads '0x28 ... dead: Atlan4 comments it ... and never sends it', but fpga_control.c:631-641 sends 0x29/0x01 then 0x28/0x00 inside fpga_do_conversion on the PORT_1014D fw_FPGA==1 path (commit 1a18ce8, 2026-07-12, 'restore FPGA 0x29/0x28 dual-ADC mode-select in do_conversion (F25)'), with only the roll-mode copy still commented at lines 599-600. The doc's own SOLVED section (lines 190-204) records the restore, contradicting its own table. 0x29 appears nowhere in the command inventory (grep: sole mention is line 197 in the SOLVED prose).

### 0x14 row documents dead code; trigger address is 13-bit, not 12-bit, in this tree

- **Where:** `FPGA_NOTES.md:53`
- **Severity:** medium

The 0x14 row says 'read trigger point (short & 0x0FFF, +2)'. In this tree that exact expression is commented out; the live code (inherited unchanged from pristine Atlan4, comment 'change 11.12.2025') is `data = fpga_read_short() & 0x1FFF` with no +2 — a 13-bit value. Line 104's '12-bit trigger address from 0x14' repeats the error: Atlan4's own protocol reference documents trigger_address[12:8]+[7:0] (13 bits) and 0x1F loading sample_read_offset[12:0]. The '& 0x0FFF, +2' form is pecostm32's official 1014D firmware, not this tree — but the doc's preamble (line 4) says everything was read out of this tree's fpga_control.c. Raw-readout/multi-sampling code written against the documented 12-bit/+2 contract would mis-mask bit 12 and be offset 2 samples versus the actual acqprobe/ringdump data.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:741-746: `//data = (fpga_read_short() & 0x0FFF) + 2;` (commented) then live `data = (fpga_read_short() & 0x1FFF);  //change 11.12.2025`; identical in Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:694-707. 'Atlan4-FPGA/FPGA commands.txt': `8'h14 ... o_mcu_data <= trigger_address[12:8]` then `[7:0]`; `8'h1F ... sample_read_offset[12:8]`. pecostm32's variant: FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/fpga_control.c:555-556 `//Get the 12 bit location ... (fpga_read_short() & 0x0FFF) + 2`.

**Suggested action:** Correct the table row to `& 0x1FFF` (no +2) for this tree, note pecostm32's 0x0FFF+2 variant explicitly as reference-only, and change '12-bit trigger address' at line 104 to 13-bit register / mod-4096 ring behavior (the bench-confirmed wrap).

**Verifier:** fpga_control.c:743-744: the '(fpga_read_short() & 0x0FFF) + 2' form is commented out and the live code is '& 0x1FFF' with no +2 ('//change 11.12.2025'), identical in the Atlan4-1.00o5 reference (lines 701-706); the 0x0FFF/+2 form is live only in pecostm32's 1014D reference (fpga_control.c:555-558). Atlan4-FPGA/FPGA commands.txt — which documents the stock FPGA (its 0x06 returns 0x14/0x32 = version 0x1432) — gives trigger_address[12:8]+[7:0] and sample_read_offset[12:8], i.e. 13 bits. FPGA_NOTES.md:53 documents the dead 12-bit/+2 expression in a table headed 'as used in this tree' (and line 4 claims everything was read out of this tree), and line 104-105 repeats '12-bit trigger address from 0x14'.

### Roll-mode 0x28/0x01 follow-up names the wrong function (also in PORT_AUDIT.md)

- **Where:** `FPGA_NOTES.md:201`
- **Severity:** medium

The SOLVED section's follow-up says 'restore roll-mode 0x28/0x01 in fpga_set_time_base (still commented)' (and line 193 similarly attributes the commented roll-mode write to fpga_set_time_base). The commented `0x28/0x01` actually lives in `fpga_set_long_timebase()`; `fpga_set_time_base()` contains no 0x28 at all, live or commented. PORT_AUDIT.md:643 repeats the misattribution ('the roll/long path (fpga_set_time_base, fpga_control.c:603)'). Since FPGA command writes are silicon-tied and must be placed exactly, a fresh session following the doc would grep fpga_set_time_base, find nothing, and either give up or patch the wrong function. The 'still commented' status itself is accurate.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:557-572 = fpga_set_time_base (only 0x0E written); lines 576-601 = fpga_set_long_timebase containing `//fpga_write_cmd(0x28); //0X28 not support in FPGA??? / //fpga_write_byte(0x01);` at 599-600. PORT_AUDIT.md:643-644: 'the roll/long path (`fpga_set_time_base`, `fpga_control.c:603`) still has its `0x28/0x01` commented out'.

**Suggested action:** Change the function name to fpga_set_long_timebase() in both FPGA_NOTES.md (lines 193, 201-202) and PORT_AUDIT.md:643.

**Verifier:** fpga_control.c:557-572 = fpga_set_time_base, which writes only 0x0E (no 0x28, live or commented); the commented '//fpga_write_cmd(0x28); //0X28 not support in FPGA??? / //fpga_write_byte(0x01);' lives at lines 599-600 inside fpga_set_long_timebase (576-601), same placement as pristine Atlan4 (reference line 598, inside fpga_set_long_timebase). FPGA_NOTES.md:193 and 201-202 both attribute it to fpga_set_time_base, and PORT_AUDIT.md:643-644 repeats the misattribution ('the roll/long path (fpga_set_time_base, fpga_control.c:603)'). The 'still commented' status is accurate; only the function name is wrong.

### samplerate==0 comp gate described as current — it was later removed (comp now unconditional)

- **Where:** `FPGA_NOTES.md:477`
- **Severity:** medium

The clock-search section presents 'Compensation apply gate in fpga_read_adc_data() corrected from timeperdiv >= 29 to samplerate == 0' as the fixed state, and the screenshot-analysis section (line 559) reasons from 'the samplerate==0 gate change is correct for measurements ... and neutral for display'. The gate no longer exists: per PORT_AUDIT F23 (commit b0daa6b, 'Apply comp at all rates (drop samplerate==0 gate)') the compensation in fpga_read_adc_data is now applied unconditionally at every rate, matching pecostm32. FPGA_NOTES never records the removal anywhere, so a session auditing interleave behavior at slow timebases would predict uncompensated data where the code compensates.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:1087-1093: '//Compensate the value for ADC inequality ... Applied unconditionally at every rate, matching pecostm32's read path (... PORT_AUDIT.md F23). The old samplerate==0 gate left the sawtooth uncorrected on every timescale except the fastest'; `sample += settings->compensation;` with no rate condition. PORT_AUDIT.md F23 table row: 'Apply comp at all rates (drop samplerate==0 gate) | b0daa6b | no change (tested at rate 0)'. No `samplerate == 0` comp gate remains in fpga_control.c (grep).

**Suggested action:** Add a dated superseded-by note at lines 477-483 (and 555-560): the gate was subsequently dropped entirely (b0daa6b / F23); compensation now applies at all rates.

**Verifier:** fpga_control.c:1087-1093 applies 'sample += settings->compensation;' unconditionally, with a comment explicitly stating it is applied at every rate matching pecostm32 (PORT_AUDIT.md F23) and that 'the old samplerate==0 gate left the sawtooth uncorrected'; commit b0daa6b (2026-07-11) is 'apply interleave comp at all rates (drop samplerate==0 gate), match pecostm32', and PORT_AUDIT F23's table has the row 'Apply comp at all rates (drop samplerate==0 gate) | b0daa6b | no change (tested at rate 0)'. FPGA_NOTES.md mentions the gate only at lines 477-478 (as the corrected/current state) and 559 ('the samplerate==0 gate change is correct...'), and grep confirms it never records the removal (no F23/b0daa6b/'all rates' anywhere in the file).

### main() init-order description swapped in two places (SPI flash vs Si5351; USB vs config load)

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/CLAUDE.md:164`
- **Severity:** low

Lines 164–167 give the boot sequence as 'clock_synthesizer_setup() → SPI flash → fpga_init() → display → SD/FatFs → USB → scope_load_configuration_data()'. Actual order in main(): sys_spi_flash_init() comes BEFORE clock_synthesizer_setup(), and scope_load_configuration_data() comes BEFORE usb_device_init(). The load-bearing constraint stated (Si5351 must precede FPGA init) is correct in the code, so this is orientation noise, not a hazard — but a session reasoning about init dependencies (e.g. whether config load can use USB, or whether the clock-gen needs SPI flash) would start from a wrong sequence.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c: sys_spi_flash_init() at line 75, clock_synthesizer_setup() at line 84 (inside #if PORT_1014D), fpga_init() at line 88, sys_init_display at line 97, f_mount at line 103, scope_load_configuration_data() at line 142, usb_device_init() at line 145.

**Suggested action:** Reorder the sequence to: SPI flash → clock_synthesizer_setup() (Si5351, must precede FPGA init) → fpga_init() → display → SD/FatFs → scope_load_configuration_data() → USB.

**Verifier:** fnirsi_1013d_scope.c main(): sys_spi_flash_init() at line 75 precedes clock_synthesizer_setup() at line 84 (inside #if PORT_1014D; the comment there explains the Si5351-before-FPGA constraint, which fpga_init() at line 88 honors); sys_init_display at 97, f_mount at 103, then scope_load_configuration_data() at 142 precedes usb_device_init() at 145. CLAUDE.md:164-166 states 'clock_synthesizer_setup() → SPI flash → … → USB → scope_load_configuration_data()' — both pairs swapped exactly as claimed. The load-bearing Si5351-before-fpga_init ordering is stated correctly, so low severity (orientation noise) is the right call.

### 'Release is an unused host target' is wrong — it is an unused stale ARM/STM32 target

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/CLAUDE.md:108`
- **Severity:** low

The build section says 'config Debug is the ARM target (Release is an unused host target)'. Makefile-Release.mk actually uses the ARM cross-compiler (CC=arm-none-eabi-gcc) and links with a leftover STM32 linker script (stm32f103-64k.ld) — it is a stale, broken ARM target, not a host one. 'Unused' is the correct operative word, but a session wanting a host-side build (e.g. to unit-test table math) might reach for Release believing it targets the host.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/nbproject/Makefile-Release.mk:17 'CC=arm-none-eabi-gcc'; line 100 links with '-T./stm32f103-64k.ld -nostdlib'.

**Suggested action:** Reword to 'Release is a stale, unusable leftover (ARM compiler + an STM32 linker script) — only Debug builds'.

**Verifier:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/nbproject/Makefile-Release.mk:17 sets CC=arm-none-eabi-gcc, and line 100 links with 'arm-none-eabi-gcc … -T./stm32f103-64k.ld -nostdlib' — an ARM cross-compile against a leftover STM32F103 linker script, not a host build (only the unused CCC/CXX=g++ entries are host compilers, irrelevant to this C project). CLAUDE.md:108's '(Release is an unused host target)' is therefore wrong about the target architecture while right about 'unused'. The failure scenario (reaching for Release expecting a host build for unit-testing table math) is plausible but minor; low severity fits.

### MEMORY.md hook for user-terminal-misclicks omits the memory's own 'don't over-apply' quota-cutoff caveat

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/.claude/projects/-home-jsantala-src-FNIRSI-1014D-Firmware/memory/MEMORY.md:7`
- **Severity:** low

The index line only says rejections may be accidental mis-clicks. The file's second half (added 2026-07-17) is the action-critical counterweight: 5-hour quota cutoffs look like a turn simply ending, are NOT mis-clicks or direction changes, and the rule is to only reason about mis-clicks when an actual denied tool call is in the transcript. An agent primed by the hook alone could misread a quota resume as a rejection-recovery situation — exactly what the caveat exists to prevent.

**Evidence:** MEMORY.md:7 'Terminal mis-clicks — tool rejections may be accidental (mouse capture on refocus); CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_MOUSE=1 suggested' vs user-terminal-misclicks.md:24-30 '**Don't over-apply (2026-07-17):** … Only reason about mis-clicks when there is an actual denied tool call in the transcript.'

**Suggested action:** Extend the hook: '…; but quota cutoffs mid-turn are NOT mis-clicks — only apply when a tool call was actually denied'.

**Verifier:** Both quotes verified verbatim. MEMORY.md:7 carries only the mis-click half ('tool rejections may be accidental (mouse capture on refocus); CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_MOUSE=1 suggested'). user-terminal-misclicks.md:24-30 contains the 'Don't over-apply (2026-07-17)' section: 5-hour quota cutoffs look like the turn simply ending (no denial result), are NOT mis-clicks or direction changes, and mis-click reasoning applies only when an actual denied tool call is in the transcript. The hook omits this counterweight entirely, and the failure mode is plausible: the hook alone primes 'rejections may be accidental' without the guard that bounds when to apply it. A one-line index can't carry everything, but the omitted half is specifically the part written to prevent misapplication of the first half — reasonable as low-severity index polish.

### AGENTS.md chmod-on-fresh-checkout gotcha has a false premise: mksunxi/flashfilepacker are committed executable (100755) on this branch

- **Where:** `AGENTS.md:105`
- **Severity:** low

The gotcha says 'mksunxi / flashfilepacker Linux ELFs are committed mode 100644. On fresh checkout, chmod +x ...'. On atlan4-base both files have been committed mode 100755 since the Atlan4 vendor commit 9daa91f (2026-07-06), so a fresh checkout needs no chmod. The advice is harmless but the premise is wrong, and CLAUDE.md repeats the same stale claim ('committed non-executable') — worth fixing in both.

**Evidence:** git ls-files -s in fnirsi_1013d_scope/: '100755 ... flashfilepacker' and '100755 ... mksunxi'; git log -p for fnirsi_1013d_scope/mksunxi shows 'old mode 100644 / new mode 100755' in 9daa91f 'vendor: Atlan4 Fnirsi1013D v1.00o5'; on-disk: -rwxr-xr-x both. AGENTS.md:105 'committed mode 100644'.

**Suggested action:** Reword to: committed executable since 9daa91f — chmod only needed on the older branches (main/PORT_A); keep the rebuild-from-source fallback. Apply the same correction to CLAUDE.md's 'committed non-executable' line.

**Verifier:** git ls-files -s shows both fnirsi_1013d_scope/mksunxi and flashfilepacker committed at mode 100755 on this branch, and git show 9daa91f (2026-07-06, 'vendor: Atlan4 Fnirsi1013D v1.00o5') contains the 'old mode 100644 / new mode 100755' change for both files; on disk both are -rwxr-xr-x. AGENTS.md:105 claims 'committed mode 100644' requiring chmod +x on fresh checkout — false premise on atlan4-base (and CLAUDE.md's 'committed non-executable' repeats it). Harmless advice, wrong premise; low severity is right.
