### F28 decimal-resolution cap ignores the frozen-zoom rescale the value receives

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/menu_1014d.c:1797`
- **Severity:** low

Commit d2fa75c (F28): in ui_display_voltage the displayed value is rescaled by vertical_scaling_factors[displayvoltperdiv][samplevoltperdiv]/10000 when the two volt/div settings differ (stop-mode zoom, lines 1786-1790), but the new 'one ADC code' resolution at line 1797 is computed without that factor; ui_display_vavg has the same asymmetry (value rescaled at 1609-1613, resolution at 1619 not). The commit explicitly fixed this omission for the Vavg *value* ('caught in the pre-commit review') but not for the resolution. vertical_scaling_factors ratios span /100…*100, so under frozen zoom the decimal cap is off by the zoom ratio: zoomed-in readouts (display finer than sampled) get an over-large resolution and drop an honest decimal; zoomed-out readouts pass an under-sized resolution and print a decimal finer than one ADC code — the exact fabricated-precision class F28 set out to remove. Minor related imprecision: '(int32)signal_adjusters[...] >> VOLTAGE_SHIFTER' truncates 3.6-3.8M/2^21 = 1.72-1.81 to 1 (and 3.62 to 3), understating one ADC code by up to ~42% even unzoomed.

**Evidence:** menu_1014d.c:1782-1797: volts gets '(volts * vertical_scaling_factors[...])/10000' inside 'if(settings->displayvoltperdiv != settings->samplevoltperdiv)', then 'int32 resolution = ((int32)signal_adjusters[settings->samplevoltperdiv] >> VOLTAGE_SHIFTER) * vcd->mul_factor;' with no vsf term; same pattern at 1605-1619 in ui_display_vavg. variables.c:1071-1082 shows vertical_scaling_factors 100…50000 (ratios /100…*5 within shown rows); variables.c:991 signal_adjusters {3600000…7600000}, variables.h:297 VOLTAGE_SHIFTER 21.

**Suggested action:** Apply the same conditional rescale to resolution in both functions (resolution = resolution * vsf / 10000 when displayvoltperdiv != samplevoltperdiv), and optionally compute it as (signal_adjusters * mul_factor) >> VOLTAGE_SHIFTER to avoid the truncation. Only affects stop-mode zoomed readouts; live traces are unaffected.

**Verifier:** Asymmetry verified at the cited lines: menu_1014d.c:1786-1790 rescales the value by vertical_scaling_factors[displayvoltperdiv][samplevoltperdiv]/10000 when display != sample volt/div, while the resolution at :1797 is built from signal_adjusters[samplevoltperdiv]>>VOLTAGE_SHIFTER * vcd->mul_factor with no vsf term; identical pattern in ui_display_vavg (:1609-1613 vs :1619). Commit d2fa75c's message confirms the frozen-zoom vsf rescale was added to the Vavg value after being 'caught in the pre-commit review' — the resolution was not given the same treatment. ui_print_value:1883-1888 confirms the semantics (larger resolution drops more decimals), so an un-rescaled resolution is off by exactly the zoom ratio and one zoom direction fabricates sub-ADC-code decimals — the class F28 targeted. Table values verified: signal_adjusters {3600000..7600000} (variables.c:991), VOLTAGE_SHIFTER=21 (variables.h:297), 3600000>>21 = 1.716 -> 1 (~42 % understatement; 3.8M -> ~45 %), vsf ratios /100..*100 (variables.c:~1068+). One detail is inverted, see correction.

**Correction:** The finding's direction mapping is swapped. Verified by a physical-invariance check of the vsf indexing (sample 5V/div displayed at 1V/div -> vsf[display][sample] = 50000 = x5, which keeps the printed voltage constant): zoomed-IN (display finer than sampled) multiplies the value by vsf>1, so the un-rescaled resolution is UNDER-sized -> the cap loop under-caps and prints decimals finer than one ADC code (fabricated precision). Zoomed-OUT (display coarser) shrinks the value (vsf<1), so the passed resolution is OVER-large -> honest decimals get dropped. The finding states the opposite pairing; the defect, its magnitude (the zoom ratio), and the existence of both failure classes are all correct.

*(from code-recent)*

### Acqprobe phase B reads CH1 unconditionally; analyzer per-sample overhead assumes enabled channels only

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/scope_functions.c:2008`
- **Severity:** low

Commit 100f9ff: scope_do_acquisition_probe phase B calls fpga_read_sample_data(&scopesettings.channel1, data) unconditionally while channel 2 is gated on enable (lines 2010-2013) — unlike the standard path it claims to mirror (scope_acquire_trace_data gates CH1 at line 869). Commit 3de9c3d's analyzer computes the phase-B readout overhead per sample as overhead / (nch * samplecount) where nch counts enabled channels (tools/acqprobe_analyze.py:210-223). In the CH1-disabled + CH2-enabled configuration the firmware reads 2 channels (6000 samples) while the analyzer divides by 3000, overstating the µs/sample readout figure ~2x; with both channels disabled the firmware still reads CH1 while the analyzer counts 0 (guarded to print 0). Harmless in the bench runs so far (all CH1-on), but the derived stacking-budget numbers in FPGA_NOTES §capture geometry would be wrong if a CH1-off run is ever used.

**Evidence:** scope_functions.c:2008 'fpga_read_sample_data(&scopesettings.channel1, data);' with no enable guard vs :2010 'if(scopesettings.channel2.enable)'; contrast :869 'if(scopesettings.channel1.enable)' in scope_acquire_trace_data. tools/acqprobe_analyze.py:210-211 'nch = (1 if info.get("ch1_enable") else 0) + (1 if info.get("ch2_enable") else 0); rsamples = nch * info.get("samplecount", 3000)'.

**Suggested action:** Either gate the CH1 read on enable in the probe (matching the standard path being measured) or make the analyzer count CH1 as always-read (nch = 1 + ch2_enable); add a note that existing runs are unaffected (CH1 was enabled in all runs).

**Verifier:** All cited evidence reproduced: scope_functions.c:2008 calls fpga_read_sample_data(&scopesettings.channel1, data) with no enable guard while :2010-2013 gates channel 2 on enable, and the standard path gates CH1 at :869 (phase B's comment at :1995 explicitly claims to mirror scope_acquire_trace_data's translation, and FPGA_NOTES §Readout cost presents phase B as the standard-path cost). tools/acqprobe_analyze.py:210-211 computes nch from ch1_enable/ch2_enable and rsamples = nch*samplecount; :220-223 divides the phase-B overhead by rsamples (guarded 'if rsamples else 0'). So a CH1-off/CH2-on run reads 6000 samples while the analyzer divides by 3000, overstating us/sample ~2x, and a both-off run reads 3000 while the analyzer prints 0. Checked all six bench acqprobe_*.txt files: every run so far is ch1_enable=1 / ch2_enable=0, so the published 2.6-3.1 us/sample and stacking-budget numbers in FPGA_NOTES are unaffected — a latent hazard only, matching the low severity.

*(from code-seams)*

### Stale comment on sm_handle_trim_actions contradicts the symmetric-trim implementation

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/sm_1014d.c:2827`
- **Severity:** low

The header comment above sm_handle_trim_actions still describes the pre-F21(b) behavior: 'Only the ADC2 compensation moves, so the finest possible difference step is available; the half count of common mode this adds is invisible...'. The code below (lines 2855-2863) implements the F21(b) symmetric split documented in PORT_AUDIT.md ('The trim now moves the difference adc2-adc1 symmetrically ... instead of only bumping adc2'): adc1compensation = -(d/2); adc2compensation = d + adc1compensation. The inner comment at 2854 is correct; only the function header is stale. A future reader (or a cal/trim debugging session) acting on the header would mis-model what the trim writes.

**Evidence:** sm_1014d.c:2827-2830 comment 'Only the ADC2 compensation moves, so the finest possible difference step is available; the half count of common mode this adds is invisible next to the DC calibration offsets.' vs sm_1014d.c:2862-2863 'settings->adc1compensation = -(d / 2); settings->adc2compensation = d + settings->adc1compensation;'. PORT_AUDIT.md F21 item (b): 'The trim now moves the difference adc2-adc1 symmetrically (one count/detent, -5/+5 style) instead of only bumping adc2, matching cal.'

**Suggested action:** Rewrite the header comment to describe the symmetric split (difference adc2-adc1 adjusted one count per detent, split -(d/2)/+(d+adc1) so rows read like cal results), dropping the ADC2-only/common-mode sentences.

**Verifier:** Reproduced exactly. The function header comment at sm_1014d.c:2826-2831 still says 'Only the ADC2 compensation moves, so the finest possible difference step is available; the half count of common mode this adds is invisible next to the DC calibration offsets' — describing the pre-F21(b) behavior. The code at sm_1014d.c:2856-2863 implements the symmetric split: 'int32 d = settings->adc2compensation - settings->adc1compensation + setvalue; ... settings->adc1compensation = -(d / 2); settings->adc2compensation = d + settings->adc1compensation;' — both compensations move. The inner comment at 2853-2855 correctly describes the symmetric split ('keep it split symmetrically so the row reads like a cal result (-5/+5), matching what Base cal writes'), and PORT_AUDIT.md's F21 item (b) (lines 443-445: 'The trim now moves the difference adc2-adc1 symmetrically (one count/detent, -5/+5 style) instead of only bumping adc2, matching cal') confirms the header is stale rather than the code being wrong. Low severity is right: doc-only, but a reader debugging cal/trim off the header would mis-model what the trim writes.

*(from code-fpga)*

### Stale timeperdiv>=29 compensation gate in fpga_average_trace_data/_long is dead at every call site and contradicts the F23 doctrine

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:1456`
- **Severity:** low

Commit 77af34b added 'if (scopesettings.timeperdiv >= 29) datatmp += settings->adc1compensation;' to fpga_average_trace_data and fpga_average_trace_data_long, and its own commit message says the gate was 'corrected from timeperdiv>=29 to samplerate==0' — but only in fpga_read_adc_data; these two functions kept the stale >=29 form. Later (F23) the read-path comp became unconditional again ('applied unconditionally at every rate, matching pecostm32', fpga_control.c:1087-1092), leaving these functions triply inconsistent: Atlan4 applied comp unconditionally here, the F23 comment claims always-on, and the actual gate can never be true because fpga_average_trace_data is called only from the long-timebase path (test.c:745/751/952/957/1072-1089) where timeperdiv <= 10. Net effect: adc1compensation is silently never applied to roll-mode reads (possibly fine — 0x24/0x26 averages mix both ADCs — but that rationale is nowhere stated); fpga_average_trace_data_long has no callers at all.

**Evidence:** fpga_control.c:1455-1457 and 1502-1504: 'datatmp = FPGA_GET_DATA(); if (scopesettings.timeperdiv >= 29) datatmp += settings->adc1compensation;'. Atlan4-1.00o5/fpga_control.c (diff): 'datatmp = FPGA_GET_DATA() + settings->adc1compensation;' unconditional. git show 77af34b: 'Interleave compensation apply gate corrected from timeperdiv>=29 to samplerate==0'. Call sites all in test.c long-mode code; long mode is timeperdiv 0-10 (sm_1014d.c:1461).

**Suggested action:** Replace the dead condition with an explicit decision: either drop the comp add with a comment ('roll-mode 0x24/0x26 averages mix both ADCs, comp meaningless') or restore Atlan4's unconditional add; delete or mark fpga_average_trace_data_long as unused.

**Verifier:** fpga_control.c:1455-1457 and 1502-1504 carry the 'if (scopesettings.timeperdiv >= 29) datatmp += settings->adc1compensation;' gate in both average functions. git show 77af34b confirms the exact inconsistency: the commit message says 'Interleave compensation apply gate corrected from timeperdiv>=29 to samplerate==0', yet its diff applies samplerate==0 only in fpga_read_adc_data while adding the >=29 form to fpga_average_trace_data and _long; the pre-image (-) lines show Atlan4's unconditional 'FPGA_GET_DATA() + settings->adc1compensation'. The later F23 pass made the main read path unconditional again with a comment claiming comp is 'applied unconditionally at every rate, matching pecostm32' (fpga_control.c:1087-1093), leaving the triple inconsistency described. Dead-gate claim verified: fpga_average_trace_data's only callers are test.c:745/751/952/957 (scope_get_long_timebase_data/_1 read loops) and test.c:1072-1089 (scope_check_long_trigger), all reachable only via the long_mode route (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:391-393), and long mode means timeperdiv < 11 (sm_1014d.c:1461-1463; roll boundary further tightened to <=6 at 1456-1457), so timeperdiv >= 29 is unreachable there and adc1compensation is silently never applied to roll reads. fpga_average_trace_data_long has zero callers (only the definition at fpga_control.c:1476 and the prototype at fpga_control.h:95).

*(from code-fpga)*

### fw_FPGA==1 never refreshes 0x0E per arm (pecostm32 did every arm); Base cal on the 1013D variant leaves 0x0E stale, and the 1014D build is only saved by a diagnostic-block side effect

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:688`
- **Severity:** low

Atlan4 moved the per-arm fpga_set_time_base out of scope_acquire_trace_data ('2.12 move to reset window', scope_functions.c:759) but the 'reset window' resend in fpga_do_conversion exists only under fw_FPGA > 1 (fpga_control.c:677-689), while pecostm32's 1014D re-sends 0x0E on every armed acquisition (his scope_functions.c:38). So on the stock FPGA the 0x0E auto-mode capture-window value is only correct as long as every code path that changes it restores it. scope_do_baseline_calibration ends by pushing only fpga_set_sample_rate (scope_functions.c:1145), leaving 0x0E at sample_rate_time_per_div[6] (the ~2MSa/s cal value) — on the 1014D build this is corrected only as a side effect of the PORT_1014D diagnostic block restoring saved_tp (scope_functions.c:1459-1463); on a 1013D-variant/stock-FPGA build (diagnostic compiled out, Atlan4-identical code) 0x0E stays stale after Base cal until the next timebase change, subtly changing auto-trigger free-run timing. Fragile: any future edit that removes or reorders the diagnostic block silently reintroduces the staleness on the 1014D too.

**Evidence:** scope_functions.c:759 '//fpga_set_time_base(scopesettings.timeperdiv); 2.12 move to reset window'; fpga_control.c:677-689 'if(fpgasettings.fw_FPGA > 1) { ... fpga_set_time_base(scopesettings.timeperdiv); }'; FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/scope_functions.c:38 'fpga_set_time_base(scopesettings.timeperdiv);' inside the per-arm block; scope_functions.c:1141-1145 cal-exit pushes (voltperdiv/offset x2 + sample rate, no time base), matching Atlan4-1.00o5/scope_functions.c:1091; the accidental restore at scope_functions.c:1446+1459-1463 (saved_tp = user timeperdiv, pushed back inside '#if PORT_1014D').

**Suggested action:** Add an explicit fpga_set_time_base(scopesettings.timeperdiv) beside the fpga_set_sample_rate at the end of scope_do_baseline_calibration (harmless on all paths), and note the fw1 no-per-arm-0x0E contract in FPGA_NOTES.

**Verifier:** Every link in the chain reproduced. (1) Per-arm 0x0E resend exists only under fw_FPGA>1: scope_functions.c:759 has Atlan4's '//fpga_set_time_base(...); 2.12 move to reset window' and the reset-window resend sits inside 'if(fpgasettings.fw_FPGA > 1)' (fpga_control.c:677-689; identical guard in Atlan4-1.00o5/fpga_control.c:643-654), while pecostm32's 1014D re-sends fpga_set_time_base(scopesettings.timeperdiv) inside every armed acquisition (his scope_functions.c ~38, verified in the arm block). (2) Base cal staleness: scope_do_channel_calibration's last unconditional 0x0E write is fpga_set_time_base(sample_rate_time_per_div[6]) at scope_functions.c:1366 (after the per-rate loop pushes at 1302-1305), and scope_do_baseline_calibration's exit restores voltperdiv/offset x2 + fpga_set_sample_rate only (scope_functions.c:1141-1145), byte-matching Atlan4-1.00o5/scope_functions.c:~1087-1091 — no time-base push. (3) The accidental save on 1014D: the diagnostic block at scope_functions.c:1445-1464 (saved_tp captured at 1446 while scopesettings.timeperdiv still holds the user value — cal never reassigns it, the reassignments at 1130-1135 are commented out) ends with fpga_set_time_base(saved_tp) at 1463, and that block is inside '#if PORT_1014D' (guard 1438, #endif 1465), so a 1013D-variant/stock-FPGA build compiles it out and leaves FPGA 0x0E at the cal value until the next timebase change. The fragility conclusion (any removal/reorder of the diagnostic block silently reintroduces the staleness on the 1014D) follows directly; low severity is right since the 1013D variant is untested on hardware and the 1014D is currently covered.

*(from code-guards)*

### 1014D get_fattime returns 0 — invalid FAT timestamp on every file the scope writes, diverging from the pecostm32 1014D reference

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/DS3231.c:379`
- **Severity:** low

The #else (PORT_1014D) branch of get_fattime returns 0. A packed FAT timestamp of 0 has month=0 and day=0, which is outside the valid FAT ranges (month 1-12, day 1-31), so every screenshot/waveform the 1014D saves gets an invalid directory timestamp that PCs render as a nonsense date. The behavior reference for anything 1014D (pecostm32's official firmware) instead returns a fixed valid timestamp (1349957149). FF_FS_NORTC is 0 (ffconf.h:240), so FatFs really calls this and stores the value verbatim.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/DS3231.c:343-380: get_fattime body under #if !PORT_1014D with "#else return 0; #endif" (else at 378, return at 379). ffconf.h:240 "#define FF_FS_NORTC 0". Reference FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/diskio.c:197-201: "DWORD get_fattime (void) { //Some date and time value return(1349957149); }". Our diskio.c:197-203 copy is commented out (block comment), so DS3231.c's is the single live definition.

**Suggested action:** Return a fixed valid packed timestamp in the #else branch (e.g. the reference's 1349957149, or a build-date constant from build_time.h) instead of 0.

**Verifier:** All evidence reproduced: fnirsi_1013d_scope/DS3231.c:341-381 defines get_fattime with the body under #if !PORT_1014D and '#else return 0; #endif' (else at 378, return 0 at 379), so every 1014D build returns 0. ffconf.h:240 has FF_FS_NORTC 0 and line 11 FF_FS_READONLY 0, so FatFs (ff.c:298 GET_FATTIME() -> get_fattime()) really calls it on writes and stores the value in directory entries. A packed FAT value of 0 decodes to year 1980, month 0, day 0 — outside the valid FAT ranges (month 1-12, day 1-31), i.e. an invalid timestamp on every file the 1014D writes. The behavior reference FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/diskio.c:197-201 instead returns 1349957149, which decodes to the valid 2020-03-22 22:48:58. Our diskio.c copy (lines 197-203) is inside a /* */ block, so DS3231.c's is the single live definition (confirmed by grep: only DS3231.c:341 is live). Severity low is appropriate.

*(from code-guards)*

### Unguarded RTC display block in the 1014D main loop can paint over P14 chrome if onoffRTC is restored as 1, with no 1014D path to clear it

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:454`
- **Severity:** low

The main-loop block "if (!(timerRTC)&&(onoffRTC)) { readtime(); ... display_fill_rect(TIME_TEXT_...); display_text(..., buffertime); }" has no variant guard. On the 1014D, readtime() is a compiled-out stub (DS3231.c:13), buffertime is never filled, and the block would black-fill the TIME_TEXT rect over the P14 layout every 500 ticks. It is dormant only because onoffRTC defaults to 0 (scope_functions.c:7838) — but onoffRTC is restored verbatim from the SD config sector (scope_functions.c:8171), whose format is shared with the Atlan4 1013D variant, and the only code that sets it to 1 is the 1013D touch toggle (statemachine.c:1479), unreachable on 1014D. So an SD card whose config sector was written by an Atlan4-1013D with RTC enabled leaves a 1014D permanently redrawing a black rectangle with empty text, with no on-device way to disable it short of a factory reset.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:453-466: unguarded "//READ and show RTC time / if (!(timerRTC)&&(onoffRTC)) { timerRTC=500; readtime(); ... display_text(TIME_TEXT_XPOS, TIME_TEXT_YPOS, buffertime); }". DS3231.c:13-54: readtime() body entirely inside #if !PORT_1014D. scope_functions.c:8171 "onoffRTC = *ptr++;" (settings restore); scope_functions.c:7838 "onoffRTC = 0;" (preset). grep shows no onoffRTC writes in sm_1014d.c/menu_1014d.c; the only toggle is statemachine.c:1479 "onoffRTC ^= 1;" (1013D touch path).

**Suggested action:** Wrap the block in #if !PORT_1014D (matching the other RTC call sites' treatment), or force onoffRTC = 0 in the PORT_1014D settings-restore path in scope_functions.c.

**Verifier:** Structural claims all reproduced: fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:453-467 is outside the variant guard (its #endif is at line 449) — the RTC block runs in both variants' main loop; DS3231.c:11-55 shows readtime()'s entire body inside #if !PORT_1014D (empty stub on 1014D, so it links but never fills buffertime — variables.c:93 char buffertime[9], zero-init BSS); when onoffRTC==1 the block black-fills TIME_TEXT (123,3,48x13 per variables.h:383-386, inside the P14 top bar) every 500 ticks and draws an empty string. onoffRTC: preset 0 at scope_functions.c:7838, restored verbatim from the config sector at 8171 (scope_restore_config_data), saved at 7953 (so once 1 it persists), and grep over all sources confirms the ONLY code setting it to 1 is statemachine.c:1479 (touch config menu, unreachable on 1014D since touch_handler() is only called on the 1013D branch of the main loop). Factory 'Restore defaults' clears it via the preset — matching the finding's own 'short of a factory reset' qualifier.

**Correction:** The trigger scenario as worded is partially wrong: a config sector written by PRISTINE upstream Atlan4 1013D firmware (v1.00o5) would be REJECTED — scope_restore_config_data (scope_functions.c:8088) checks SETTING_SECTOR_VERSION, and Atlan4-1.00o5 uses VERSION_LOW 0x0015 while this tree uses 0x0016 (variables.h:123 vs Atlan4-1.00o5 variables.h:109), so onoffRTC would fall back to the preset 0. The scenario is live only for a sector written by THIS tree's own 1013D-variant build (PORT_1014D=0, same 0x0016 version, identical layout, toggle reachable via touch) whose SD card then boots a 1014D build. Latent hazard stands, but narrower than 'any Atlan4-1013D'.

*(from code-gotchas)*

### compute_all_math_ops int8 conversion is asymmetric: mul/div math modes produce sign-wrong results

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/ref_and_math.c:147`
- **Severity:** low

`int8 a = A[i];` wraps raw ADC bytes >=128 to negative (a mid-screen trace at ADC 128 becomes -128) while `int8 b = B[i]-128;` is properly centered. For add/sub (mathmode 1/2) the wrap cancels mod 256 when stored back into the uint8 math_tracebuffer, but for multiply (mode 3) and divide (mode 4) the operand sign matters: a trace sitting at/above mid-scale multiplies/divides as a large negative number, producing garbage. On the 1014D build mathmode is only reachable via restored configuration (no UI sets it; set-sites are in 1013D-only statemachine.c:2807-2925), so exposure is mainly the 1013D variant. Pre-existing: ref_and_math.c is byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5.

**Evidence:** ref_and_math.c:147-153 `int8 a = A[i]; int8 b = B[i]-128; ... result = (a * b); ... a / b`; A/B are uint8* into raw trace buffers (get_channel_data, ref_and_math.c:159-168); math_tracebuffer is uint8* (variables.h:748); empty diff vs Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/ref_and_math.c.

**Suggested action:** Center both operands the same way (int32 a = A[i]-128, b = B[i]-128), compute in int32, then offset/clamp into 0..255 when storing.

**Verifier:** Reproduced at ref_and_math.c:147-155: 'int8 a = A[i]; int8 b = B[i]-128;' with A/B being uint8* into raw trace buffers (get_channel_data at 159-168) and results stored to uint8 math_tracebuffer. int8 is signed char (types.h:11), so (int8)128 == -128 as claimed. The mod-256 analysis holds: for add, a+b === A + B - 128 (mod 256), which equals the properly-centered-and-recentered (A-128)+(B-128)+128; for sub likewise A-B+128 — so add/sub come out right despite the asymmetry. For multiply and divide the congruence does not carry through (mul differs by 128*b mod 256 for odd b vs centered operands, and integer division has no modular structure at all: e.g. A=192,b=10 gives -64/10=-6 vs centered 64/10=+6), so a trace at/above ADC 128 operates as a large negative operand — sign-wrong results as claimed. Exposure claim verified: mathmode is written only in statemachine.c:2807-2925 (1013D-only UI) and the config restore at scope_functions.c:8177; no sm_1014d.c/menu_1014d.c site sets it. Pre-existing confirmed: ref_and_math.c is byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5.

*(from code-gotchas)*

### X-Y mode indexes the trace buffer with disp_trigger_index-315 without a floor: negative-index OOB reads

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/scope_functions.c:3707`
- **Severity:** low

In X-Y display mode `uint32 index = disp_trigger_index - 315;` (and -317 in the thumbnail path at line 6963) underflows when the trigger is found early in the buffer (disp_trigger_index is simply the sample index where the trigger search matched, scope_functions.c:955, or fpgasettings.settriggerpoint at 3468). The wrapped uint32 converts back to a negative int32 in scope_get_x_sample/scope_get_y_sample (int32 index parameter, scope_functions.c:3801), which read `settings->tracebuffer[index]` with no bounds check — up to 315 bytes below the buffer. Outputs are clamped to the screen (3836-3848), so the effect is garbage pixels in the X-Y trace/thumbnail, not wild writes. Pre-existing: identical code in Atlan4-1.00o5 (scope_functions.c:2793 display path, 6014 thumbnail path).

**Evidence:** scope_functions.c:3707-3708, 6963-6964, 3801-3806 (unchecked tracebuffer[index]), 3836-3848 output clamps; Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/scope_functions.c:2793 and 6014 contain the same expressions.

**Suggested action:** Clamp index to >= 0 (or skip drawing) when disp_trigger_index < 315/317; cheap guard in both X-Y paths.

**Verifier:** The cited code is exactly as described and I verified it all: scope_functions.c:3707 'uint32 index = disp_trigger_index - 315;' (X-Y display), 6963 '- 317' (thumbnail), scope_get_x_sample/scope_get_y_sample take int32 index (3801/3853) and read settings->tracebuffer[index] with no bounds check (3806), outputs clamped to screen at 3836-3848 so the effect is bounded to garbage pixels from OOB reads; identical expressions exist in pristine Atlan4 (scope_functions.c:2793 and 6014), so pre-existing. The unchecked-underflow defect is real; only the stated activation path needs correcting (see correction), which does not change the low-severity latent-hazard conclusion.

**Correction:** The claimed trigger condition ('when the trigger is found early in the buffer') cannot occur in this tree or in pristine Atlan4: scope_process_trigger is called with fpgasettings.settriggerpoint (scope_functions.c:899) and scans only index = count-20 for 40 samples (938-940; same in Atlan4), so disp_trigger_index is always within +/-20 of settriggerpoint, whose only in-tree values are the default 1500 (7829), config restore (8270), and 1013D statemachine.c values samplecount/4 / /2 / -1000 (750/1500/2000 at default samplecount) — minimum ~730, far above 315. The underflow is reachable only through the unvalidated view-file/config restores (disp_trigger_index = ptr[index++] at scope_functions.c:5516 and menu_1014d.c:3317), i.e. a corrupt or hand-crafted file on the SD card, not normal acquisition.

*(from code-gotchas)*

### uart1_receive_data blocks forever on the key controller every main-loop iteration

**Resolved 2026-10-16:** commit `9aad854` replaces the scope application's blocking
UART transactions with nonblocking polling and 20 ms retries. Modal confirmations
still deliberately wait for a key. See PORT_AUDIT.md §5k; the finding below is historical.

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/uart.c:54`
- **Severity:** low

uart1_receive_data() spins on `(*UART1_LS_REG & UART_LSR_TEMT) == 0` (line 48) and then on `(*UART1_LS_REG & UART_LSR_DR) == 0` (line 54) with no timeout. It is called every main-loop pass via sm_handle_user_input -> uart1_get_user_input (sm_1014d.c:72, fnirsi_1013d_scope.c main loop), because the protocol is poll-response (write 0xFF, wait for the controller's answer, even when no key is pressed). If the front-panel key-controller MCU is absent, held in reset, or wedged, the DR wait never completes and the entire scope (acquisition + display, not just input) freezes with no recovery. uart1_wait_for_user_input (line 81) has the same property at its call sites (calibration prompts, screenshot viewer). Provenance: uart.c is byte-identical (whitespace only) to pecostm32's official 1014D firmware, and Atlan4 has no uart.c at all — so this is inherited reference behavior of the import source, not a port regression; CLAUDE.md already documents the blocking poll. Reported for completeness of the hazard class.

**Evidence:** uart.c:45-58 (both unbounded while loops), uart.c:78-82; sm_1014d.c:69-76 calls uart1_get_user_input each main-loop pass; `diff uart.c ../FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/uart.c` shows a single whitespace-only difference; no uart.c exists in Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/.

**Suggested action:** If robustness is wanted beyond pecostm32 parity, bound the DR wait with a counter timeout (like the FPGA ready waits at fpga_control.c:653-671) and return 0 (no key) on expiry; otherwise leave as documented reference behavior.

**Verifier:** Reproduced: uart1_receive_data (uart.c:45-58) spins unbounded on UART_LSR_TEMT (line 48), writes the 0xFF poll byte, then spins unbounded on UART_LSR_DR (line 54); uart1_wait_for_user_input (78-82) loops on uart1_receive_data. Called every main-loop pass: fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:441 sm_handle_user_input -> uart1_get_user_input (sm_1014d.c:69-76) -> uart1_receive_data, so an absent/wedged key-controller MCU stalls acquisition and display with no timeout or recovery; uart1_wait_for_user_input call sites in scope_functions.c:1190/1954/2117 and menu_1014d.c:204/5049/5097 have the same property. Provenance verified: diff against the pecostm32 reference uart.c shows exactly one whitespace-only difference (line 71), and Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/ has no uart.c — inherited reference behavior, already documented as a blocking poll in CLAUDE.md. The finding's own framing (low severity, hazard-class completeness, not a port regression) is accurate.

*(from code-gotchas)*

### display_character(int8) sign-extends codes >= 128: the degree-sign glyph (176) never renders

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/display_lib.c:1484`
- **Severity:** low

display_character takes `int8 text` and casts `(uint16)text` (lines 1494/1499): any character code >= 128 becomes 0xFF80+, so check_char_in_vw_font's range test (display_lib.c:1608-1615, first_char/last_char) fails and nothing is drawn. menu.c:6838 passes literal 176 (0xB0, degree sign) for the generator menu — int8 receives -80, lookup gets 0xFFB0, glyph silently dropped. display_text (line 1505) is immune because plain char is unsigned on this target (no -fsigned-char in CFLAGS, nbproject/Makefile-Debug.mk:86). menu.c is 1013D-variant-only code and the same signature/call exist in pristine Atlan4 (display_lib.c:1488, menu.c:6787), so this is pre-existing; it becomes visible only if/when the 1013D variant is hardware-tested.

**Evidence:** display_lib.c:1484-1500 (`int8 text`, `(uint16)text` casts), 1608-1615 range check; menu.c:6838 `display_character(..., 176)`; Makefile-Debug.mk:86 CFLAGS has neither -fsigned-char nor -funsigned-char (arm-none-eabi default: unsigned char); Atlan4-1.00o5 display_lib.c:1488 and menu.c:6787 identical.

**Suggested action:** Change the parameter to uint8 (or cast via uint8 before widening to uint16) so codes 128-255 reach the font lookup intact.

**Verifier:** Reproduced: display_character(uint32, uint32, int8 text) at display_lib.c:1484 casts '(uint16)text' at 1494/1499; int8 is signed char (types.h:11), so a literal 176 arrives as -80 and sign-extends to 0xFFB0. check_char_in_vw_font (display_lib.c:1607-1615) tests character >= first_char && <= last_char per range; font_3 — the font active at the call (display_set_font(&font_3) at menu.c:6811) — has ranges 0x20-0x7E and 0xA0-0xFF (font_3.c:412/416), so the 0xB0 degree glyph EXISTS in the font but 0xFFB0 fails both ranges and nothing is drawn: the defect is real, not moot. menu.c:6838 passes literal 176 exactly as cited (generator menu, 1013D-variant UI). display_text (1505) is immune as claimed: it dereferences plain char, CFLAGS (nbproject/Makefile-Debug.mk:86) has neither -fsigned-char nor -funsigned-char, and arm-none-eabi-gcc defaults char to unsigned. Pre-existing confirmed: Atlan4-1.00o5 has the identical display_character signature (display_lib.c:1488) and the identical call (menu.c:6787).

### 0x3C is never sent by this tree — the 'init write' is fully commented out

- **Where:** `FPGA_NOTES.md:59`
- **Severity:** low

The command table (headed 'as used in this tree') lists 0x3C as a '"battery level"-ish init write (short 32431), purpose still unclear', and the special-IC section (lines 371-372) says 'our init's odd 0x3C write is this handshake's vestige' — both implying the write happens. The only writer, fpga_set_battery_level(), is wrapped in a block comment and its sole call site in main() is also commented out (same in pristine Atlan4), so this tree never sends 0x3C. Unlike the 0x28 row, no dead-code marker is present. Also, 'purpose still unclear' at line 59 is superseded by the doc's own special-IC decode (touch-panel-address loopback / anti-clone handshake).

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:1526-1535: `/* void fpga_set_battery_level(void) { fpga_write_cmd(0x3C); fpga_write_short(32431); } */`; fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:187-188 `//fpga_set_battery_level(); //Only called here and in hardware check`; fpga_control.h:97 prototype commented. grep for other 0x3C writes in fnirsi_1013d_scope/*.c finds none.

**Suggested action:** Mark the 0x3C row as dead/commented-out in this tree and cross-reference the special-IC section for its decoded purpose.

**Verifier:** The only 0x3C command writer, fpga_set_battery_level(), is wrapped in a /* */ block comment (fpga_control.c:1526-1535), its sole call site is commented (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:188 '//fpga_set_battery_level();'), and the prototype is commented (fpga_control.h:97) — identical in pristine Atlan4 (reference fpga_control.c:1437-1446), and grep finds no other fpga_write_cmd(0x3C) in active source. So this tree never sends 0x3C, yet FPGA_NOTES.md:59 lists it in the 'as used in this tree' inventory with no dead-code marker (unlike the 0x28 row), and lines 369-370 say 'our init's odd 0x3C write is this handshake's vestige', implying the write happens.

### 0x0B/0x0C table rows omit the /2 halving of the written values

- **Where:** `FPGA_NOTES.md:66`
- **Severity:** low

The Atlan4-additions table says 0x0B writes 'fpgasettings.settriggerpoint' and 0x0C 'fpgasettings.totalsamples'. The code writes settriggerpoint/2 and totalsamples/2 (per-ADC counts: 750 and 1500 for the 1500/3000 defaults). For a doc that serves as the fw2/fw3 protocol contract, the halving is load-bearing for anyone re-implementing or porting the path.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:677-685: `if(fpgasettings.fw_FPGA > 1) { fpga_write_cmd(0x0B); fpga_write_short(fpgasettings.settriggerpoint/2); fpga_write_cmd(0x0C); fpga_write_short(fpgasettings.totalsamples/2); ... }`; defaults scope_functions.c:7828-7829 (totalsamples=3000 '(1500*2)', settriggerpoint=1500 '(750*2)').

**Suggested action:** Amend the two rows to note the /2 (values sent are per-ADC halves).

**Verifier:** fpga_control.c:677-685 (the only 0x0B/0x0C write sites, per grep) writes fpga_write_short(fpgasettings.settriggerpoint/2) and fpga_write_short(fpgasettings.totalsamples/2) under fw_FPGA > 1, while FPGA_NOTES.md:66-67 documents the unhalved 'fpgasettings.settriggerpoint' / 'fpgasettings.totalsamples'. Defaults at scope_functions.c:7828-7829 (totalsamples=3000 '(1500*2)', settriggerpoint=1500 '(750*2)') confirm the written values are per-ADC counts (1500/750) — the /2 is load-bearing for anyone re-implementing the fw2/fw3 path from the doc.

### Sector-710 table row lists readers/writers that don't exist on the current 1014D chain

- **Where:** `BOOT_NOTES.md:60`
- **Severity:** low

The SD-sector-map row for 710 says 'both loaders + display_control.c read; scope_reset_config_data() and the sm boot-switch write'. On the tree this map claims to describe ('as used by this tree', default 1014D variant): pecostm32's 1014D loader never reads sector 710 from SD — its display_control.c only validates the DRAM mirror at 0x81BFFC00, which nothing populates (as the doc's own next paragraph states); the 'sm boot-switch' was removed 2026-07-09 (doc's own line 38); and scope_reset_config_data()'s sector-710 write is #if !PORT_1014D-guarded (doc's own lines 66-73). Each correction exists elsewhere in the doc, but the table — the quick-reference artifact — describes the 1013D chain / pre-2026-07-09 state without saying so.

**Evidence:** FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_startup/: no sd_card_read of sector 710 anywhere (grep '710|DISPLAY_CONFIG|0x81BFFC' hits only display_control.c:12,51 = DRAM mirror validation); the sector-710 SD read exists only in the Atlan4 1013D-chain loader ('Bootloader fw0.02...'/fw0.04 fnirsi_1014d_startup.c:121). fnirsi_1013d_scope/scope_functions.c:7849-7855: `ptr[0] = 4;` then `#if !PORT_1014D ... sd_card_write(DISPLAY_CONFIG_SECTOR, 1, (uint8 *)0x81BFFC00); #endif`. No STARTUP_CONFIG writer remains in sm_1014d.c (grep).

**Suggested action:** Annotate the row per-chain: '1013D chain: loader reads into DRAM + scope reset writes; 1014D chain: nobody reads the sector from SD, write guarded out' — matching the body text.

**Verifier:** BOOT_NOTES.md:60's row says 'both loaders + display_control.c read; scope_reset_config_data() and the sm boot-switch write' under a header 'as used by this tree' (line 52), but: (a) pecostm32's 1014D startup (the committed 1014D-chain loader) has no sd_card_read of sector 710 anywhere — grep hits only display_control.c:12/51, the DRAM-mirror (0x81BFFC00) validation, which the doc's own lines 62-64 say nothing populates; the sector-710 SD read exists only in the 1013D-chain loaders (fw0.04's modified fnirsi_1014d_startup.c:121); (b) scope_reset_config_data()'s write is #if !PORT_1014D-guarded (scope_functions.c:7850-7855), so it never fires on the default variant; (c) the sm boot-switch was removed 2026-07-09 per the doc's own line 38, and grep finds no STARTUP_CONFIG writer in sm_1014d.c. The table row aggregates the 1013D-chain/pre-fix state without saying so, each correction living elsewhere in the same doc — exactly as the finding states.

## Refuted by adversarial verification (kept for the record)

- **Top-pointer bridge lost P14's clipping: negative-x 'H' pointer draw wraps to an out-of-bounds write** (code-seams, high) — The divergence evidence is real and I reproduced all of it: P14's display_top_pointer(int32 xpos, int8 id) clips via startpixel/lastpixel with early returns (reference FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/display_lib.c:1427-1459); ours is Atlan4's unclipped display_top_pointer(uint32,uint32,int8) (display_lib.c:1363-1373) over display_copy_icon_fg_color's raw 'ptr = screenbuffer + xpos + (ypos*pixels)' (display_lib.c:1206-1217); the call site menu_1014d.c:577-580 kept P14's partial-visibility band verbatim (HORIZONTAL_POINTER_LEFT=6, WIDTH=15 per variables.h:167/169, so positions -8..6 pass xpos -15..-1); Atlan4's own call sites pre-guard position<3 (Atlan4-1.00o5 menu.c:9356-9366, scope_functions.c:631-643); and the band is reachable (trigger_position_min = (TRACE_HORIZONTAL_END - disp_xrange)+5 goes deeply negative at fast timebases, scope_functions.c:3406/3416, with sm_set_trigger_position clamping only to min/max, sm_1014d.c:1314-1323; ui_draw_pointers runs per frame at scope_functions.c:3772). But the finding's conclusion — an out-of-bounds write up to ~30 bytes BEFORE displaybuffertmp corrupting whatever global precedes it — is arithmetically wrong. The 'H' call passes ypos = TRACE_VERTICAL_START = 59 (variables.h:144/152) and pixelsperline = SCREEN_WIDTH = 800 (display_lib.c:47-52, menu_1014d.c:24), so the destination pointer is screenbuffer + (xpos + ypos*pixels) where xpos+ypos*pixels = 0xFFFFFFF1 + 47200 = 47185 (mod 2^32): 47185 pixels INTO the 384000-pixel buffer, not before it. Every write of the 21-row icon stays in-bounds (max offset ~ 79*800+798 << 384000), and display_character's draw_vw_character (display_lib.c:1572) uses the same dominating '(ypos+y)*pixelsperline + xpos' sum. A write before the buffer would require ypos=0, which no caller passes. The finding's first-row-OOB claim silently dropped the +ypos*pixels term.
- **uint16 triggerverticalposition legally stores negative values: trigger level snaps to opposite rail** (code-gotchas, high) — The storage wrap is real (uint16 at variables.h:760 vs int16 in reference variables.h:459; negative positions down to ~traceposition-219 are storable wrapped, verified at sm_1014d.c:1339-1372), but the claimed failure does not occur. Both level computations multiply the position difference by 2^21 — fpga_control.c:512 uses '* 2097152' and sm_1014d.c:1356 uses '<< VOLTAGE_SHIFTER' (21) — and (x + 65536) * 2^21 == x * 2^21 (mod 2^32), so the mod-2^16 wrap is annihilated: the int32 result is bit-identical to what the int16 reference computes (the true difference is clamp-bounded to |diff| <= ~460 < 1024, so the true product always fits int32). I verified this empirically: a test compiling the exact expressions at -O2 gives identical levels for wrapped-uint16 vs int16 inputs (7==7, 1==1, -5==-5, -203==-203), and arm-none-eabi-gcc -O2 compiles fpga_set_trigger_level's multiply as 'lsl r0, r0, #21', which wraps mod 2^32. Therefore the immediate fpga_set_trigger_level() push computes the same small/negative level as the reference and clamps toward the BOTTOM rail (the direction the user dialed), not 255/top; the next detent likewise takes the level<0 branch and re-clamps to the lower limit exactly as the reference does — it never parks at traceposition+218. Since int16 and uint16 have identical 16-bit bit patterns, the += at line 1600 and the config save/restore (scope_functions.c:7924/8136, uint16* buffer, no clamping on restore) are also behavior-identical to the reference. Triggering does not stop and the level never snaps to the opposite rail.
- **F25 0x28/0x00 restoration is #if PORT_1014D-guarded, excluding the stock-FPGA 1013D variant that pecostm32's 1013D firmware also feeds 0x28** (code-fpga, low) — The code facts are accurate — fpga_control.c:631-642 wraps the restored 0x29/0x01 + 0x28/0x00 in '#if PORT_1014D' plus fw_FPGA==1, and FNIRSI_1013D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:495-496 sends 0x28/0x00 unconditionally with no 0x29 anywhere in that file — but the finding's actual complaint, that 'neither the code comment nor PORT_AUDIT F25 records that the 1013D variant is intentionally left on the pre-F25 behavior', is factually wrong. PORT_AUDIT.md's F25 entry states explicitly: 'Fix applied (fpga_do_conversion, #if PORT_1014D + fw_FPGA==1 so the untestable 1013D and the PECO fw_FPGA>1 paths are untouched)'. That is precisely a record of deliberate conservatism, with its rationale (the 1013D variant has never been hardware-tested), in the exact document the finding says lacks it. The finding misses this sentence in the F25 text it otherwise cites.

## Coverage critic — what this review did NOT look at

The 10-agent review was broad and evidence-disciplined on its planned dimensions — the five doc groups were read end-to-end with claim-level verification, the imported-module seams and FPGA paths were diffed hunk-by-hunk, and the guard/gotcha sweeps were systematic. The misses concentrate in five places. (1) Verdict integrity: the three REFUTED verdicts include one that directly contradicts another agent's CONFIRMED finding on the same lines (the F25 0x28 restore IS '#if PORT_1014D'-guarded at fpga_control.c:629 while pecostm32's 1013D upstream sends 0x28/0x00 at its fpga_control.c:495 — the 'refuted' claim is factually true), and 2 of 3 REFUTEDs were the reviewers' high-severity findings, so the refutation reasoning deserves a spot-audit. (2) One false CLAUDE.md build claim nobody caught: the 'checked-in .c sources' for mksunxi/flashfilepacker do not exist anywhere in the tree — only the opaque committed binaries. (3) Whole infrastructure files were never opened: start.s, arm32.h, the linker script, interrupt.c (D-cache bit is set with no MMU anywhere, so the data side actually runs uncached; stacks are hard-wired with no guard), and the plan's stack/cache/alignment hazard classes were not executed. (4) IRQ-context concurrency was never hunted, and the USB mass-storage path — raw SD reads/writes from interrupt context against the same card the main loop uses via FatFs — went unaudited along with BOOT_NOTES' sector-0-exposure claim. (5) test.c, which is in the build for both variants and implements the roll-mode path the docs track as broken (F21/ROADMAP 12), was read only at one loop; the tracked sibling loader trees (129 files) were never even listed. The FatFs/SD/display_lib layer was covered only by byte-diff against Atlan4 — a defensible priority call, but it should be recorded as diffed-not-audited rather than reviewed. None of these gaps undermines the review's shipped findings; they define the next, much smaller pass.

- **Verdict integrity: cross-agent contradiction on the F25 1013D guard (REFUTED vs CONFIRMED on the same lines)** — code-guards CONFIRMED (medium) that the F25 0x28/0x29 restore is 1014D-only and the 1013D variant keeps the regression, while code-fpga's same-substance finding was REFUTED (low). I re-verified the facts: the restore in /home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c is inside '#if PORT_1014D' (line 629, closing #endif line 641), and pecostm32's 1013D upstream sends 0x28/0x00 unconditionally in fpga_do_conversion (reference FNIRSI_1013D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c:494-496, 'Set the FPGA for short time base mode'). The factual claim in the REFUTED finding is true, so either the verify pass erred or it refuted on unstated grounds (e.g. 'Atlan4's shipped 1013D also omitted it'), which is exactly the F25 bug class. More broadly, 2 of the 3 REFUTED verdicts across the review were the reviewers' 'high' findings — the costliest place for a verify-pass error. Spot-check of the refuted triggerverticalposition claim: the level-rotary path IS clamped (sm_1014d.c:1362-1372), so that refutation looks plausibly right, but a second writer 'scopesettings.triggerverticalposition += delta' at sm_1014d.c:1600 is deliberately unclamped ('without worry about limit' comment) and was not discussed by either side.
  - Follow-up: Re-adjudicate the F25-guard pair against the two file locations above and record one verdict (the CONFIRMED one matches the code). Then have one agent audit the refutation reasoning of the two high REFUTEDs (menu_1014d.c top-pointer clipping; sm_1014d.c triggerverticalposition, including the unclamped writer at line 1600) — verify passes overturning top-severity claims deserve a second pair of eyes.
- **False CLAUDE.md build claim: mksunxi/flashfilepacker sources are NOT checked in** — CLAUDE.md line 107 says host gcc can 'rebuild mksunxi/flashfilepacker if needed' and line 128-129 says to 'rebuild from the checked-in .c sources'. No such sources exist anywhere: 'git ls-files | grep -i sunxi\|packer' returns only the ELF binaries (fnirsi_1013d_scope/mksunxi, fnirsi_1013d_scope/flashfilepacker, plus copies in the sibling loader trees), and 'find . -name mksunxi*.c -o -name *packer*.c' over the whole checkout (vendored trees included) returns nothing. doc-claude-repo verified the neighbouring chmod claim but never tested this one. Consequence: a user told to rebuild the tools hunts for nonexistent files, and every build's final image passes through two opaque committed host binaries with no in-tree source — an unauditable link in the flash chain that no agent examined (doc-claude-repo also explicitly skipped hash-verifying bootloader provenance).
  - Follow-up: Fix CLAUDE.md (drop or correct the 'checked-in .c sources' claim at /home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/CLAUDE.md lines 107 and 128-129), locate the actual upstream sources (pecostm32's repos/EEVblog) and either vendor them or document where they live; optionally hash-compare the committed binaries against a rebuild.
- **USB stack + mass-storage internals and the IRQ-vs-main-loop concurrency hazard class — never executed** — The only USB finding filed was the CDC 'f' debug command; code-gotchas explicitly skipped 'mass_storage_class.c/cdc_class.c protocol internals' and 'interrupt handling'. Facts I verified: USB service is interrupt-driven (setup_interrupt(USB_IRQ_NUM,...) at /home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/usb_interface.c:138/153/195/204), and mass_storage_class.c performs raw sd_card_read/sd_card_write in that IRQ context (lines 368, 481, 512, 589), giving the PC raw-sector write access to the same card the main loop uses via FatFs (screenshot/wave/config/acqprobe writes). Nobody verified that the 1014D USB view (sm_1014d.c:2978 sm_start_usb_export -> ui_setup_usb_screen) actually blocks all main-loop SD/FatFs activity while mounted, audited SCSI LBA/length bounds, or checked the BOOT_NOTES claim that USB MSC exposes sector 0 (doc-fpga-boot listed it as explicitly unverified). The SCSI buffer aliasing onto viewthumbnaildata (mass_storage_class.c:352/420/481) is also unexamined.
  - Follow-up: One targeted agent: read mass_storage_class.c (619 lines) + the usb_device_irq_handler dispatch, confirm ui_setup_usb_screen is a modal loop that serializes SD access (and that no timer/other ISR path touches SD concurrently), check scsi_start_lba/scsi_available_blocks bounds vs card capacity and the viewthumbnaildata aliasing, and settle the sector-0-exposure claim in BOOT_NOTES.
- **CPU-infrastructure layer never opened: start.s, arm32.h, fnirsi_1013d.ld, interrupt.c (stack sizing, cache coherency, alignment classes unexecuted)** — No agent names any of these files. Facts I verified: (1) main() enables I-cache and D-cache (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:56-57, 'Instead of full memory management just the caches enabled') but nothing anywhere calls arm32_mmu_enable/arm32_ttb_set (grep over all first-party code) — on ARM926EJ-S the D-cache C-bit is inert with the MMU off, so the data side actually runs uncached: the comment is misleading, a large performance win is silently forfeited, and anyone later enabling the MMU with a flat cacheable map would break FPGA PE-bus reads, the DEBE framebuffer scanout, and USB — with no doc warning. (2) The linker script /home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d.ld defines no stack or heap region at all; the six mode stacks are hard-wired in start.s lines ~139-150 (0x81FB5000..0x82000000, descending) with no overflow guard, and fixed-address data (display-config page 0x81BFFC00, the deliberately unaligned STARTUP_CONFIG_ADDRESS uint32* at 0x81BFFC1F, variables.h:108) sits below them unprotected. (3) interrupt.c (69 lines) was read by nobody. All four files are byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5 (I diffed), so this is inherited — but the review plan's 'stack sizing / cache coherency / ARM926 alignment' hazard classes were simply not run.
  - Follow-up: Short single pass: read interrupt.c + the IRQ frame in start.s, document the D-cache-inert-without-MMU fact in FPGA_NOTES or AGENTS.md (and add 'enable MMU + write-through/flush discipline' as a ROADMAP perf item), note the stack map and the buffers below it, and grep first-party code for misaligned uint32*/uint16* casts onto byte buffers beyond the already-documented 0x...1F quirk.
- **test.c is load-bearing production code (long-timebase/roll path) and essentially unread** — test.c is in the build (Makefile-Debug.mk:78) with no variant guard (compiled into BOTH variants) and contains the entire long-timebase/roll implementation the docs actively track as broken (F21 'roll renderer geometry-broken', ROADMAP 12): scope_get_long_timebase_data/-data1, scope_preset_long_mode, scope_check_long_trigger with a hard-coded '+25 compensation' magic (test.c:1063) that bypasses the per-channel input_calibration (the calibrated forms are commented out in favour of a global 'multiply'), plus soft-float DSP helpers (double/size_t sinc/FIR/EMA on ARM926) and scope_test_* diagnostics. Of ~1411 lines, only 'the roll-mode read loop' was read (code-fpga); code-seams and code-gotchas both scoped it out. The file most relevant to the open roll-mode defect fell between every dimension.
  - Follow-up: Targeted review of /home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/test.c: the roll acquisition/trigger path (signedness, bounds, the +25 magic and stale 'multiply' scaling vs the calibrated path, triggerverticalposition semantics), reachability of the scope_test_*/DSP helpers (dead-code candidates for ROADMAP 7), and whether soft-float in the roll loop matters for its known geometry problem.
- **Three tracked first-party sibling firmware trees never mentioned by any agent** — git ls-files shows tracked source trees at repo root outside fnirsi_1013d_scope/: fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/ (55 files), fnirsi_1013d_startup_from_sd_card/ (38), fnirsi_1013d_sd_card_bootloader/ (36) — real buildable loader/startup code (each with Makefile, start.s, bl_fpga_control.c, dram_control.c, its own mksunxi copy). The review plan's framing ('all first-party/active code is in fnirsi_1013d_scope/') scoped them out and no agent even listed them. Unassessed: whether they are stale relative to the committed bootloader binaries actually flashed (bootloader_base.bin / bootloader_1014d_base.bin, whose provenance BOOT_NOTES ties to the untracked vendor trees instead), and whether any doc should label them live vs provenance-only. CLAUDE.md's module map does not mention them at all.
  - Follow-up: 30-minute pass: identify what each tree builds, whether output matches either committed bootloader (BOOT_NOTES cross-check), and add a one-line status for each to CLAUDE.md (e.g. 'upstream 1013D loader sources, provenance-only, superseded by bootloader_1014d_base.bin').
- **FatFs/SD/display_lib internals covered only by 'diff -q vs Atlan4'; malloc.c not linked vs CLAUDE.md's description** — ff.c (8756 lines), ffunicode.c, diskio.c (beyond get_fattime), sd_card_interface.c (1274 lines, known unbounded register waits, un-itemized), and display_lib.c's clipping internals (1914 lines; only display_character audited — ironically the one REFUTED-high finding turns on exactly the unaudited clipping behaviour) were verified byte-identical to Atlan4 but never read. I verified one concrete consequence chain: malloc.c is absent from the link (Makefile-Debug.mk object list), its only would-be consumer is FatFs ff_memalloc, and ffconf.h compiles those paths out (FF_USE_LFN=1 static, FF_USE_MKFS=0) — benign today, but CLAUDE.md's architecture line 'no libc (... tiny malloc.c)' implies a live allocator that does not exist in the image, and malloc.c joins font_6/7/8.c, icons_extract.c, strcpy.s as unlinked tree weight (ROADMAP 7 class).
  - Follow-up: Record the 'diffed-not-audited' status of the SD/FatFs/display_lib layer as an accepted-risk note in PORT_AUDIT (so future sessions don't assume it was reviewed); correct CLAUDE.md's malloc.c mention to 'present but not linked'; fold malloc.c into the ROADMAP 7 dead-weight list. Deep audit only if SD/file-path bugs surface.
- **Declared-but-never-closed doc claim classes (binary-only and shared-core §1 claims)** — Several agents correctly declared these skips, but no dimension owns them, so they will stay unverified forever unless assigned: (a) BOOT_NOTES' v0.8 loader dead-code claim (call-site-free FPGA-version wait) rests on one past disassembly, never re-derived; (b) bootloader_1014d_base.bin's 'hash-verified local rebuild' provenance never re-checked (both from doc-fpga-boot's and doc-claude-repo's skip lists); (c) PORT_AUDIT §1's 'verified good' byte-diff list was re-established by code-seams for the imported 1014D modules only — the shared-core files in §1 were sampled (code-gotchas diffed six) but not systematically re-verified; (d) tools/acqprobe_analyze.py got a smoke-run and table check but no code review, and bench-conclusion numbers in FPGA_NOTES rest on it. These are cheap, mechanical closures compared to the review effort already spent.
  - Follow-up: Batch them into one mechanical session: objdump the committed v0.8/1014D loader binaries to re-confirm the dead-code and provenance claims (scripted, no hardware), run a scripted diff of every PORT_AUDIT §1 shared-core file against its named reference, and give acqprobe_analyze.py a one-pass code review (its outputs are now load-bearing for FPGA_NOTES' capture-geometry conclusions).

### code-fpga — FPGA command/path/push audit

Checked: (1) Full diff of fnirsi_1013d_scope/fpga_control.c and fpga_control.h against Atlan4-1.00o5 (205 diff lines, all read) — every changed byte accounted for: 0x29/0x01+0x28/0x00 trace to pecostm32's 1014D fpga_do_conversion (reference read verbatim) and pecostm32-RE bus captures; fpga_dump_ring's 0x1F/0x20-0x23 mirror fpga_read_sample_data and the calibration ADC commands; bounded ready-waits (F26), averagesum (F28), sampling_clock_scale rescale (F33, math verified consistent with the nominal-rate frequency division), comp-gate changes (F23 + the stale >=29 gate reported); no FPGA calls removed relative to Atlan4 (scope_functions.c diff contains zero '-fpga_' lines). Per-channel command bytes (0x02/0x03, 0x32-0x37, 0x20-0x23, 0x24/0x26) verified against pecostm32-RE 'FPGA explained.txt'; command-set comparison vs pecostm32 1014D done (his 0x18 fpga_had_trigger is unused here by design — Atlan4 does software trigger detection; our extra 0x0B/0x0C are fw>1, 0x50-0x52 reported). (2) fw_FPGA 1/2/3 asymmetries walked in fpga_do_conversion, fpga_set_time_base, read-pointer translation (3345/750, 7441, 5393 — unchanged from Atlan4) and interleave_runtime_triggerpoint (consistent). (3) long_mode transitions traced end to end: entry/exit via sm_set_time_base, AUTO (clears long_mode, 0x0F/0x28 self-heal via per-conversion writes), wave-view close (ui_close_view_screen verbatim pecostm32, pushes verified), factory reset (reboots), scope_preset_values (no FPGA writes); roll-mode read loop in test.c read; the missing per-cycle 0x0D/0x28-0x01 reported. (4) Missing-push enumeration: every writer of trigger mode/edge/channel/level/50%/origin, timebase, samplerate, v/div, position, enable, coupling, probe, dcoffset, interleave trim and sampling clock in sm_1014d.c, plus baseline/channel calibration, clock search (saves/restores verified line by line incl. 0x0F re-enable and backlight), auto-setup old+new, input calibration and acqprobe in scope_functions.c — each confirmed to push, be display-side, or self-heal at the next arm; the two genuine gaps (v/div->trigger level, enable->trigger swap) reported; channel-enable-push itself already documented as a decision (PORT_AUDIT F31 note/ROADMAP 24), not re-reported. (5) Init order verified in main(): clock_synthesizer_setup (line 84) before fpga_init (88); config load (142) before all FPGA setting pushes (153-181); long/short branch at boot correct (169-170). Also chased and CLEARED a div-by-zero suspicion: dc_shift_size/dc_shift_center used as divisors in fpga_set_trigger_level are loaded/defaulted at boot via scope_load_input_calibration_data (scope_functions.c:7613-7638, called from the config-restore path at 8253) — no finding; verified libgcc __divsi3 div-by-zero behavior by disassembling the shipped ELF while investigating. Skipped/limits: 1013D-only touch paths (menu.c, statemachine.c, generator.c, test.c UI internals) audited only where they intersect the shared FPGA layer or served as references; USB/storage and display pipelines not audited; no hardware verification possible (build-only repo, no automated tests); FPGA_NOTES' stale '0x14 mask 0x0FFF+2' table entry not reported separately (the 0x1FFF divergence is already recorded in PORT_AUDIT F27).

### code-guards — variant-guard static audit

Scope: every .c/.h/.s in fnirsi_1013d_scope/ (dist/, build/, and the vendored reference trees excluded; references used only for comparison). Enumerated all occurrences with grep -n: 97 PORT_1014D and 5 PORT_A_KEYDEBUG sites, across fpga_control.c, scope_functions.c(.h), fnirsi_1013d_scope.c, variables.c/.h, menu.c, menu_1014d.c, sm_1014d.c, fonts_1014d.c, font_0/2/3/4.c, touchpanel.c, DS3231.c, power_and_battery.c, port_config.h. No .s file contains either macro. Confirmed both variants compile the identical object list (nbproject/Makefile-Debug.mk:38-82); noted in passing that font_6/7/8.c, icons_extract.c, malloc.c, strcpy.s exist in the tree but are absent from the object list (not variant-related). Audit per task class: (1) guard-before-includes — checked the include order of every guard-bearing file individually; all whole-file guards (font_0/2/3/4.c:8 after port_config.h:6; fonts_1014d.c:11 after port_config.h:9; menu_1014d.c:15 and sm_1014d.c:15 after variables.h) and all per-body guards (DS3231, touchpanel, power_and_battery, menu, fpga_control, scope_functions, variables, fnirsi_1013d_scope) come after port_config.h/variables.h — zero instances of the F3/F4 bug class. (2) #ifdef/defined() misuse — grep for ifdef/ifndef/defined over PORT macros found only the PORT_CONFIG_H include guard; every variant test is a value-form #if — clean. (3) cross-variant symbols — scripted a preprocessor-nesting map over all guard-bearing files (all #if/#else/#endif pairs balanced, none unclosed) and a cross-reference scan of all 230 function/global definitions in menu_1014d.c+sm_1014d.c against every other file with guard-state tracking: no unguarded references anywhere (only harmless header declarations in statemachine.h/variables.h); the 1014D-only scope functions (measure_high_rate_artifact, auto_detect_max_clean_sampling_clock, scope_do_acquisition_probe at scope_functions.c:1492/1561/1933) sit inside the 1478-2119 guard with every caller guarded; font_0/2/3/4 dual definitions (font_*.c 1013D vs fonts_1014d.c 1014D) verified complementary; sampling_clock_scale is statically 1.0 (variables.c:40) so the unguarded F33 rescale at fpga_control.c:996 is safe on 1013D; PORT_A_KEYDEBUG=1 links on both variants (toprocesscommand, font_3/5, fpga_get_version all both-variant); verified single live get_fattime (diskio.c copy commented out) and single live ini_SysParam (PC_interface.c:136 copy inside a block comment). Also scanned for 1014D-visible references into the \"1013D-only\" files (finding 2). (4) asymmetric fixes — read every #if/#else PORT region in scope_functions.c, fnirsi_1013d_scope.c, menu.c, fpga_control.c, variables.c, touchpanel.c, DS3231.c and compared suspicious ones against Atlan4-1.00o5 and both pecostm32 references: all asymmetries except finding 1 are documented deliberate 1014D differences (trace-buffer-clear skip, chrome routing, copy-rect widths, restore-screen skip, boot-sector-write skip, composite-menu gate); coupling push (F31) verified symmetric (Atlan4's 1013D already had it at statemachine.c:1927/1947; sm_1014d.c:2492 added parity); trigger_position_min/max fix is unguarded shared code benefiting both variants. (5) inverted guard senses — none; menu.c:102-108 (generator-settings skip) is unreachable on 1014D because scope_setup_main_screen routes-and-returns at menu.c:42-46 (redundant but harmless). Skipped/limits: did not run make (per instructions — findings on 1013D-variant linkability are static analysis); did not audit non-variant correctness of shared code or the USB/CDC protocol behavior on 1014D (usb_CDC_receive_callback is live on both variants — same code, not a guard issue); regex-based symbol scans could in principle miss macro-generated identifier references, but this codebase does not use that style.

### code-gotchas — signedness/string/buffer/blocking-loop hazard hunt

Scope: first-party fnirsi_1013d_scope/ only; vendored trees used strictly as references. All cited lines read in this checkout.

(a) Signedness: Confirmed CFLAGS (nbproject/Makefile-Debug.mk:86) carry neither -fsigned-char nor -funsigned-char, so plain char is UNSIGNED (arm-none-eabi default); -fno-builtin-strcpy and -Wno-char-subscripts present. Audited types.h, all key/rotary handling (uart.c returns uint8; toprocesscommand is uint8; UIC_* codes are 0..~50 so no sign issues in dispatch), every speedvalue/setvalue consumer in sm_1014d.c (int8 deltas against uint16 fields -> findings 1-3; index/menuitem/newitem/probeindex/slider/adc-comp paths verified safe as int16/int32/clamped int8), int8 compensation fields (int16, safe), display_character int8 parameter (finding 9), compute_all_math_ops int8 wrap (finding 6). Grepped for char-vs-negative comparisons across all .c: only ff.c's internal prec<0 (int, fine).

(b) Asm routines: read all six in full. strlen, memset, memcmp are ISO-conformant (traced). memcpy and memmove are byte-identical direction-aware copies (overlap-safe both ways); quirk: src==dst returns NULL instead of dest (the 'len=0' comment is wrong) — verified NO first-party caller uses a memcpy/memmove/memset return value, so not reported as a finding. strcpy.s (returns dest+strlen+1, past the NUL) is NOT in the build object list (Makefile-Debug.mk links only memcmp/memcpy/memmove/memset/strlen); the live strcpy is the C one at scope_functions.c:5106 returning the terminator position, which the `buffer = strcpy(buffer, ...)` concat idiom in scope_functions.c/menu_1014d.c requires — all `= strcpy` call sites checked, all use the concat idiom. This whole area is already documented as PORT_AUDIT.md F2 (retracted, -fno-builtin-strcpy insurance), so not re-reported; note strcpy.s remains in the tree as a latent landmine if ever added to the build.

(c) Buffer geometry: verified the F17 705-wide 1014D copy rect vs 728 Atlan4 path (scope_functions.c:3785/3794 — correct, offscreen 728/730-wide fills are into the 800-wide buffer, safe); acqprobe ring dump correctly sized (5x4608 static vs count); fpga_read_sample_data/fpga_read_adc_data stride-2 writes bounded by totalsamples (default 3000, 1013D memory-deep max 15000, both < 16380) except the USB debug path (finding 4); the 16382-vs-16380 floor-division mismatch (finding 5); timebase tables: sm_set_time_base uses sizeof-derived bounds and clamps 0..34, time_per_div_sample_rate[35] values max 28 fit sample_rate[29]/freq_calc_data[29]/sample_rate_settings (fpga_control.c:388 also bounds-checks); screen_time_calc_data correctly extended to [35] here (pecostm32's was [24] — seam handled); volt_calc_data/volt_div_texts_short/vertical_scaling_factors [7][7] vs magnification {1,2,5} and voltperdiv 0..6 — in range; probe index wraps 0..2; measurement_names[12] == measurement_functions[12]; wave-file restore (menu_1014d.c:3309-3350) is gated by ID+version (0x01000002, distinct from pecostm32's 0x00000001) and a checksum before ui_restore_setup_from_file, so cross-firmware/corrupt index injection is blocked; config-sector restore is checksum+version gated (scope_functions.c:8088) but does not range-check totalsamples (folded into finding 4). X-Y negative-index reads = finding 7. The 16-bit truncated triggerhorizontalposition config save (scope_functions.c:7923 '&0xFFFF //int32!!!') is pre-existing Atlan4 (identical lines) and self-heals via the range clamp on next recalculation — not reported.

(d) Blocking loops: uart.c (finding 8, provenance: byte-identical to pecostm32, absent from Atlan4). clock_synthesizer.c I2C is write-only bit-bang with fixed delays, no ACK sampling, cannot hang. fpga_control.c: both conversion-ready waits are already timeout-bounded (2M iterations, lines ~653-671); fpga_check_ready (fpga_control.c:214-228) spins forever on version==0x1432 only (would hang with the 0x1532 Atlan4/rebuilt FPGA) but is DEAD CODE — no caller in this tree; same body in both references; worth deleting or fixing before the FPGA-swap project revives it. sd_card_interface.c, spi_control.c, timer.c, ccu_control.c, display_control.c, usb_interface.c verified byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5 (diff -q), so their unbounded register waits (e.g. SD0_CMDR start, SPI FIFO) are pre-existing Atlan4 behavior, working on hardware — not itemized. DS3231.c/touchpanel.c differ from Atlan4 only by the F3/F4 #if !PORT_1014D guard insertions (diff shows guards only); their waits are pre-existing and 1013D-only.

Skipped / not audited in depth: ff.c/ffunicode.c (FatFs internals), malloc.c, mass_storage_class.c/cdc_class.c protocol internals, icons/fonts data content, generator.c, PC_interface.c and menu.c/statemachine.c beyond the specific items above (1013D-only, never hardware-tested per docs), interrupt handling, and display_lib draw-clipping internals beyond the character path. No caps hit; no truncation.

# Follow-up audits (2026-08-21, same day)

The coverage critic's five gaps, each closed by a dedicated agent (sequential run,
workflow `wf_7da5adae-958`), all findings adversarially verified: 28/28 CONFIRMED.
Fix disposition per finding is recorded in PORT_AUDIT.md §5e addendum; the roll-mode
patch below was applied the same day (hw-verify pending).

## test.c deep pass + roll-mode fix design

### F21 mechanism 1: in roll mode the 1014D main loop runs TWO renderers that fight, and roll poisons the standard renderer's geometry state

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:442`
- **Severity:** high — **CONFIRMED**

The PORT_1014D main loop routes long_mode to scope_get_long_timebase_data() (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:391-393), which draws the roll trace incrementally straight into maindisplaybuffer (test.c:997-1021), and then UNCONDITIONALLY also calls scope_display_trace_data() every pass (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:440-445, gated only on enabletracedisplay, which defaults 1 — variables.c:13, sm_1014d.c:42). scope_display_trace_data has no long_mode early-return (scope_functions.c:3450-3453; long_mode only rescales disp_xpos_per_sample at 3490): it repaints the whole trace window into displaybuffertmp and blits (2,48,705,432) to screen (scope_functions.c:3796-3800), overwriting the roll renderer's pixels in x=2..706 every pass. Worse, the roll loop feeds it garbage: test.c:1035-1038 writes SAMPLE-INDEX values 375/1125/1875/2625 into scopesettings.triggerhorizontalposition, a SCREEN-coordinate field whose valid range is ~6..704 (variables.h:761, clamps at sm_1014d.c:1323-1332), and test.c:1031 forces disp_have_trigger=1 so disp_trigger_index keeps its stale value from the last short-mode capture (scope_functions.c:3472-3479). With triggerposition=1125..2625, disp_xstart=triggerposition-xrange lands beyond disp_xend after the 704 clamp (scope_functions.c:3518-3536), so the standard renderer draws little or nothing inside the grid — the roll trace is visible mainly as the junk the roll renderer leaves OUTSIDE the standard blit (x=705..726), which is exactly the bench-observed F21 symptom ('trace crawling past the right edge'). Atlan4's 1013D flow never has this fight: its main loop calls only touch_handler() (line 446-449), so scope_display_trace_data runs on touch events only. This per-pass call is a port addition; test.c itself is byte-identical to Atlan4's (diff verified).

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:440-445: '#if PORT_1014D sm_handle_user_input(); if(enabletracedisplay || ui_menu_composite_active()) { scope_display_trace_data(); }'. test.c:1031: 'disp_have_trigger=1;  //musi byt 1 aby nevlozilo sample/2 tj 1500'. test.c:1035-1038: 'if (scopesettings.count < 750) { disp_xstart = 0; disp_xend = 750; scopesettings.triggerhorizontalposition = 375;} ... else if (scopesettings.count < 3000) { ... scopesettings.triggerhorizontalposition = 2625;}'. scope_functions.c:3468 'int32 triggerposition = scopesettings.triggerhorizontalposition;' and 3533-3535 'if(disp_xend > 704) { disp_xend = 704; }'.

**Suggested action:** Make one renderer own roll mode: gate the main-loop scope_display_trace_data call on !scopesettings.long_mode (keeping the ui_menu_composite_active() leg if overlay menus must stay live during roll), or alternatively drop the roll renderer's screen drawing and teach the standard path to render the roll buffer. Also stop writing sample indices into triggerhorizontalposition from test.c (those values only make sense for the thumbnail/save path and should go through a separate variable). Coordinate with the geometry finding below — both are needed to close F21.

**Verifier:** Reproduced every element: fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:391-393 routes long_mode to scope_get_long_timebase_data(); lines 440-445 (#if PORT_1014D) call scope_display_trace_data() every pass gated only on enabletracedisplay (=1 at variables.c:13, re-set in sm_init at sm_1014d.c:42) || ui_menu_composite_active(). scope_display_trace_data has no long_mode early-return (scope_functions.c:3449-3453; long_mode only rescales disp_xpos_per_sample at 3490) and blits (2,48,705,432) at 3797, overwriting x=2..706 each pass. test.c:1031 forces disp_have_trigger=1 ('musi byt 1...'), and 1035-1038 write sample indices 375/1125/1875/2625 into triggerhorizontalposition, an int32 screen-coordinate field (variables.h:761) clamped to trigger_position_min/max (screen coords from disp_xrange) at sm_1014d.c:1323-1332. Standard renderer clamps 6..704 (scope_functions.c:3527-3535). test.c is byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5's (diff exit 0); Atlan4's own main loop calls only touch_handler() (its line 363) with scope_display_trace_data commented out at 216 — the per-pass call is a port addition. The 'draws little or nothing' sub-narrative varies with xrange (some segments draw full-width garbage instead of nothing), but the dual-renderer fight mechanism is exactly as stated.

### F21 mechanism 2: roll renderer uses Atlan4 1013D geometry — draws to x=726 and fills/blits (0,48,730,432) over the P14 sidebar and border

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/test.c:992`
- **Severity:** high — **CONFIRMED**

scope_get_long_timebase_data sweeps xpos 4..726 (reset at >726, test.c:960/985-986, line draw at 1006/1019 up to x=726), and on each sweep wrap black-fills and blits (0,48,730,432) (test.c:967/992). The 1014D P14 layout ends the grid at x=704 and the F17-corrected per-frame trace blit is (2,48,705,432) covering x=2..706 (variables.h:143-157: border x=5 w=701, TRACE_HORIZONTAL_END=705; scope_functions.c:3796-3800 comment: 'the trace window ends at x=706 (grid at 704) and the measurements sidebar starts right after it'). So the roll renderer (a) draws trace pixels 22 px past the grid end into the sidebar seam (x=705..726) where the standard renderer's 706-wide blit never erases them — the persistent 'crawl past the right edge'; and (b) once per sweep blits displaybuffertmp x=0..729 rows 48..479 over the sidebar columns x=707..729 and the left border x=0..1. displaybuffertmp's content there is the standard renderer's fill (2,48,728,432) i.e. black (scope_functions.c:3583-3588), so each sweep wrap paints a black band over the F17-protected sidebar chrome — the 'trace-area shadow at the wrong location'. test.c is byte-identical to vendored Atlan4-1.00o5/fnirsi_1013d_scope/test.c (diff verified), where these numbers are correct for the 1013D layout (blit clamp 727, scope_functions.c:3544-3547 #else branch); they were never ported to P14 geometry. The dead sibling scope_get_long_timebase_data1 has the same rects with height 403 (test.c:713/810).

**Evidence:** test.c:960-992: 'if ( scopesettings.xpos >726) { display_set_screen_buffer(displaybuffertmp); display_set_fg_color(BLACK_COLOR); display_fill_rect(0, 48, 730, 432); ... scopesettings.xpos = 4; scopesettings.lastx = 3; ... display_copy_rect_to_screen(0, 48, 730, 432); }' vs the P14 standard blit scope_functions.c:3800 'display_copy_rect_to_screen(2, 48, 705, 432);' and P14 clamps scope_functions.c:3527-3535 (6..704).

**Suggested action:** Under #if PORT_1014D use the P14 numbers in scope_get_long_timebase_data: sweep x within 6..704 (reset xpos=7/lastx=6, wrap test at >704), fill/copy (2,48,705,432); keep the 1013D numbers in the #else per the no-1013D-changes policy. Do this together with the single-renderer decision above.

**Verifier:** test.c:960-994 verified verbatim: xpos>726 reset, display_fill_rect(0,48,730,432) into displaybuffertmp, xpos=4/lastx=3, display_copy_rect_to_screen(0,48,730,432); line draws at 1006/1019 reach x=726. P14 geometry verified: variables.h:146-157 (border x=5 w=701, TRACE_HORIZONTAL_END=705), F17 blit (2,48,705,432) + comment at scope_functions.c:3792-3797. Standard renderer's fill (2,48,728,432) at 3588 leaves displaybuffertmp black in sidebar columns, so each sweep-wrap blit paints a black band over x=707..729 (and 0..1) rows 48-479. Dead sibling scope_get_long_timebase_data1 rects with height 403 confirmed at test.c:713/810. test.c byte-identical to Atlan4 (diff exit 0), where 1013D clamps are 3..727 (scope_functions.c:3538-3547 #else).

**Correction:** Trivial: the standard blit (2,48,705,432) covers x=2..706, so the never-erased roll pixels are x=707..726 (20 px), not x=705..726.

### Roll normal/single trigger permanently dead after uint16 triggerverticalposition wrap — the one consumer where the wrap is NOT annihilated

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/test.c:1062`
- **Severity:** high — **CONFIRMED**

scopesettings.triggerverticalposition is uint16 in this tree (variables.h:762) but int16 in pecostm32's official 1014D (reference FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/variables.h:459), whose handlers this tree imported. Two live writers can store a negative value that wraps to ~65xxx: (1) sm_1014d.c:1639 'scopesettings.triggerverticalposition += delta' — deliberately unclamped ('without worry about limit'), goes negative when the trace is moved down while the trigger pointer sits low; (2) sm_set_trigger_level's own lower clamp sm_1014d.c:1371 'triggerverticalposition = ((-127 * signal_adjusters[voltperdiv]) >> VOLTAGE_SHIFTER) + traceposition' assigns trp-218 (signal_adjusters 3.6M-3.8M, variables.c:991) or trp-460 at voltperdiv 6 (7.6M) — negative whenever traceposition<218 (autoset/50% setup set it to 200, scope_functions.c:2481/2494; always negative at voltperdiv 6 since traceposition<=399). REVIEW-2026-08-21 (line 1113) proved this wrap harmless for the FPGA paths because both multiply by 2^21, annihilating the mod-2^16 error mod 2^32. But scope_check_long_trigger does NOT multiply: test.c:1062 'level = scopesettings.triggerverticalposition+25' promotes the wrapped uint16 to ~65540 and compares it against sample1/sample2 <= 448 (test.c:1108-1110) — neither edge can ever fire. In roll normal/single mode (triggermode!=0 forces triggerlong=0, scope_functions.c:280; data acquisition is gated on triggerlong, test.c:835) the scope then waits forever with no trace, until the user happens to dial the level back into range (the level<0/level>255 re-clamp at sm_1014d.c:1368-1377 restores a sane value on the next detent).
