Failure recipe: roll timebase, normal mode, spin trigger-level rotary to the bottom stop with the trace at/below center — acquisition freezes on WAITING.

**Evidence:** variables.h:762 'uint16 triggerverticalposition;' vs reference int16; sm_1014d.c:1637-1639 '//Move the trigger pointer without worry about limit ... scopesettings.triggerverticalposition += delta;'; test.c:1062 'level = scopesettings.triggerverticalposition+25;   //+25 compensation'; test.c:1108-1110 compare sample1/sample2 (max (255*3689650)>>21 = 448) against level.

**Suggested action:** Either change the field to int16 to match the pecostm32 reference semantics (audit the config save/restore raw-buffer copies — bit patterns identical, so save format is unaffected), or compute the roll level in a signed local: 'int32 level = (int32)(int16)scopesettings.triggerverticalposition + 25;' plus treat sample comparisons as signed. The f0561ee signed-local pattern applies; this is the same class it fixed for traceposition and the cursors.

**Verifier:** All links re-derived: variables.h:762 uint16 triggerverticalposition vs reference FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/variables.h:459 int16. Writers: sm_1014d.c:1639 unclamped '+= delta' ('without worry about limit'); sm_1014d.c:1371 lower clamp (-127*signal_adjusters[vpd])>>21 + traceposition = trp-219 (3.6M) / trp-461 (7.6M at vpd 6, always negative since traceposition<=399); autoset sets traceposition=200 (scope_functions.c:2481/2494), so trp<219 wraps to ~65317. test.c:1062 'level = triggerverticalposition+25' promotes to ~65342; max sample = (255*3689650)>>21 = 448 (multiply=3689650 variables.c:994, VOLTAGE_SHIFTER=21 variables.h:297), so neither edge test at test.c:1108-1110 can fire. Gate verified: scope_functions.c:280 'if(triggermode) triggerlong=0 else 1' and test.c:831/835 gate acquisition on triggerlong. Freeze-until-redial scenario is real (uint16 wrap class matches f0561ee's other fixes, which did not touch this one).

**Correction:** Arithmetic nit: -127*3600000>>21 rounds to -219 (finding says trp-218) and 7.6M gives -461 (finding says -460); immaterial to the bug.

### Roll trigger's '+25 compensation'/global multiply is wrong on the calibrated 1014D: ignores input_calibration, v/div, trace position and DC offset

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/test.c:1080`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

scope_check_long_trigger converts the raw ADC average to 'screen space' as sample = avg * multiply >> 21 with the global const multiply=3689650 (gain 1.759 px/count, variables.c:994), the per-channel calibrated forms being commented out (test.c:1077-1078/1094-1095), and compares against level = triggerverticalposition + 25. The correct screen mapping (scope_get_y_sample, scope_functions.c:3865-3896) is (avg-128)*input_calibration[samplevoltperdiv]>>21 + traceposition + dcoffset-term. The two agree ONLY at traceposition≈200 (128*1.759=225=200+25 — that is what the +25 magic encodes) AND input_calibration≈3689650. On the current calibrated 1014D input_calibration is tuned per channel per v/div in 3000000..4000000 (scope_functions.c:2326-2345), i.e. gain 1.43-1.91 — up to ~23% off the hard-coded 1.76; and the formula tracks the trace-position pointer with slope 0 instead of 1: move the trace to position 100 and put the trigger pointer on the visible trace, and the actual ADC threshold is (100+25)/1.759≈71 instead of 128 — the roll trigger fires ~2 divisions away from where the pointer says. DC offset (dcoffset/dc_shift_size, used by both scope_get_y_sample and fpga_set_trigger_level fpga_control.c:493-512) is ignored entirely. Answer to the audit question: yes, the magic is wrong on the calibrated unit except at the default center geometry. Note this is verbatim Atlan4 code (test.c byte-identical to the vendor tree), so the 1013D keeps it as-is per policy; a fix must be 1014D-guarded or clearly shared-safe.

**Evidence:** test.c:1062 'level = scopesettings.triggerverticalposition+25;   //+25 compensation'; test.c:1080-1081 'sample1 = ((sample1AVG) * multiply) >> VOLTAGE_SHIFTER;' with the calibrated forms commented at 1077-1078; variables.c:994 'const int32 multiply = 3689650;'; VOLTAGE_SHIFTER=21 (variables.h:297); calibration search bounds 3000000..4000000 at scope_functions.c:2326-2344.

**Suggested action:** Compare in ADC space instead of inventing a second screen mapping: fpga_set_trigger_level already computes the exact ADC-space level from triggerverticalposition, traceposition, dcoffset and signal_adjusters and stores it (fpga_control.c:512-516, scopesettings.triggerlevel) — have scope_check_long_trigger compare the raw averages (sample1AVG/sample2AVG) against that. Caveats: triggerlevel is uint16 and is stored BEFORE the 0..255 clamp (fpga_control.c:516-526), so either store the clamped value or clamp locally; and push fpga_set_trigger_level()/recompute on roll entry so the value is fresh.

**Verifier:** Verified: roll trigger uses sample = avg*3689650>>21 with calibrated forms commented out (test.c:1077-1081/1094-1098) and level = trp+25 (1062). Correct screen mapping confirmed at scope_get_y_sample (scope_functions.c:3869-3896): (raw-128)*input_calibration[samplevoltperdiv]>>21 + traceposition + (dcoffset*100)/dc_shift_size (doubled at vpd 6). Calibration search bounds 3000000..4000000 confirmed at scope_functions.c:2326-2344. fpga_set_trigger_level uses the dcoffset term (fpga_control.c:493-512). The math checks: 128*3689650>>21 = 225 = 200+25, so the +25 encodes traceposition 200 with the default gain; the roll formula tracks traceposition with slope 0 and ignores calibration and DC offset. test.c byte-identical to Atlan4, so 1013D-as-is policy note is apt.

### Roll wait loop blocks all key input with no abort on the 1014D — up to 1 s per sample at 50 s/div

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/test.c:895`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

scope_get_long_timebase_data paces samples with 'while((havetouch == 0)) { curticks = timer0_get_ticks(); if(curticks >= timeout) goto skip_delay; tp_i2c_read_status(); }' (test.c:895-912) with timeout 10..1000 ms per sample by timeperdiv (1 ms tick — timer.c:15/38). On the 1013D a touch sets havetouch and aborts the wait; on the 1014D tp_i2c_read_status() is an empty stub (touchpanel.c:185-187, body '#if !PORT_1014D'-guarded; havetouch only ever set at touchpanel.c:234 inside the guard), so the loop can ONLY exit by timeout, and sm_handle_user_input (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:441) — the only key poll — runs once per sample. At 50 s/div that is one key/rotary event processed per second; a rotary burst during roll is served at 1 detent/s while the blocking uart1 poll queue drains. No crash, but the scope feels hung at the slowest timebases and there is no in-loop UART equivalent of the touch abort. (Roll currently reachable for timeperdiv 0..6, i.e. 50s..500ms — sm_1014d.c:1462-1466.)

**Evidence:** test.c:895-912 wait loop; touchpanel.c:185-188 'void tp_i2c_read_status(void) { #if !PORT_1014D ...'; timer.c:15 '*TMR0_INTV_VALUE_REG = 24000;' + :36-38 1 ms tick; timeout table test.c:839-887 (case 0 -> 1000).

**Suggested action:** Inside the 1014D wait loop, poll the key controller (or at least check for a pending byte non-blockingly) and break out on input, mirroring the 1013D touch abort; alternatively shorten the wait granularity and run sm_handle_user_input between sub-waits. Needs care with uart.c's blocking write-0xFF/wait protocol (CLAUDE.md known constraint).

**Verifier:** test.c:895-912 wait loop exits only on timeout on 1014D: tp_i2c_read_status body is '#if !PORT_1014D'-guarded (touchpanel.c:185-187) and both havetouch writes (=1, =0) sit inside that guard (verified #endif placement). timer.c: 1 ms tick (TMR0_INTV=24000 of OSC24M, timer0ticks++ per IRQ). Timeout table test.c:839-887: case 0 -> 1000 ms; roll reachable only for timeperdiv 0..6 (sm_1014d.c dial remap: index 7 -> 11 sweep, 10 -> 6 roll), so effective per-sample waits are 10..1000 ms. sm_handle_user_input is called once per main-loop pass (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:441), i.e. once per roll sample, so 1 key event/s at 50 s/div is correct.

### test.c dead code inventory: scrolling roll variant, DSP helpers with 16 KB stack array, CSV exporter — all uncalled but linked into both variants

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/test.c:583`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

Verified callers across the tree (grep, excluding vendor trees): scope_get_long_timebase_data1 (test.c:583, the alternate scrolling roll renderer with its own divergent 403-height rects and CH1-only support) has NO callers; scope_preset_long_mode exists only as a commented stub (test.c:565-579, prototype commented at test.h:43, sole reference commented at fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:260); fpga_average_trace_data_long (fpga_control.c:1480) has zero callers; moving_average, exponential_moving_average, fir_filter, filter_bit_changes, add_one_bit, average_filter, convert_waveforms_to_csv_excel, scope_load_REFx_trace_data and display_text_message are all uncalled (only commented references, e.g. menu.c:2319). The link has no -ffunction-sections/--gc-sections (nbproject/Makefile-Debug.mk:107), so all of it plus the double soft-float libgcc support pulled in by fir_filter/exponential_moving_average ships in the flash image of BOTH variants — pure code-size cost (ROADMAP 7 class). Latent hazards if ever wired up: fir_filter allocates uint8_t output[MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE] = 16382 bytes on the stack (test.c:485, variables.h:528) and moving_average a size-byte VLA (test.c:440) — either would smash the bare-metal stack for full-buffer sizes. Soft-float answer for the audit: the LIVE roll loop (scope_get_long_timebase_data + scope_check_long_trigger) is integer-only; the only per-frame double math in roll is scope_display_trace_data's disp_* computation, which short mode already pays every frame — soft-float is not a roll-specific cost today. Live test helpers: scope_test_trigerposition_value is reachable under dev_mode (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:426, dev_mode default 0 at variables.c:161) and paints a 100x220 rect at x=600 over the P14 sidebar; scope_test_TP is live only in 1013D statemachine.c paths.

**Evidence:** grep across *.c/*.h: scope_get_long_timebase_data1 / fpga_average_trace_data_long / the DSP-helper names appear only at their definitions, in test.h prototypes, and in commented call sites; Makefile link line 'arm-none-eabi-gcc -o ... ${OBJECTFILES} ... -nostdlib -lgcc' with no --gc-sections; test.c:485 'uint8_t output[MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE];' with MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE 16382 (variables.h:528).

**Suggested action:** Delete (or #if 0 with a provenance comment) scope_get_long_timebase_data1, the DSP helpers and the CSV exporter, and drop fpga_average_trace_data_long, per ROADMAP 7; keep scope_load_REFx_trace_data only if the REF feature plan needs it. If any DSP helper is ever revived, replace the stack buffers with a static/heap buffer first. test.c is byte-identical to Atlan4's, so any cleanup is a deliberate divergence — document it in PORT_AUDIT.

**Verifier:** Grep across first-party *.c/*.h reproduces the inventory: scope_get_long_timebase_data1, fpga_average_trace_data_long, moving_average, exponential_moving_average, fir_filter, filter_bit_changes, add_one_bit, average_filter, convert_waveforms_to_csv_excel, scope_load_REFx_trace_data, display_text_message appear only at definitions/prototypes/commented call sites; scope_preset_long_mode stub commented everywhere. Link line has no --gc-sections/-ffunction-sections (Makefile-Debug.mk:107 'arm-none-eabi-gcc -o ... -nostdlib -lgcc'). fir_filter declares uint8_t output[MAX_SAMPLE_BUFFER_SIZE=16382] on stack (test.c:485, variables.h:528); moving_average has VLA uint8_t temp[size] (test.c:440). dev_mode=0 default (variables.c:161); scope_test_trigerposition_value live under dev_mode (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:426) fills 100x220 at x=600,y=100 over the P14 sidebar (test.c:1298-1305); scope_test_TP live only at statemachine.c:1853/2982/3486 (1013D).

### Design proposal (test-c)

```
ROLL-MODE 0x28/0x01 FIX (F25's other half) — design only, not applied.

== Stock sequence derived from the captures (stock FPGA 0x1432; capture is from the stock 1013D firmware — same FPGA family/version as the 1014D's, flagged below) ==
Per read cycle, repeating (pecostm32-RE/'FPGA explained'/100mS_50S-div_signal_data_read_sequence.txt:63-82, four identical cycles):
  0x0D  write 4  data 0x00 0x00 0x07 0xD0     (rate; 0x7D0 for ALL nine roll timebases — FPGA explained.txt:205-214)
  0x28  write 1  data 0x01                     (mode select: roll — FPGA explained.txt:76)
  0x24  read 10                                (CH1 data)
  0x26  read 10                                (CH2 data)
Corroborated by FPGA explained.txt:202: '0x28 is written 0x01 after or before every read sequence. 0x0D is also written.'
Mode entry / after touch (100mS_50S:60-64 preceded by the special-IC dance; FPGA explained.txt:218-219): 0x0D <- 0x00 0x00 0x00 0x27, then 0x0D <- 0x00 0x00 0x07 0xD0 ('a kind of reset which is only done when time base is switched to 100mS and up'). No 0x0F appears anywhere in the roll capture; 0x0F/0x00 -> 0x28/0x00 -> ... -> 0x0F/0x01 is the SHORT-mode cycle (50mS capture lines 5-12).

== What our tree sends today (verified) ==
Entry (sm_1014d.c:1469-1475 -> fpga_set_long_timebase, fpga_control.c:576-601): only 0x0F/0x01; the 0x28/0x01 is dead-commented at :599-600 ('0X28 not support in FPGA???'). Then sm_1014d.c:1491-1496 -> fpga_set_sample_rate -> 0x0D + 0x00061A7F (sample_rate_settings[22]=399999, ~500 Sa/s — ALL roll indices map to it; variables.c:421-435/794-825) — a rate value that diverges from stock's 0x7D0 (~100 kSa/s). Per cycle (test.c:949-957 -> fpga_average_trace_data, fpga_control.c:1443): only the 0x24/0x26 averaged reads — no 0x0D, no 0x28. Net: the stock FPGA runs roll with the mode-select still at 0x28/0x00 (last written by fpga_do_conversion's F25 restore, fpga_control.c:642-643) and at an invented rate.

== Proposed change ==
(1) fpga_control.c — add a helper immediately AFTER fpga_set_long_timebase (ends at line 601), keeping all silicon-tied bytes in fpga_control.c per repo convention:

//----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
//Roll-mode per-read-cycle arm (F25's other half). The stock FPGA is fed 0x0D (rate) + 0x28/0x01
//before EVERY 0x24/0x26 read cycle, not just at mode entry: pecostm32-RE "FPGA explained".txt:202
//and 100mS_50S-div capture lines 63-82 (0x0D 0x000007D0 -> 0x28 0x01 -> 0x24 x10 -> 0x26 x10,
//repeating). Stock writes 0x000007D0 for ALL roll timebases (table lines 205-214) and paces the
//reads with a timer, exactly like scope_get_long_timebase_data's timeout loop does.
//Stock fw only: PECO FPGAs (fw_FPGA 2/3) have their own long-timebase handling. 1014D only:
//the 1013D variant stays at Atlan4's shipped behavior (no hardware to test — policy 2026-08-21).
void fpga_arm_long_timebase_cycle(void)
{
#if PORT_1014D
  if(fpgasettings.fw_FPGA == 1)
  {
    //ADC clock rate for roll mode: stock always 0x000007D0 here (fpga_write_int is MSB-first,
    //matching the capture byte order 0x00 0x00 0x07 0xD0)
    fpga_write_cmd(0x0D);
    fpga_write_int(0x000007D0);

    //Mode select: 0x01 = roll (counterpart of the 0x28/0x00 fast select in fpga_do_conversion)
    fpga_write_cmd(0x28);
    fpga_write_byte(0x01);
  }
#endif
}

Prototype in fpga_control.h next to fpga_set_long_timebase's declaration.

(2) fpga_set_long_timebase (fpga_control.c:576-601) — replace the dead lines 598-600 with the guarded stock entry sequence, keeping the existing 0x0F/0x01:

  //Disable trigger system???
  fpga_write_cmd(0x0F);
  fpga_write_byte(0x01);

#if PORT_1014D
  if(fpgasettings.fw_FPGA == 1)
  {
    //Stock roll-entry "reset": 0x0D <- 0x27 then the run rate + mode select
    //(pecostm32-RE FPGA explained.txt:218-219; seen at switch-to-roll and after touch).
    //The 0x27 write is the least-understood byte here -- first thing to drop on bench trouble.
    fpga_write_cmd(0x0D);
    fpga_write_int(0x00000027);

    fpga_arm_long_timebase_cycle();
  }
#endif

(3) test.c scope_get_long_timebase_data — one call per acquisition cycle, inserted right before line 949 ('//Check channel 1 is enabled', after the count>2999 redraw block ending at 947), so the 0x0D+0x28 pair brackets each 0x24/0x26 read group exactly like the capture (ONE pair covers both channels — do not call per channel):

    //Stock roll protocol: re-arm rate + mode select before every 0x24/0x26 read cycle
    //(no-op on 1013D / PECO FPGAs)
    fpga_arm_long_timebase_cycle();

(4) test.c scope_check_long_trigger — same single call at the top of the function (before line 1069's channel check): in normal/single mode this function performs the 0x24/0x26 reads INSTEAD of the main loop while waiting for the software trigger, and it is the first reader to run after mode entry, so without the arm its reads would still execute under the fast mode-select. Its two averaged reads are 5 ms apart; one arm covering both mirrors the capture's one-pair-per-read-group granularity (a per-read arm is the fallback if bench shows staleness).

Deliberately NOT changed: sm_1014d.c:1493-1496 keeps calling fpga_set_sample_rate (0x0D=0x00061A7F) at entry — scopesettings.samplerate must keep being set for the renderer/time math, and the per-cycle re-arm overwrites the FPGA-side rate to 0x7D0 before the first read anyway (self-healing ordering: entry-reset -> sample_rate write -> first cycle re-arm). The dead scope_get_long_timebase_data1 gets no call (leave dead / delete per the dead-code finding). 1013D and fw_FPGA>1 paths are untouched by construction of the guard.

== Open hardware questions (flag for the bench pass) ==
1. Does 0x28/0x01 change what 0x24/0x26 return on the stock 1014D FPGA (fresh rolling samples vs whatever the fast-mode select returns today)? A/B: current build vs patched, driven hum source, 500 ms/div, watch for trace actually tracking the input.
2. Rate semantics: stock 0x7D0 = ~100 kSa/s means the 10-byte average spans ~100 us; Atlan4's 399999 (~500 Sa/s) spans ~20 ms — more smoothing. If the stock rate makes roll visibly noisier, keeping Atlan4's value in fpga_arm_long_timebase_cycle (sample_rate_settings[scopesettings.samplerate] instead of 0x7D0) is a legitimate documented divergence — but start byte-exact to the reference per repo doctrine.
3. The 0x0D <- 0x00000027 entry write's meaning is unknown (possibly a park/stop divider); it is in the reference at entry/after-touch, so it is included, but drop it first if roll misbehaves after the patch.
4. 0x0F/0x01 at roll entry is Atlan4's, absent from the stock roll capture; kept because current roll demonstrably acquires data with it. If the patched sequence glitches, try removing it second.
5. Capture provenance: the pecostm32-RE roll captures were taken on a stock 1013D (GT911 0x41 read present at capture top); the 1014D's stock FPGA reports the same 0x1432 and PORT_AUDIT already validated the short-mode half of the same document on this unit's hardware (F25), so cross-applicability is likely but is itself a bench question.
6. This is the ACQUISITION half only — the F21 renderer findings (dual renderer + P14 geometry, reported above) must be fixed separately or roll will still look broken even with correct data.
```

**Coverage notes:** TASK 1: Read all 1412 lines of /home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/test.c and diffed it against the vendored Atlan4-1.00o5 copy (byte-identical — every internal defect is inherited; the 1014D-specific breakage is at the port seams). Traced the full roll path end-to-end: main-loop routing (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:391-445), sm_set_time_base long branch + rate push (sm_1014d.c:1430-1517), fpga_set_long_timebase/fpga_set_sample_rate/fpga_average_trace_data(+_long) (fpga_control.c:380-395, 576-601, 1433-1525), scope_preset_values long branch (scope_functions.c:257-287), scope_display_trace_data geometry+blit (3450-3800), scope_get_y_sample (3865-3913), sm writers of triggerverticalposition/traceposition (sm_1014d.c:1340-1392, 1601-1645), P14 trace-window constants (variables.h:143-177), rate/cal tables (variables.c:421-435, 794-825, 991-994; scope_functions.c:2326-2345). uint16-wrap sweep over roll state: count/xpos/lastx are uint32 (variables.h:768-770, safe); averager accumulators bounded (10*255 and 100*255 < 65535); the one live wrap is triggerverticalposition (reported; REVIEW-2026-08-21's annihilation argument re-checked and confirmed inapplicable to the roll compare). Checked the timeout table (cases 7/8 now unreachable after the F21 boundary — dead cases only, not reported), tracebuffer bounds (max index 2999 vs 16384-byte buffers — safe), timer tick = 1 ms (timer.c:15), touch-stub behavior on 1014D (touchpanel.c:185-244), dev_mode reachability, and link flags (no --gc-sections). Cross-checked against REVIEW-2026-08-21.md and today's f0561ee/aea6f8a to avoid re-reporting fixed items (the FPGA_NOTES fpga_set_time_base misattribution is already corrected; the tvp unclamped writer was explicitly left open for this pass). TASK 2: personally read the >=100ms stock captures — pecostm32-RE/'FPGA explained'/100mS_50S-div_signal_data_read_sequence.txt (all 84 lines), FPGA explained.txt (full), 50mS-div capture header (short-mode 0x0F/0x28 cycle for contrast), Startup sequence 0x0D/0x0F/0x28 hits, parameter_analysis.txt, readme.txt — and verified fpga_write_int is MSB-first so 0x000007D0 matches the capture byte order, and averagecommand=0x24/0x26 (scope_functions.c:7389/7419). Not done: no build (make forbidden), no hardware verification (all roll behavior claims are code+capture-derived; F21 symptoms themselves are bench-recorded in PORT_AUDIT), no findings reported on vendored trees (used strictly as references), 1013D behavior left untouched per policy. The proposal below is a design only — nothing was applied.

## USB mass-storage / IRQ concurrency

### MSC WRITE_10 larger than the staging buffer silently drops one 512-byte packet per buffer flush

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/mass_storage_class.c:502`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

In MSC_RECEIVE_DATA, the buffer-full branch (else at lines 502-543) flushes the accumulated data to the card with sd_card_write and repositions pointers, but never calls usb_read_from_fifo for the packet that triggered the flush. The IRQ dispatcher then unconditionally clears RX_PKT_READY (usb_interface.c:810 '*USBC_REG_RXCSR &= ~(USBC_BP_RXCSR_D_RX_PKT_READY | USBC_BP_RXCSR_D_OVERRUN);'), which discards that packet's 512 bytes from the double-buffered FIFO. scsi_byte_count is also never decremented for the dropped packet, so the transfer-complete test 'if(length >= scsi_byte_count)' (line 466) can never fire: no CSW is sent and the host times out and resets the device. The staging buffer is sizeof(viewthumbnaildata) = 1000 x 409 = 409,000 bytes (struct tagThumbnailData, variables.h:858-875: 33+12+182+182 all-byte fields; VIEW_ITEM_TRACE_POINTS=182 variables.h:46), so 798 x 512-byte packets (408,576 B) are accepted and packet 799 of a single CBW is dropped. Latent in practice because common host stacks cap one WRITE_10 at 64-128 KB, which is why bench USB writes work. Inherited verbatim: file is byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5 (empty diff) and this region is identical to pecostm32's official 1014D mass_storage_class.c (diff shows only descriptor/mounted_to_PC/READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY differences).

**Evidence:** mass_storage_class.c:455-461 accept path reads FIFO only when 'tptr < scsi_data_end_ptr'; lines 502-543 (else branch) contain sd_card_write/memcpy but no usb_read_from_fifo call; usb_interface.c:807-810 calls usb_mass_storage_out_ep_callback then clears RX_PKT_READY regardless; mass_storage_class.c:421 'scsi_data_end_ptr = scsi_data_in_ptr + sizeof(viewthumbnaildata);'

**Suggested action:** In the buffer-full branch, read the triggering packet from the FIFO after flushing (into the buffer start, after preserving the partial-sector remainder) and account for it in scsi_bytes_received/scsi_byte_count. Since the same code is in pecostm32's official 1014D, treat as an upstream-inherited latent bug; at minimum document it in PORT_AUDIT/ROADMAP so a large-transfer host isn't debugged as an SD failure.

**Verifier:** Reproduced structurally: MSC_RECEIVE_DATA accept path reads the FIFO only when tptr < scsi_data_end_ptr (mass_storage_class.c:455-461) and decrements scsi_byte_count only there (496); the buffer-full else (502-543) flushes via sd_card_write and repositions but contains no usb_read_from_fifo, and the dispatcher clears RX_PKT_READY unconditionally after the callback (usb_interface.c:803-810 '&= ~(RX_PKT_READY|OVERRUN)'), discarding the packet. Buffer math verified: tagThumbnailData = 33+12+2*182 = 409 all-byte fields (variables.h:857-875, no padding), VIEW_MAX_ITEMS=1000 -> 409,000 B; 798 packets (408,576 B) fit, packet 799 hits the else. scsi_check_read10_write10 (161-210) imposes no size cap, and WRITE_10's 16-bit block count allows up to 32 MB per CBW, so the path is host-reachable. File byte-identical to Atlan4 (empty diff); diff vs pecostm32 official 1014D shows hunks only at lines 36-155/281-333 (descriptors, mounted_to_PC, READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY) — the 452-543 region is inherited verbatim.

### 1014D USB screen exits on any key or rotary event, ignoring the host's PREVENT_MEDIUM_REMOVAL (mounted_to_PC never checked)

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/menu_1014d.c:204`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

ui_setup_usb_screen() is: draw, usb_device_enable() (line 201), uart1_wait_for_user_input() (line 204), usb_device_disable() (line 207). uart1_wait_for_user_input (uart.c:78-82) returns on ANY nonzero key/rotary code, so a knob bump or key press while the host is mid-write soft-disconnects USB instantly, corrupting whatever the host had buffered (FAT metadata included). The MSC code maintains mounted_to_PC exactly for this (mass_storage_class.c:287-288, comment 'Use this command to block the return to scope mode if the host says so!!!'), and Atlan4's 1013D touch UI honors it (menu.c:329 'if (mounted_to_PC)' shows an error and refuses to exit), but the 1014D path never reads it — grep shows the only consumer is menu.c:329, unreachable on 1014D (touch_handler is only called in the #else branch, fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:445-448). pecostm32's official 1014D has the identical ungated wait (FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/user_interface_functions.c:197-203), so this is reference parity, not a port regression — but this tree already carries the flag and the 1013D precedent for the fix.

**Evidence:** menu_1014d.c:201-213: usb_device_enable(); uart1_wait_for_user_input(); usb_device_disable(); ... ui_sync_thumbnail_files(); — no mounted_to_PC test. uart.c:80-82: 'while((lastreceivedcommand = uart1_receive_data()) == 0);'. mass_storage_class.c:288: 'if(scsi_cbw.command[4] & 0x01) mounted_to_PC=1; else mounted_to_PC=0;'. menu.c:329 is the only reader.

**Suggested action:** Mirror the 1013D gate: after uart1_wait_for_user_input returns, if mounted_to_PC is set, show an eject-first message and loop back to waiting instead of disabling USB. Small, reference-backed (Atlan4 menu.c:329 pattern), 1014D-only change.

**Verifier:** menu_1014d.c:200-213 verified: usb_device_enable(); uart1_wait_for_user_input(); usb_device_disable(); ... ui_sync_thumbnail_files() — no mounted_to_PC test. uart.c:78-82 returns on any nonzero key/rotary byte. Grep confirms the only reader of mounted_to_PC is menu.c:329 (touch USB screen, shows error and refuses exit when set), and touch_handler is only called in the #else (1013D) branch of the main loop (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:446-449), so it is unreachable on the 1014D. The setter exists at mass_storage_class.c:288 with the quoted comment at 287. pecostm32's official user_interface_functions.c:196-209 has the identical ungated enable/wait/disable — reference parity, not a regression, exactly as stated.

### READ_10 SD-error path sends the error CSW and then keeps sending data, desynchronizing the bulk-only transport

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/mass_storage_class.c:368`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

In SCSI_CMD_READ_10, when sd_card_read fails (line 368) the code writes the FAIL CSW to EP1 (line 377) but does NOT break out: it falls through to 'usb_write_ep1_data((void *)scsi_data_in_ptr, cardsectorsize)' (line 381) and sets msc_state = MSC_SEND_DATA (line 396), after which the IN-endpoint callback streams stale buffer contents and eventually a second CSW. The host, expecting data first, interprets the 13-byte CSW as data; the transport is desynced until a BOT reset. This is deterministically host-triggerable: a buggy/malicious host issuing READ_10 with LBA past capacity makes sd_card_read return SD_ERROR_SECTOR_OUT_OF_RANGE (sd_card_interface.c:322-323), hitting exactly this path. Memory-safe (buffer pointers stay inside viewthumbnaildata) but a robustness defect. Identical in Atlan4-1.00o5 and pecostm32's official 1014D (inherited). The same missing-abort pattern exists in the IN-callback refill (lines 589-596: on error it sets the CSW status but still advances and keeps sending).

**Evidence:** mass_storage_class.c:368-378: 'if(sd_card_read(...) != SD_OK) { scsi_csw.status = MSC_CSW_STATUS_FAIL; ... usb_write_ep1_data((void *)&scsi_csw, MSC_CSW_LENGTH); }' with no break/return; line 381 then sends a data block; line 396 'msc_state = MSC_SEND_DATA;'

**Suggested action:** After sending the error CSW, set msc_state = MSC_WAIT_COMMAND and break (and stall the IN endpoint per BOT spec if being thorough). Same for the refill error at line 589. Low-risk, protocol-level fix; note as inherited-from-upstream in PORT_AUDIT.

**Verifier:** mass_storage_class.c:368-397 verified: on sd_card_read failure the FAIL CSW is written at 377 with no break/return; execution falls to usb_write_ep1_data(scsi_data_in_ptr, cardsectorsize) at 381 and msc_state = MSC_SEND_DATA at 396, so the host receives CSW-then-data — transport desync. Host-triggerable: scsi_check_read10_write10 (161-210) checks only direction/byte-count consistency, never LBA range, so an out-of-range LBA reaches sd_card_read which returns SD_ERROR_SECTOR_OUT_OF_RANGE (sd_card_interface.c:322-323). Same pattern in the IN-callback refill at 589-602 (sets CSW status, still advances counters and keeps streaming). Region identical to Atlan4 (empty file diff) and to pecostm32 official (no diff hunks beyond line 333).

### SD sector-range guard off-by-one (allows one sector past the end) plus uint32 sum wrap; flagged by its own comment

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/sd_card_interface.c:322`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

sd_card_read (line 322) and sd_card_write (line 393) both use 'if((sector + blocks - 1) > cardsectors) return(SD_ERROR_SECTOR_OUT_OF_RANGE);'. Valid LBAs are 0..cardsectors-1 (READ_CAPACITY_10 itself reports cardsectors-1 as the last LBA, mass_storage_class.c:300-306), so the guard admits sector+blocks-1 == cardsectors, one past the end; the card then rejects with an address error, feeding the READ_10 error-path desync above. Additionally 'sector + blocks - 1' can wrap for sector near 2^32 and pass the guard, but I traced the downstream consequences: SDHC gets cmdarg = huge sector (card rejects); SDSC byte-address wrap cannot reach a valid low sector because the guard's cardsectors ceiling (<= ~4M for SDSC) already excludes every sector large enough to wrap. So no wrong-sector access is host-reachable — only spurious accept-then-card-error. The code's own comment at line 320 says 'This might be wrong. Need testing with last sector!!!!!'. Inherited from upstream.

**Evidence:** sd_card_interface.c:320-323: '//This might be wrong. Need testing with last sector!!!!!\n  //Check if last bytes to read in range of the card sectors\n  if((sector + blocks - 1) > cardsectors)'; same at 393; mass_storage_class.c:300 'uint32 sectors = cardsectors - 1;' for READ_CAPACITY.

**Suggested action:** Change the guard to reject when blocks == 0 or sector >= cardsectors or blocks > cardsectors - sector (wrap-free form), and drop the stale comment. Applies to both variants (shared core).

**Verifier:** sd_card_interface.c:319-323 and 392-394 verified: '(sector + blocks - 1) > cardsectors' admits sector+blocks-1 == cardsectors, one past the last valid LBA, while READ_CAPACITY_10 reports cardsectors-1 as last LBA (mass_storage_class.c:300-306); the code's own comment 'This might be wrong. Need testing with last sector!!!!!' is at 319. Re-derived the wrap analysis: guard passes for sector near 2^32, SDHC passes the huge sector as cmdarg (card rejects); for SDSC the wrap-passing sectors give byte addresses (sector mod 2^23)*512 >= 2^32 - 65535*512 =~ 0xFE000200, far beyond any <=2 GB SDSC capacity, so no wrong-sector access is reachable — only accept-then-card-error, matching the finding.

### READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY answers with raw, possibly-zero READ_CAPACITY bytes in the wrong response format

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/mass_storage_class.c:327`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

SCSI_CMD_READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY (lines 327-334) replies with the 8-byte scsi_capacity buffer, which is only populated when the host has previously issued READ_CAPACITY_10 (lines 303-311); a host that probes READ FORMAT CAPACITIES first (Windows commonly does) receives 8 bytes of zeros from .bss. The SCSI response format is also wrong (spec wants a 4-byte capacity-list header plus 8-byte descriptor(s), 12 bytes minimum). Hosts tolerate a malformed/failed response here, so impact is cosmetic. This branch is an Atlan4 addition — pecostm32's official 1014D instead groups READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY with MODE_SENSE (diff vs FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_scope/mass_storage_class.c shows the case moved and reshaped).

**Evidence:** mass_storage_class.c:327-334: 'case SCSI_CMD_READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY: { usb_write_ep1_data((void *)scsi_capacity, sizeof(scsi_capacity)); ... msc_state = MSC_SEND_STATUS; }'; scsi_capacity is a zero-initialized global (line 150) written only in READ_CAPACITY_10.

**Suggested action:** Either populate scsi_capacity from cardsectors/cardsectorsize inside this case too (cheap), or reply with a proper 12-byte format-capacities structure; alternatively fail the command like the default case. Polish only.

**Verifier:** Core defect verified: this tree's SCSI_CMD_READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY (mass_storage_class.c:327-334) sends the raw 8-byte scsi_capacity buffer, a zero-initialized global (line 150) populated only by READ_CAPACITY_10 (303-311), so a host probing READ FORMAT CAPACITIES first gets 8 zero bytes; the SCSI response format (capacity-list header + descriptor, >=12 B) is indeed not followed. This branch shape is Atlan4's (file byte-identical to Atlan4; diff hunk 325,333d318 vs pecostm32).

**Correction:** One comparison detail is wrong: pecostm32's official 1014D groups SCSI_CMD_READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY with SCSI_CMD_READ_CAPACITY_10 (reference mass_storage_class.c:291-292, so its buffer is freshly populated before sending), not with MODE_SENSE; MODE_SENSE_6 is a separate case at its line 314.

### IRQ-context raw SD I/O is safe only by an unwritten invariant (main loop parked while USB enabled); ~400 KB bursts also stall the 1 ms tick

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/mass_storage_class.c:481`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

All MSC SD traffic runs inside usb_device_irq_handler (usb_interface.c:807/834 -> usb_mass_storage_out_ep_callback / usb_mass_storage_in_ep_callback), performing sd_card_read/sd_card_write of up to SCSI_MAX_BLOCK_COUNT = 400000/512 = 781 blocks (399,872 B) per call (lines 368, 481, 512, 589; mass_storage_class.h:55). This never collides with FatFs or the sd_command/sd_data globals today ONLY because every usb_device_enable() site immediately parks the main context: menu_1014d.c:201-204 (busy-poll in uart1_wait_for_user_input, no SD side effects — uart.c:44-58 is pure UART register I/O) and the 1013D menu.c:315/344 touch loop (tp_i2c only); the FatFs resync (ui_sync_thumbnail_files, menu_1014d.c:213) runs only after usb_device_disable() at line 207. Nothing in the code states or enforces this invariant — a future caller enabling USB outside a modal screen (background MSC has been floated on the ROADMAP) would race main-loop FatFs on the shared SD driver state and clobber viewthumbnaildata (the SCSI staging buffer, lines 352/420/481). Secondary: interrupt.c's flat non-nesting dispatcher (irq_handler, interrupt.c:31-67) means a multi-hundred-block SD burst in the USB IRQ blocks timer0's 1 ms tick; multiple elapsed ticks collapse into one pend, so timer0ticks/timer0_delay undercount during transfers (harmless while parked, but real).

**Evidence:** usb_interface.c:153 'setup_interrupt(USB_IRQ_NUM, usb_device_irq_handler, 2);' (registered from main() via usb_device_init, fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:145, USB_CH340 defaults 0 at variables.c:160); usb_interface.c:807/834 callback dispatch; mass_storage_class.c:368/481/512/589 sd_card_* calls; interrupt.c irq_handler loop calls handlers to completion with no nesting.

**Suggested action:** Add a comment block at the top of mass_storage_class.c and a paragraph in PORT_AUDIT or FPGA_NOTES-adjacent docs stating the invariant: 'USB may only be enabled while the main loop is parked in a modal wait; all SD/FatFs access is otherwise unserialized against this IRQ path.' No code change needed now.

**Verifier:** All factual anchors verified: MSC SD traffic runs in usb_device_irq_handler via the EP callbacks (usb_interface.c:803-834); SCSI_MAX_BLOCK_COUNT = VIEW_THUMBNAIL_DATA_SIZE/512 = 400000/512 = 781 blocks = 399,872 B (mass_storage_class.h:55, variables.h:53) at call sites 368/481/512/589; registration setup_interrupt(USB_IRQ_NUM, usb_device_irq_handler, 2) at usb_interface.c:153/204. Parking invariant verified at both live enable sites: menu_1014d.c:201-207 busy-polls uart1_wait_for_user_input (uart.c:44-58 is pure UART1 register I/O) and menu.c:315/344 (1013D touch loop); ui_sync_thumbnail_files runs only after usb_device_disable() (menu_1014d.c:207/213). Nothing documents or enforces the invariant. interrupt.c's dispatcher (31-67) calls handlers to completion, no nesting, so long SD bursts in the USB IRQ do stall timer0 ticks (multiple elapsed periods collapse to one pend).

### 1013D-only: power-loss ISR calls scope_save_configuration_data() from IRQ context, able to preempt an in-flight main-loop SD operation

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_scope/power_and_battery.c:113`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

On the 1013D variant (#if !PORT_1014D, guard verified inside both power_interrupt_init and power_interrupt_handler), the Port-E power interrupt (priority 3, registered at line 94) calls scope_save_configuration_data() at line 113 and then hangs in while(1). Because IRQs preempt main context, a power-off arriving while the main loop is mid FatFs/SD operation (screenshot save, view-file write) re-enters the SD driver's shared sd_command/sd_data state mid-command — the config save can fail or write garbage during the dying-gasp window. It cannot preempt the USB MSC IRQ itself (flat non-nesting dispatcher), so the USB-mounted case only interleaves between complete USB interrupts. Compiled out on the 1014D (no PORTE power IRQ registered), so this tree's active variant is unaffected. Inherited from Atlan4. Per repo policy (no 1013D behavior changes without hardware), this is a documentation finding only.

**Evidence:** power_and_battery.c:94 'setup_interrupt(PORTE_EINT_IRQ, power_interrupt_handler, 3);' inside '#if !PORT_1014D'; lines 100-118: handler body guarded '#if !PORT_1014D', calls scope_save_configuration_data() at 113 when viewactive == VIEW_NOT_ACTIVE, then 'while(1);'

**Suggested action:** Do not change code (1013D untestable here). Add a note in PORT_AUDIT.md's 1013D-variant section: power-loss config save runs in IRQ context and is unsafe if power fails during a main-loop SD write.

**Verifier:** power_and_battery.c verified: setup_interrupt(PORTE_EINT_IRQ, power_interrupt_handler, 3) at line 94 inside '#if !PORT_1014D' (guard confirmed in both power_interrupt_init and the handler); handler calls scope_save_configuration_data() when viewactive == VIEW_NOT_ACTIVE then while(1). scope_save_configuration_data -> sd_card_write(SETTINGS_SECTOR,...) (scope_functions.c:7494-7505), so an IRQ arriving mid main-loop FatFs/SD operation re-enters the shared SD driver state. Compiled out on 1014D; doc-only per policy — as the finding itself frames it.

**Coverage notes:** Read in full: mass_storage_class.c (619 lines), usb_interface.c lines 1-1436 (MSC IRQ handler complete; CDC handler skimmed — its 'f' debug-command bug is already REVIEW-2026-08-21 finding at usb_interface.c:1542, not duplicated), interrupt.c (complete), timer.c ISR, uart.c poll/wait functions, sd_card_read/sd_card_write in sd_card_interface.c, power_and_battery.c ISR, ui_setup_usb_screen (menu_1014d.c:164-214), sm_start_usb_export (sm_1014d.c:3025-3041), scope_setup_usb_screen (menu.c:267-360), main-loop dispatch guard (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:430-455). Diffed mass_storage_class.c against Atlan4-1.00o5 (byte-identical) and pecostm32's official 1014D (only descriptors, mounted_to_PC, READ_FORMAT_CAPACITY differ; all READ_10/WRITE_10/state-machine logic identical), and checked the official 1014D's ui_setup_usb_screen (same ungated wait). TASK ANSWERS: (1) USB service IS fully interrupt-driven — setup_interrupt(USB_IRQ_NUM, usb_device_irq_handler, 2) at usb_interface.c:153 (MSC is the boot default: USB_CH340=0, variables.c:160; CDC registration at :138 only if USB_CH340==1), and mass_storage_class.c performs raw sd_card_read (368, 589) and sd_card_write (481, 512) from IRQ context, up to 781 blocks (~400 KB) per interrupt. (2) YES, the 1014D USB view is a full serializer: sm_start_usb_export runs in main-loop context (dispatched from sm_handle_user_input at fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:441) and blocks inside ui_setup_usb_screen -> uart1_wait_for_user_input (pure UART 0xFF-poll busy-wait, no SD side effects) until a key arrives; usb_device_disable() precedes the FatFs resync (ui_sync_thumbnail_files), so no FatFs op can interleave with host sector writes. Other SD touchers while mounted: none on 1014D — timer0 ISR only increments ticks, only three IRQs exist (timer0, USB, PORTE-power), and the PORTE power ISR is compiled out on 1014D (1013D-only hazard reported as finding 7). Screenshot/config/acqprobe paths are unreachable while the wait loop holds. (3) SCSI bounds: no check in mass_storage_class.c itself; sd_card_read/write guard against cardsectors with an off-by-one and a wrap-tolerant sum (finding 4), but I traced that no host-supplied LBA can reach a wrong real sector or overflow memory — worst case is a card-level error return, which then triggers the READ_10 error-path protocol desync (finding 3). Read bursts are capped at SCSI_MAX_BLOCK_COUNT=781 (399,872 B); writes are capped by scsi_data_end_ptr = buffer + sizeof(viewthumbnaildata) with a pre-read check. (4) Aliased buffer sized: viewthumbnaildata = THUMBNAILDATA[1000], sizeof = 1000 x 409 B = 409,000 B (struct is all byte fields: 33+12+182+182, VIEW_ITEM_TRACE_POINTS=182); read bursts (399,872 B) and the write bound (409,000 B) both fit; no overlap with live UI while the USB screen draws, because all drawing completes before usb_device_enable() and the thumbnail catalog is rebuilt from FatFs after disable — safety is implicit, hence finding 6. (5) BOOT_NOTES.md:82 claim CONFIRMED from the LBA math: READ_10/WRITE_10 pass the host LBA unmodified to sd_card_read/write (mass_storage_class.c:339/368, 403/481) and READ_CAPACITY_10 reports cardsectors-1 over the whole raw card (line 300), so MSC exposes the raw device from LBA 0 — a PC can read/rewrite the MBR, the boot image at 8 KB (sector 16), and config sectors 708-710; the documented 'dd of=<usb-disk> bs=1024 seek=8' reflash route is real. Variant guards on this path verified: mass_storage_class.c/usb_interface.c are unguarded shared core (correct, both variants use MSC); menu_1014d.c/sm_1014d.c carry whole-file #if PORT_1014D guards after includes (lines 15 / 5537 and 15 / 3044); the 1013D touch USB screen (menu.c:267, with its mounted_to_PC gate and CDC re-enable on exit) is reachable only via statemachine.c:1179 whose entry point touch_handler() is called solely in the #else branch at fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:448 — unreachable dead code on 1014D (the compiled-but-unreachable variant-table issue is already a REVIEW-2026-08-21 finding, not duplicated). Not audited: sd_card_send_command internals/DMA, the CDC/PC_interface protocol beyond confirming zero SD/FatFs access in that path (grep for f_open/f_read/f_write/sd_card_* over cdc_class.c and PC_interface.c is empty), USB PHY/enumeration correctness, and usb_interface.c lines 1437-1757 (CDC EP2 TX tail) beyond the dispatch structure.

## CPU infrastructure layer (start.s / arm32.h / .ld / interrupt.c)

### D-cache enable is a silent no-op: MMU is never enabled, so ALL data accesses run uncached and unbuffered

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:57`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

main() calls arm32_icache_enable(); arm32_dcache_enable() under the comment 'Instead of full memory management just the caches enabled' (lines 55-57). arm32_dcache_enable() only sets the CP15 c1 C bit (arm32.h:71-75). Grep over fnirsi_1013d_scope/ confirms arm32_mmu_enable/arm32_ttb_set/arm32_domain_set/arm32_tlb_invalidate are defined in arm32.h (lines 59, 108, 117, 126) but never called anywhere; start.s never touches the M bit either. On ARM926EJ-S, cacheability/bufferability of DATA accesses comes solely from the page-table C/B bits, so with the MMU off every data access is treated as noncacheable AND nonbufferable — the C bit is latently set but ineffective. The I-cache, by contrast, does work with the MMU off on ARM926 (flat mapping, all instruction fetches cacheable), so code runs cached. Net effect: every load/store — trace buffers, the six ~750 KB display buffers, FatFs sector copies, USB FIFO PIO — is a single-beat DDR transaction, and every store stalls (no write buffer). Rough cost (estimate, not measured; CPU=600 MHz, AHB=300 MHz per ccu_control.c:14-18): memcpy.s uses 8-word LDM/STM so bulk copies still get AHB bursts (maybe 2-4x slower than cached), but per-pixel uint16 rendering in display_lib and all pointer-chasing pay full single-access latency, plausibly 5-15x slower than with a working D-cache. The per-frame trace-window copy alone ((2,48,705,432) = ~609 KB read + ~609 KB written) is >1.2 MB of uncached traffic per displayed frame — this is very likely the firmware's frame-rate ceiling and the single biggest untapped performance lever in the whole tree. Inherited byte-identical from Atlan4 (and same in pecostm32 upstream).

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:55-57 '//Instead of full memory management just the caches enabled / arm32_icache_enable(); / arm32_dcache_enable();'. Grep for arm32_mmu_enable|arm32_ttb_set|arm32_domain_set|arm32_tlb_invalidate over fnirsi_1013d_scope/*.c/h: only the arm32.h definitions match — zero call sites. start.s touches CP15 c1 only to clear the V bit (lines 52-54). diff vs Atlan4-1.00o5: start.s, arm32.h, interrupt.c/h, fnirsi_1013d.ld all IDENTICAL.

**Suggested action:** Three homes: (1) ROADMAP.md perf item — 'enable MMU + real D-cache' with the quantification above and the uncacheable-region map from the companion finding; flag it as the largest single perf win available. (2) AGENTS.md gotcha — 'the D-cache is NOT actually on; do not reason about cache coherency, and do not expect dcache_enable to speed anything up'. (3) Fix the misleading comment at fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:55 to say the D-cache enable is ineffective without the MMU (code change is one comment line, zero behavior risk).

**Verifier:** Verified: fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:55-57 comment + arm32_icache_enable()/arm32_dcache_enable(); arm32_dcache_enable sets only CP15 c1 C-bit (arm32.h:71-75); grep over all first-party .c/.h/.s finds zero call sites for arm32_mmu_enable/arm32_ttb_set/arm32_domain_set/arm32_tlb_invalidate (definitions only, arm32.h:59/108/117/126); start.s touches CP15 c1 only to clear the V bit (lines 52-54) and copy vectors — the M bit is never set. The ARM926EJ-S architectural claim is correct (TRM: with MMU disabled all data accesses are noncachable/nonbufferable; instruction fetches remain cachable with I-cache on), so the D-cache enable is an ineffective latent bit. start.s/arm32.h/interrupt.c/h/fnirsi_1013d.ld all diff-identical to Atlan4. Performance figures are explicitly labeled estimates; the structural claim stands.

### Hazard map for any future MMU enablement: regions that must stay uncacheable (FPGA PE bus, all 0x01Cxxxxx/0x01Exxxxx peripherals, DEBE-scanned framebuffer)

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/display_control.h:31`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

If someone later enables the MMU with a naive flat cacheable+bufferable 1 MB-section map to get the D-cache win, these break: (1) the entire sunxi peripheral space — Port E PIO at 0x01C20800 (fpga_control.c bit-bangs the FPGA parallel bus through PORT_E_* registers; cached/buffered access would batch, reorder, or satisfy reads from cache, destroying the strobe protocol), INTC 0x01C20400 (interrupt.h:13-36), CCU, SD0, USB MUSB FIFO/regs (usb_interface.h:80-81, FIFO word reads at usb_interface.c:271/304), TCON 0x01C0C000, DEBE 0x01E60800 — all must be mapped device/NCNB. (2) maindisplaybuffer (BSS, at 0x8026BC1C in the current ELF, handed to sys_init_display at fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:97): DEBE scans it from DRAM continuously; with write-back caching the rendered pixels sit in dirty lines and the panel shows stale content — must be uncacheable or write-through (or every render must clean lines). Importantly, DEBE scanout is the ONLY DRAM-side DMA agent in this firmware: I verified SD is PIO (sd_card_interface.c:969-979 'Send or receive the data using the cpu' — the DMA of the original code was removed; SD0_GCTL DMA bits only appear in reset masks) and USB is PIO FIFO (usb_read_from_fifo/usb_write_to_fifo, alignment-checked CPU copies), so no other buffer needs coherency management. (3) The vector page copied to 0x00000000 (SRAM, start.s:56-65) must stay mapped+executable. (4) Both BROM jumps — sm_enter_fel_mode's 0xFFFF0020 and the loader path — would need clean+disable first. None of this is a bug today (D-cache is effectively off), but the FPGA PE-bus item is exactly the kind of silent corruption that would look like an FPGA protocol heisenbug.

**Evidence:** fpga_control accesses PORT E data/config registers in 0x01C208xx space; interrupt.h:13-36 INTC at 0x01C204xx; display_control.h:8-37 TCON 0x01C0Cxxx and DEBE 0x01E608xx; sys_init_display(SCREEN_WIDTH, SCREEN_HEIGHT, (uint16 *)maindisplaybuffer) at fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:97; sd_card_interface.c:972-975 '//For larger blobs of data it uses DMA / //Send or receive the data using the cpu'; usb_interface.c:264-293 alignment-checked PIO FIFO copy.

**Suggested action:** Attach to the same ROADMAP.md MMU/D-cache item as its prerequisite checklist (flat map with: DRAM cacheable-WB, maindisplaybuffer's section write-through or uncached, 0x01C00000-0x01FFFFFF + 0x01E60000 device, SRAM page at 0 mapped, cache clean+disable before any BROM jump). Add one cross-reference line in FPGA_NOTES.md near the PE-bus protocol description: 'PE bus correctness currently depends on the D-cache being de-facto off; any MMU work must map 0x01C2xxxx device/uncacheable'.

**Verifier:** All cited facts verified: FPGA parallel bus registers at 0x01C20890/94/A0 in Port E PIO space (fpga_control.h:14-16); INTC 0x01C204xx (interrupt.h); TCON 0x01C0C000 and DEBE 0x01E60800 (display_control.h:7-37); sys_init_display(SCREEN_WIDTH, SCREEN_HEIGHT, maindisplaybuffer) at fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:96-97 with DEBE scanning DRAM; SD is CPU PIO with the 'original code used DMA' comment (sd_card_interface.c:970-975); USB FIFO copies are alignment-checked CPU PIO (usb_interface.c:264-293); vector page copied to 0x00000000 (start.s:56-65); FEL BROM jump 0xFFFF0020 (sm_1014d.c:2946-2964). maindisplaybuffer = 0x8026bc1c in the current ELF (nm). Advisory hazard map, factually sound.

### sm_enter_fel_mode jumps to BROM with the VIVT I-cache still enabled and never invalidated — stale-code risk for the FEL reflash workflow

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/sm_1014d.c:2963`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

The Factory-settings FEL path (sm_enter_fel_mode, sm_1014d.c:2944-2965) saves config, paints the FEL message, calls arm32_interrupt_disable(), and jumps straight to 0xFFFF0020. The ARM926 I-cache — enabled since main() and VIVT — is left on and full of lines from the RUNNING firmware image at 0x80000000+. The standard workflow then has sunxi-fel write a NEW build over those same addresses (data writes, which bypass the I-cache) and 'exe 0x80000000'. Unless BROM invalidates the I-cache on its own (not guaranteed by anything we control), stale lines from the old build can be executed after the jump — the classic 'flashed the new build but observed old behavior' failure, which this project is specifically sensitive to (intermittent-symptom discipline, F26/F27 history). I am NOT claiming this caused any past symptom — only that the hole exists and is closable for two instructions. Note arm32.h has enable/disable helpers only; there is no invalidate helper (ICIALLU equivalent: mcr p15, 0, Rd, c7, c5, 0). The bootloader's own F3 FEL path is a separate binary and out of scope.

**Evidence:** sm_1014d.c:2960-2964: 'arm32_interrupt_disable(); ... __asm__ __volatile__ ("mov pc, %0\n" ...)' with address = 0xFFFF0020; no cache operation between them. arm32.h:83-93 defines only arm32_icache_enable/disable; grep confirms no c7,c5,0 invalidate anywhere in first-party code.

**Suggested action:** Small code fix, low risk, hardware-verifiable via the existing FEL workflow: add an arm32_icache_invalidate() (and/or disable) helper to arm32.h and call it in sm_enter_fel_mode after arm32_interrupt_disable(), before the BROM jump. Also add a line to BOOT_NOTES.md's FEL section documenting the ordering requirement. Per the ask-vs-act calibration this is reference-backed and reversible, but since it touches the recovery path, flag it for the user rather than treating it as a silent cleanup.

**Verifier:** sm_1014d.c:2944-2964 verified: save config, paint message, arm32_interrupt_disable(), 'mov pc, %0' to 0xFFFF0020 — no cache maintenance anywhere between. I-cache is enabled from main() (fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:56) and stays on. arm32.h provides only arm32_icache_enable/disable (lines 83-93); grep finds no ICIALLU (c7,c5,0) invalidate anywhere in first-party code (arm32_tlb_invalidate is c7,c10,4 drain + c8,c7,0 TLB only). The stale-VIVT-line concern over sunxi-fel write+exe of a new build at the same addresses is technically valid and the finding is appropriately hedged about BROM behavior.

### IRQ infrastructure verified correct, but the discipline is implicit: SP reset on every IRQ entry permanently bans nesting, and USB mass-storage does multi-sector SD PIO with IRQs masked

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/start.s:118`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

Dispatch correctness (verified): interrupt.c's setup_interrupt bit math is right for all three registered sources — timer0 IRQ13/prio0 (timer.c:21), USB IRQ26/prio2 (usb_interface.c:138/153/195/204), Port E EINT IRQ39/prio3 (power_and_battery.c:94): PRIO word index (irq&0x30)>>4 and enable word irq>>5 are word-scaled pointer arithmetic on uint32* (correct: 13->PRIO0 bit26, 26->PRIO1 bit20, 39->PRIO2 bit14; EN0 bit13/bit26, EN1 bit7). irq_handler walks both pending registers, restarting numbering at 32 correctly; handler table is 64 entries and irq maxes at 63. The start.s frame is the u-boot pt_regs layout (72 bytes: r0-r12, user sp/lr via STM^, lr_irq, SPSR, old_r0) with the required one-instruction gap (mov r0,r0 at line 130) after LDM^ before touching banked registers, and returns via subs pc, lr, #4 — all correct ARMv5 form. Three implicit contracts worth writing down: (a) start.s:118 'ldr sp, _stack_irq_end' RE-INITIALIZES the IRQ SP to 0x81FDA800 on EVERY entry — safe only because CPSR.I stays set for the whole handler (hardware sets it, nobody clears it); if any future handler re-enables IRQ, the nested entry overwrites the live frame at fixed 0x81FDA7B8 and the outer return goes to garbage. (b) Because of that all-masked discipline, USB mass storage runs its whole SCSI data path in IRQ context — mass_storage_class.c:368/481/512/589 call sd_card_read/sd_card_write (multi-sector, PIO) from the endpoint callbacks invoked by usb_device_irq_handler — so during USB file transfers, IRQs are masked for milliseconds at a time and timer0ticks stalls (timekeeping jitter while mounted; inherited, works). (c) interrupt.c:44 'INTC_PEND_REG0[i] = 0': writing 0 to a pending register is a no-op if the register is write-1-to-clear (the likely F1C100s semantics), and would DROP interrupts arriving between the read and the write if it were write-value; it is safe today only because all three sources latch at the peripheral until acked there and so re-assert. Also for completeness: FIQ is permanently dead (F bit set in every mode word start.s:15-49, never cleared — arm32_interrupt_enable clears only bit 7; fiq: handler is 'b .'), and undefined/prefetch/data-abort handlers are silent 'b .' hangs (start.s:102-115) — a crash shows as a hard freeze with no diagnostic.

**Evidence:** start.s:117-133 (irq entry, sp reload, 72-byte frame, subs pc,lr,#4); start.s:15-49 (mode words 0xdb/0xd7/0xd2/0xd1/0xdf/0xd3, all with I+F set); arm32.h:33-44 (enable clears only bit 7); interrupt.c:41-44 (pending read then '= 0'); mass_storage_class.c:368 'if(sd_card_read(scsi_start_lba, scsi_available_blocks, (uint8 *)scsi_data_in_ptr) != SD_OK)' reached from usb_device_irq_handler (usb_interface.c:368+) via the ep callbacks.

**Suggested action:** AGENTS.md gotcha section, three bullets: 'never re-enable IRQs inside an interrupt handler (start.s resets the IRQ SP on every entry — nesting corrupts the frame)'; 'USB mass-storage file I/O runs entirely in IRQ context with IRQs masked — do not add timing-sensitive code that assumes timer ticks advance during transfers'; 'the INTC pending clear-write is a no-op/W1C — real acking happens at each peripheral; any future edge-style source must be latched at the source'. No code change proposed.

**Verifier:** Independently re-derived all of it. Bit math: (irq&0x30)>>4 word-index and enable irq>>5 on uint32* are word-scaled (interrupt.c:20-25); irq 13 -> PRIO0 bits 26-27/EN0 bit 13; 26 -> PRIO1 bit 20/EN0 bit 26; 39 -> PRIO2 bit 14/EN1 bit 7 — all as claimed, and exactly three sources registered (timer.c:21, usb_interface.c:153/204, power_and_battery.c:94). start.s: mode words 0xdb/0xd7/0xd2/0xd1/0xdf/0xd3 all with I+F set (15-49); IRQ entry reloads sp from _stack_irq_end (=0x81FDA800) on every entry (117-118), 72-byte u-boot-style frame, mov r0,r0 gap after ldmia^ (129-130), subs pc,lr,#4 return — so nesting would overwrite the fixed frame at 0x81FDA7B8. interrupt.c:41-44 reads pending then writes 0 (the W1C concern is correctly conditional). MSC SD calls run from the IRQ dispatcher with I set throughout (usb_interface.c EP callbacks -> mass_storage_class.c:368/481/512/589). FIQ: F set in every mode word, arm32_interrupt_enable clears only bit 7 (arm32.h:33-44), fiq: is 'b .' (start.s:135-136); undef/prefetch/data-abort are silent 'b .' (102-112).

### Stack map documented: six fixed mode stacks in the top 300 KB of DRAM; main() runs on the 50 KB SVC stack with the unused FIQ region as a de-facto guard; no plausible overflow threat

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/start.s:139`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

start.s:139-150 hard-codes six full-descending stack tops; the sizes are just the gaps between consecutive literals (nothing enforces them): UND top 0x81FB5000 (region below is open DRAM); ABT 0x81FB5000-0x81FC1800 = 50 KB; IRQ 0x81FC1800-0x81FDA800 = 100 KB; FIQ 0x81FDA800-0x81FE7000 = 50 KB; SVC 0x81FE7000-0x81FF3800 = 50 KB; SYS 0x81FF3800-0x82000000 = 50 KB (0x82000000 is the exact end of the 32 MB DRAM; first push lands at 0x81FFFFFC, in bounds). The mode dance ends in SVC (0xd3, start.s:47-49), so main() and the whole main loop run on the 50 KB SVC stack; SYS mode is never entered (its 50 KB is dead), FIQ is never taken (F masked forever, handler 'b .'), ABT/UND stacks are set but their handlers hang without pushing. What sits below: NOT the framebuffers — all display/trace buffers are linker-placed BSS ending at BSS_END = 0x8058A0E1 (from the built ELF; displaybuffer2 at 0x8040E4E0, channel1tracebuffer at 0x805860E0, maindisplaybuffer at 0x8026BC1C). Below the UND floor lie ~3.66 MB of unallocated DRAM, then the 512-byte SD-sector-710 staging page at 0x81BFFC00-0x81BFFDFF (display config + legacy boot byte + touch config), then a ~23.5 MB gap down to BSS_END. Threat assessment: an SVC overflow first consumes the never-used FIQ 50 KB (silent), then the live IRQ stack at 50-150 KB depth. Worst-case main-loop depth is nowhere near that: FATFS/FIL/FILINFO are globals (variables.c:196/318/320), FatFs LFN buffer is static (FF_USE_LFN=1), and the largest stack locals found are uint16 buffer[256] = 512 B (scope_functions.c:7513/7661) — inspected paths stay in low single-digit KB. The IRQ side uses one 72-byte frame + handler depth (USB mass-storage chain is the deepest) against 100 KB. Conclusion: no guard exists and none of the linker, which knows nothing about these addresses (fnirsi_1013d.ld allocates from 0x7FFFFFE0 upward only, no overlap check), would catch a collision — but with ~5.8 MB used of 32 MB the margins are enormous. Related ld quirk worth one doc line: MEMORY org 0x7FFFFFE0 places the 32-byte eGON header below the documented DRAM base (works on hardware; entry branch 0xea000006 jumps to _reset at exactly 0x80000000).

**Evidence:** start.s:139-150 literals (0x81FB5000/0x81FC1800/0x81FDA800/0x81FE7000/0x82000000/0x81FF3800); start.s:45-49 final mode 0xd3 = SVC with sp = _stack_srv_end; arm-none-eabi-nm on dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d_scope.elf: BSS_END = 0x8058a0e1; display_control.c:13 DISPLAY_CONFIG_ADDRESS 0x81BFFC00; variables.c:196/318 'FATFS fs; ... FIL viewfp;'.

**Suggested action:** Add as an AGENTS.md memory-map subsection (the table above plus the three rules: main lives on the 50 KB SVC stack; FIQ/SYS regions are dead weight usable as expansion/guard space; the stack addresses exist ONLY in start.s — the linker cannot detect a BSS/stack collision, so re-check nm output if BSS ever grows by tens of MB). No code change.

**Verifier:** Stack literals verified in start.s:139-150 and gap arithmetic recomputed: ABT/FIQ/SVC/SYS regions 0xC800 = 50 KB each, IRQ 0x19000 = 100 KB, SYS top exactly 0x82000000; final mode 0xd3 = SVC with sp=_stack_srv_end (45-49), SYS never entered, so main runs on the 50 KB SVC stack with the dead FIQ region directly below it. nm on the current ELF reproduces BSS_END=0x8058a0e1, channel1tracebuffer=0x805860e0, displaybuffer2=0x8040e4e0, maindisplaybuffer=0x8026bc1c — all below the stack region. DISPLAY_CONFIG_ADDRESS 0x81BFFC00 (display_control.c:13); FATFS fs/FIL viewfp/FILINFO globals (variables.c:196/318/320); FF_USE_LFN=1 (ffconf.h:116); uint16 buffer[256] at scope_functions.c:7513/7661; ld MEMORY org 0x7FFFFFE0 (fnirsi_1013d.ld:3) with the 0xea000006 header branch landing _reset at 0x80000000 (32-byte header verified in start.s:5-11).

### Alignment audit: BOOT_NOTES' STARTUP_CONFIG_ADDRESS quirk confirmed and sharpened (the word-store also stomps the touch-config byte at 0x1C); no other misaligned multi-byte casts in first-party code; 1014D build never even emits the access

- **Where:** `/home/jsantala/src/FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1013d_scope/variables.h:108`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

Verified ARM926 semantics for the deliberately unaligned '(uint32*)0x81BFFC1F' (variables.h:108): start.s never sets the CP15 A bit, so ARMv5 legacy behavior applies — no alignment fault ever. LDR from ...1F loads the aligned word at ...1C rotated right 24, putting byte 0x1F in the result's LSB, so menu.c:7082 'boot_menu_start = ptr[0] & 0x07' genuinely reads the intended byte (works by accident, as BOOT_NOTES.md already documents). STR to ...1F ignores addr[1:0] and stores the WHOLE WORD at 0x81BFFC1C — so the 1013D-chain writer statemachine.c:5686 'ptr[0] = boot_menu_start' puts the boot choice's LSB into byte 0x1C, which is TOUCH_CONFIG_ADDRESS (touchpanel.c:19, read as xswap when the config is valid, touchpanel.c:70/149), and zeroes bytes 0x1D-0x1F — including the 0x1F byte that BOTH readers (the rotated LDR above and Atlan4's fw0.04 loader, which reads it as uint8) actually consume. BOOT_NOTES.md line ~76 says 'read and write paths are asymmetric at machine level' — the sharper statement is: the write clobbers the touch-config word and the saved choice can never round-trip through byte 0x1F. 1013D-only path, no hardware here, doc-only per policy. On the 1014D build this is moot at machine level: objdump of the current ELF finds exactly one 0x81BFFCxx literal (display_control.c's 0x81BFFC00 validation) — neither the LDR nor the STR to ...1F/...1C is emitted (users are variant-guarded out). Hunt for OTHER misaligned casts came back clean: scope_functions.c:7613/7624/7670/7680 cast (uint32*)&buffer[8]/[40] on 'uint16 buffer[256]' — byte offsets 16/80, aligned provided the local array base is word-aligned (the type only guarantees 2-byte alignment; de-facto fine in this build since cal data round-trips on hardware, but worth a one-line comment if ever touched); diskio.c:177 '*(uint32*)buff' writes FatFs' aligned LBA_t pointer; usb_interface.c:264-293/297-326 FIFO copiers explicitly test 'buffer & 3' and drop to halfword/byte paths; all other uint32* casts target naturally aligned peripheral registers.

**Evidence:** variables.h:108; menu.c:7081-7082; statemachine.c:5641/5686; touchpanel.c:19 '#define TOUCH_CONFIG_ADDRESS (uint8 *)0x81BFFC1C' and :149 'xswap = ptr[0]'; start.s has no write to CP15 c1 except the V-bit clear (lines 52-54), so A stays 0; objdump -d fnirsi_1013d_scope.elf | grep -ci '81bffc00|81bffc1c|81bffc1f' == 1; scope_functions.c:7613 'ptr32 = (uint32*)&buffer[INPUT_CALIBRATION_SETTING_OFFSET]' with 'uint16 buffer[256]' at 7513.

**Suggested action:** Tighten the existing BOOT_NOTES.md quirk paragraph with the two verified specifics: (1) the store lands on the word at 0x1C and overwrites the touch X-swap config byte while zeroing 0x1F, so the 1013D boot choice cannot round-trip; (2) the 1014D binary contains no access to the unaligned address at all (confirmed by disassembly). Keep the existing prescription (redeclare uint8* if the byte contract is ever adopted). Optionally add an AGENTS.md gotcha: 'CP15 A bit is never set — misaligned word accesses silently rotate (loads) or drop low address bits (stores) instead of faulting'.

**Verifier:** All anchors verified: STARTUP_CONFIG_ADDRESS (uint32*)0x81BFFC1F (variables.h:107); reader menu.c:7081-7082 'ptr[0] & 0x07'; writer statemachine.c:5686 'ptr[0] = boot_menu_start' then sd_card_write of 0x81BFFC00; TOUCH_CONFIG_ADDRESS (uint8*)0x81BFFC1C (touchpanel.c:19) read as xswap/yswap at 149-150. ARMv5 semantics with A=0 (start.s only clears the V bit, never sets A): LDR from ...1F returns the ...1C word ROR 24, putting byte 0x1F in the LSB (read works); STR ignores addr[1:0] and stores the whole word at 0x1C, putting the choice's LSB in the touch-config xswap byte and zeroing 0x1D-0x1F — the sharpened 'never round-trips through byte 0x1F' statement is correct. BOOT_NOTES.md:77 carries the 'asymmetric' sentence. objdump of the 1014D ELF finds exactly 1 occurrence of 0x81BFFCxx literals (the 0x81BFFC00 validation) — the unaligned access is not emitted in this variant. scope_functions.c:7513/7613 cast claims and usb_interface.c alignment-checked copiers verified.

**Coverage notes:** Read in full: fnirsi_1013d_scope/start.s (151 lines), arm32.h (143), interrupt.c (69) + interrupt.h, fnirsi_1013d.ld (32), and main()'s init sequence in fnirsi_1013d_scope.c:47-120. All five infrastructure files diff-confirmed byte-identical to Atlan4-1.00o5/ (findings describe inherited code; no findings reported on vendor trees). Verified with greps that arm32_mmu_enable/ttb_set/domain_set/tlb_invalidate have zero call sites. Supporting evidence pulled from the built ELF (dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d_scope.elf): nm for BSS_END=0x8058a0e1 and buffer/symbol placement, objdump literal-pool scan proving the 1014D binary contains no access to 0x81BFFC1C/1F. Audited DMA agents for the MMU-hazard map: SD path read (sd_card_interface.c:960-1035 — PIO confirmed), USB FIFO copiers (usb_interface.c:264-326 — alignment-checked PIO), DEBE scanout of maindisplaybuffer — the only DRAM DMA. Traced the IRQ registration set (timer0/USB/PortE-EINT) and the USB-mass-storage-in-IRQ call chain into mass_storage_class.c. Checked interrupt masking discipline (I/F bits through all mode words and arm32_interrupt_enable). Alignment hunt: pattern greps for (uint32*)&/(uint16*)&/(int16*)&/*(uint32*) across fnirsi_1013d_scope/*.c/h plus manual triage of every hit; STARTUP_CONFIG semantics cross-checked against BOOT_NOTES.md (found already-accurate; sharpened). CPU/AHB clocks from ccu_control.c. Caps/limits: performance figures are order-of-magnitude engineering estimates (no measurements possible; no automated tests, make not run per ground rules); F1C100s INTC pending-register write semantics (W1C vs write-value) not silicon-verified — finding states both cases and why current sources are safe either way; did not audit the deep display_lib/scope_functions render paths beyond sampling access widths; bootloader binaries and the loader-side FEL path out of scope; 1013D-chain items reported doc-only per policy. Worst-case stack depth assessed by inspection of large locals + FatFs object placement, not by static call-graph analysis.

## tracked repo-root loader trees

### Building fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/ silently clobbers the active build's flashable SD image

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/Makefile:70`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

The tree's post-build step packages its loader-only image and then runs 'cp dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin ../fnirsi_1013d_scope/dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin' — the exact filename the active scope build produces as its bootable SD image (fnirsi_1013d_scope/Makefile:129-130). The copied image is the upstream 1013D loader chain only (SPL at image 0, splash at 0x4000/sector 48, 38472 bytes total) whose splash stage expects the scope program at sector 92 — which the current packaging never populates (1014D packs at 0x8000/sector 80, 1013D-Atlan4 at 0x5BC00). Companion step: fnirsi_1013d_sd_card_bootloader/Makefile:67 copies its SPL into ../fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/dist/.../fnirsi_1013d.bin as the seed of that chain. Failure scenario: user builds the 1014D scope image, then (following the repo-root README's provenance text, which describes building these three projects as the normal flow) runs make in fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/; the last artifact at the flash path is now a scope-less loader image; dd it to SD and the scope boots to the PECO splash then halts on the magenta/cyan eGON-check error screen (recoverable by re-dd, but it is exactly the 'wrong artifact left as last build' class CLAUDE.md warns about, arriving from an undocumented direction). Mitigating factor: the next scope 'make' fully re-seeds fnirsi_1013d.bin from the committed base blob, so the clobber only bites if these trees are built after the scope build and before flashing.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/Makefile:67-70: './flashfilepacker -o dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin -i dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen.bin -l 0x4000' then '# Copy to the scope directory' / 'cp dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin ../fnirsi_1013d_scope/dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin'. fnirsi_1013d_sd_card_bootloader/Makefile:67: 'cp dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d_sd_card_bootloader.bin ../fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin'. Splash stage expects scope at sector 92 (fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen.c:25 '#define PROGRAM_START_SECTOR 92') while current packaging overlays at 0x8000/0x5BC00 (fnirsi_1013d_scope/Makefile:126-130).

**Suggested action:** Doc-first (these are tracked upstream-heritage sources, better left byte-identical to upstream for provenance): add a warning line to CLAUDE.md's building section — 'never run make in the three sibling loader trees; fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen's post-build overwrites the scope dist's fnirsi_1013d.bin; if it happens, rebuild the intended scope variant last'. Only if the user prefers active protection, comment out the cp at fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/Makefile:70 with a note that it diverges from upstream.

**Verifier:** Reproduced from the Makefiles: fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/Makefile .build-post packs its loader at 0x4000 and then 'cp dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin ../fnirsi_1013d_scope/dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin' — the active build's flashable SD image name; fnirsi_1013d_sd_card_bootloader/Makefile seeds the chain into startup_screen's dist with the same name. PROGRAM_START_SECTOR 92 confirmed (fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen.c:25, sd_card_read at 94/131), while current packaging overlays the scope at 0x8000 (1014D) or 0x5BC00 (1013D) per fnirsi_1013d_scope/Makefile:120-131 — sector 92 is never populated with an eGON image (it falls mid-scope-blob on 1014D builds). The checked-in 38472-byte fnirsi_1013d.bin exists in startup_screen/dist. Mitigation accurate: the scope make re-seeds from the committed base blob each run.

### Three tracked loader/startup trees absent from CLAUDE.md and BOOT_NOTES.md (status now established: pristine upstream, provenance-only)

- **Where:** `CLAUDE.md:1`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

fnirsi_1013d_sd_card_bootloader/, fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/, and fnirsi_1013d_startup_from_sd_card/ are tracked at repo root but appear in no working doc: CLAUDE.md's module map omits them, BOOT_NOTES.md's chain description never names them (grep over repo .md files finds only PORT_A.md:38 — historical — and the provenance-marked upstream README text). Verified status: all three are byte-identical to the vendored pecostm32 1013D upstream (recursive diff clean, including dist/ binaries and nbproject/), every commit is pecostm32's own (2022-01-15..2024-01-07: a68e48c/43b0b64/0b74b68, e61ae88/8aaaafb, 07e993b), working tree clean, zero local modifications or orphaned fixes. They implement pecostm32's ORIGINAL 1013D boot chain (SPL sector 16 -> splash sector 48 -> scope sector 92), which predates and is incompatible with both shipped chains. Nothing in the active build references them (fnirsi_1013d_scope/Makefile seeds solely from the committed base blob, lines 126-130).
