### fnirsi_1013d_startup_from_sd_card/ is doubly stale: upstream itself flagged it unmaintained

- **Where:** `fnirsi_1013d_startup_from_sd_card/fnirsi_1013d_startup_from_sd_card.c:13`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

The alternative single-stage loader (no splash; loads the scope directly from sector 48 to 0x80000000) was already declared stale by pecostm32 in the upstream README ('this project has not been adapted for the new display configuration setup nor has it been tested with the latest code' — repo README.md:42): it never loads sector 710, so a scope program relying on the DRAM display-config staging block would run on fallback timing only. Its build is self-contained (objcopy + mksunxi only, Makefile:61-62 — no cross-tree copies), so unlike the startup_screen tree it is harmless to build. Recording this prevents anyone from picking it as a 'simpler loader' base without knowing upstream abandoned it.

**Evidence:** fnirsi_1013d_startup_from_sd_card.c:13 '#define PROGRAM_START_SECTOR 48', loads to 0x80000000 (lines 79-96), no DISPLAY_CONFIG_SECTOR read anywhere in the tree; README.md:42 (upstream provenance text) flags it unadapted/untested; Makefile:55-62 shows no post-build copy steps.

**Suggested action:** Cover via the CLAUDE.md one-liner (proposal); no code change.

**Verifier:** Verified: repo README.md:42 carries the upstream caveat verbatim ('this project has not been adapted for the new display configuration setup nor has it been tested with the latest code'); fnirsi_1013d_startup_from_sd_card.c:13 PROGRAM_START_SECTOR 48, loads header+payload to 0x80000000/0x800001E0 (lines 59-96); grep finds no DISPLAY_CONFIG/sector-710 reference anywhere in the tree; its Makefile .build-post is objcopy + mksunxi only, with no cross-tree copy steps — harmless to build, unlike startup_screen.

### Design proposal (loader-trees)

```
One-line status verdicts for CLAUDE.md (repository-layout section), verified 2026-08-21:

- `fnirsi_1013d_sd_card_bootloader/` — pecostm32-upstream 1013D stage-1 SPL (BROM sector 16; loads the splash program from sector 48 to 0x81C00000 plus sector-710 display config to 0x81BFFC00). Byte-identical to upstream, provenance-only — not the shipped loader, unreferenced by the active build; its `make` copies its output into the startup_screen tree's dist.
- `fnirsi_1013d_startup_screen/` — pecostm32-upstream stage-2 "PECOs sCOPE" splash + scope loader (loads the scope from sector 92). Byte-identical to upstream, provenance-only — **do not run `make` here: its post-build overwrites `fnirsi_1013d_scope/dist/Debug/GNU_ARM-Linux/fnirsi_1013d.bin` with a scope-less loader image** (rebuild the intended scope variant last if it happens).
- `fnirsi_1013d_startup_from_sd_card/` — pecostm32-upstream alternative single-stage SPL (no splash; scope direct from sector 48), flagged unmaintained by upstream's own README (never adapted to the display-config setup). Byte-identical to upstream, provenance-only, self-contained build (no cross-tree writes).

Shared note: none of the three builds either committed loader binary — `bootloader_base.bin` (Atlan4 v0.8, binary-only, 0x5BC00 layout) and `bootloader_1014d_base.bin` (md5-matched rebuild of the vendored `FNIRSI_1014D_Firmware/fnirsi_1014d_startup`) contain nothing from these trees, and their sector map (48/92) is incompatible with both shipped chains (sector 80 / 0x5BC00).
```

**Coverage notes:** Checked: full recursive diff of all three trees against the vendored pecostm32 1013D upstream (byte-identical, including dist/ binaries and nbproject/ metadata); git history and authorship of every commit touching them (all pecostm32, 2022-2024, present on both PORT_A and atlan4-base, working tree clean); read all three main .c files in full, all three Makefiles' post-build steps, linker-script origins; hashed and size-compared all four tree dist binaries against both committed loader blobs plus a byte-substring scan (whole-file and 512-byte-prefix) of both blobs — no matches; eGON header layout of both blobs; verified BOOT_NOTES.md's bootloader_1014d_base.bin provenance claim by exact md5 match to the vendored fnirsi_1014d_startup dist binary; confirmed the active scope Makefile (lines 121-130) references only the committed base blobs; cross-checked the trees' sector map (16/48/92, upstream scope overlay -l 0x9800 = sector 92) against BOOT_NOTES.md's shipped chains (sector 80 / -l 0x8000 for 1014D; 0x5BC00 for 1013D-Atlan4); checked repo-root README provenance (fork-status banner added 204201d marks the upstream build instructions as provenance-only). Skipped: line-by-line review of the ~40 shared driver files per tree (byte-identical to the read-only reference, so per ground rules nothing to report on their content); no builds run (policy); no analysis of bootloader_base.bin internals beyond confirming none of the tree outputs are embedded (its source lives in the untracked 'Bootloader fw0.02...' snapshots, outside this task). Per policy, no findings are reported on the vendored reference trees themselves.

## mechanical claim closures

### acqprobe_analyze.py never received the review's fill-geometry units fix; FPGA_NOTES implies it did

- **Where:** `tools/acqprobe_analyze.py:330`
- **Severity:** medium — **CONFIRMED**

REVIEW-2026-08-21.md's capture-geometry finding (its lines 321-327) corrected the ring-address-vs-interleaved-sample unit mixing and its suggested action explicitly named analyzer lines 196/325/326. aea6f8a applied the correction to FPGA_NOTES.md and ROADMAP.md, and f0561ee touched the analyzer - but only to add the CH1-enable divisor comment (lines 210-214); the four wrong prints remain and I reproduced them live against bench/acqprobe/2026-07-19-trigger-probe/: (1) line 196 '4096-sample ring spans %s per ADC' % si_time(4096 / rate) uses the combined interleaved rate - prints '204.8 ms per ADC' at 5 ms/div where the true per-ADC span is 409.6 ms (addresses tick at rate/2); (2) line 330 window end '(disp_start + info.get("samplecount", 3000)) % n' adds 3000 interleaved samples as ring addresses - prints 'display window ring 161..3161 (raw-750)' where the corrected window is 161..1661 (= raw-750..raw+750, samplecount//2 addresses); (3) line 331 'trigger at ~%d%% width' computes int(100*750/samplecount) = 25% - the corrected figure is 50% (750 addresses = 1500 of 3000 interleaved samples, matching the firmware's own settriggerpoint == samplecount/2 '50%' label); (4) lines 334-336 'the gap sits past the display's right edge' - with the corrected window end (ring 1661) the gap starts ~950-1750 addresses past it with fresh off-window data in between (5ms run: gap start 2616 = 955 past). Meanwhile FPGA_NOTES.md:137-138 says 'the analyzer's capture fill geometry section now reports them: (units corrected 2026-08-21 ...)' immediately followed by the corrected bullets - a reader will assume the analyzer's output matches the corrected units. The next bench run's output will contradict the doc it is supposed to feed.

**Evidence:** Live run (python3 tools/acqprobe_analyze.py bench/acqprobe/2026-07-19-trigger-probe): 'settings: 5ms/div (idx 16), 20 KSa/s (idx 12); 4096-sample ring spans 204.8 ms per ADC' / 'raw 0x14 trigger at ring 911 ; display window ring 161..3161 (raw-750)' / '-> pre-trigger fresh = 910 samples (750 on-screen, trigger at ~25% width)' / 'the 1481-sample gap sits past the display's right edge'. git show f0561ee -- tools/acqprobe_analyze.py: only the CH1-gating comment hunk. FPGA_NOTES.md:137-146 carries the corrected numbers (window 161..1661, 'mid-window, 50 %').

**Suggested action:** Apply the review's analyzer fix: line 196 use 4096/(rate/2); line 330 use info.get('samplecount',3000)//2 for the window end; line 331 report 50% (750 addresses = samplecount/2 interleaved pre-trigger samples); lines 334-336 say the gap starts past the readout window's right edge with fresh off-window data between (report the distance: (gstart - (disp_start + samplecount//2)) % 4096). Until then, add '(analyzer prints still pre-correction)' to FPGA_NOTES.md:137.

**Verifier:** Reproduced end-to-end. Live run of tools/acqprobe_analyze.py on bench/acqprobe/2026-07-19-trigger-probe prints exactly the four uncorrected outputs: '4096-sample ring spans 204.8 ms per ADC' (line ~196 uses combined rate; true per-ADC span 409.6 ms), 'display window ring 161..3161 (raw-750)' (line ~330 adds samplecount=3000 as ring addresses; corrected end 1661), 'trigger at ~25% width' (line ~331 computes 100*750/3000; corrected 50%), and 'the 1481-sample gap sits past the display's right edge'. git show f0561ee -- tools/acqprobe_analyze.py contains only the CH1-enable-divisor comment hunk (added before the nch computation). REVIEW-2026-08-21.md's finding explicitly named analyzer lines 196/325/326 in its suggested action. FPGA_NOTES.md:137-139 says 'the analyzer's capture fill geometry section now reports them: (units corrected 2026-08-21 ...)' followed by the corrected bullets (window 161..1661, mid-window 50%) — so the doc does imply the analyzer matches the corrected units while it does not.

### CLOSED/CONFIRMED: v0.8 loader FPGA-version wait is dead code (BOOT_NOTES claim re-derived independently)

- **Where:** `BOOT_NOTES.md:122`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

Re-derived the 2026-07-10 claim from scratch with arm-none-eabi-objdump -D -b binary -marm on fnirsi_1013d_scope/bootloader_base.bin (375808 B, md5 cc594b02e3d08609906b5cb3163e5d38). The image contains exactly two 0x00001432 literal-pool words (file offsets 0x13a0 and 0x126a0), anchoring two version-wait routines at exactly the claimed offsets: (a) 0x1340 (SPL region, unoptimized): sends cmd 0x06 via bl 0x1320, compares the readback against the 0x1432 literal, runs a counted-to-1000 delay loop, and retries forever (bne 0x1350); (b) 0x125dc (main stage, optimized/inlined): orr r3,#6 command write, reads two bytes, cmp r3, ip (ip = 0x1432 from 0x126a0), popeq on match, else 1000-count delay (subs/bne 0x12688) and b 0x125f0 forever. Caller search over the whole image: no branch of any kind (bl/b/blx/conditional) targets 0x1340, 0x1320, or any address in 0x125dc-0x12694 from outside the routines themselves; no fall-through is possible (0x133c is pop {fp,pc}; 0x125d0 is bx lr followed by literal words); a scan of every aligned 32-bit word for pointer-like references to either routine under plausible load bases (0x00xxxxx/0x80xxxxx/0x81xxxxx) found none (the only textual hits are font bitmap data). The adjacent routine at 0x126a4 is confirmed as the cmd-0x04 enable (orr r2,#4 after the bic-0xFF command pattern) and has exactly one caller (bl 0x126a4 at 0x29e7c) - matching BOOT_NOTES' 'branches only to adjacent FPGA functions' example. Residual caveat: a flat -marm disassembly cannot expose hypothetical Thumb-mode callers, but the image is uniform ARM GCC output and the pointer-literal scan came back empty, so the risk is negligible. BOOT_NOTES.md lines 117-128 stand as written, including the exact offsets.

**Evidence:** bl_base.dis (scratchpad): '1340: e92d4800 push {fp, lr}' ... '1370: e3530ffa cmp r3, #1000' ... '1384: e59f3014 ldr r3, [pc, #20] @ 0x13a0' / '13a0: 00001432'; '125dc: e92d4010 push {r4, lr}' ... '12608: e3833006 orr r3, #6' ... '1267c: e153000c cmp r3, ip' / '126a0: 00001432' ... '12694: eaffffd5 b 0x125f0'. grep for branches to 0x1340 and range 0x125dc-0x12694: only internal 'bne 0x12688' and 'b 0x125f0'. Single caller of adjacent 0x126a4: '29e7c: ebffa208 bl 0x126a4'.

**Suggested action:** No doc change required - optionally append a dated line to BOOT_NOTES' v0.8 section: 're-verified 2026-08-21: full re-disassembly reproduced both routines at 0x1340/0x125dc, zero call sites, no pointer references; CONFIRMED'.

**Verifier:** Fully re-derived from scratch: md5 cc594b02e3d08609906b5cb3163e5d38, exactly two aligned 0x00001432 words at file 0x13a0/0x126a0; routine (a) at 0x1340 (push {fp,lr}, bl 0x1320 command send, cmp #1000 delay, literal 0x13a0) preceded by pop {fp,pc} at 0x133c; routine (b) at 0x125dc (push {r4,lr}, orr #6 at 0x12608, cmp r3,ip at 0x1267c, bne 0x12688 delay, b 0x125f0 forever) preceded by bx lr + literal pool at 0x125d0-0x125d8. Branch scan over the whole image: no external branch targets 0x1340/0x1320 or 0x125dc-0x12694 (only internal bne/b and the SPL-internal bl 0x1320). Adjacent 0x126a4 confirmed as the cmd-0x04 enable (bic 0xFF / orr #4 at 0x126c0-0x126c4) with exactly one caller, bl 0x126a4 at 0x29e7c. Pointer-literal claim required care: a naive flat-base scan surfaces 0x8001246c-0x80012690 words at 0x1a47c-0x1a4d0, but these are ldrls-pc switch jump tables, and the image is multi-stage (eGON.BT0 SPL size 0x7a00 at file 0, eGON.EXE main stage at file 0x8000 loading at 0x7FFFFFE0) — under the true mapping (runtime = file + 0x7FF F7FE0) those entries resolve to the switch's own case bodies at file 0x1a48c-0x1a6b0, and a scan for the wait routine's real runtime range 0x8000A5BC-0x8000A674 finds zero references; SPL base-0 scan finds only the constant 0x1387 (=4999) in a literal pool. Dead-code conclusion and BOOT_NOTES.md:111-128 stand.

### seam_scan reports step positions 16-32 samples low (left-bin center instead of the bin boundary)

- **Where:** `tools/acqprobe_analyze.py:139`
- **Severity:** low — **CONFIRMED**

seam_scan bins the block (binsize=32) and ranks steps between consecutive bin means, but reports position 'i * binsize + binsize // 2' - the center of the LEFT bin - for a step that physically lies at or after the boundary (i+1)*binsize. Empirically (synthetic data): a hard step exactly at sample 1024 (a bin boundary) is reported '@1008' (16 low); a step at sample 1000 (mid-bin) reports its largest candidate '@976' (24 low). The section header ('position is samples from the raw trigger address') overstates the precision, and anyone comparing reported seam positions against window edges (750, 1661) inherits the bias. Two smaller notes: bin 0 includes the known-stale sample 0 (can synthesize a small false step at the first boundary - '@16' candidates appear in flat bench blocks), and with fully flat data the median step is 0 so ratios print 'inf x median' (guarded, cosmetic only).

**Evidence:** seam_scan([0]*1024 + [100]*3584) -> [(1008, 100.0, inf), ...]; seam_scan([0]*1000 + [100]*3608) -> [(976, 75.0, inf), (1008, 25.0, inf), ...]. Code: 'return [(i * binsize + binsize // 2, steps[i], ...)' at tools/acqprobe_analyze.py:139.

**Suggested action:** Report the boundary '(i + 1) * binsize' (optionally +/- binsize/2 as the stated uncertainty), start binning at sample 1 to skip the stale byte, and note the +/-1-bin resolution in the printed header.

**Verifier:** Code verified at tools/acqprobe_analyze.py:132-140: seam_scan reports '(i * binsize + binsize // 2, ...)' — the center of the left bin, while the step lies at/after boundary (i+1)*binsize. Synthetic tests reproduce the exact claimed values: seam_scan([0]*1024+[100]*3584) -> top candidate (1008, 100.0, inf) i.e. 16 low for a boundary step at 1024; seam_scan([0]*1000+[100]*3608) -> (976, 75.0, inf) then (1008, 25.0, inf), 24 low for a mid-bin step at 1000. The 'inf x median' flat-data behavior visible in the same outputs; bin 0 includes ring sample 0 (build_ring's stale slot); the section header 'position is samples from the raw trigger address' confirmed at line 291.

Positive closure of the rest of the task-4 review. (a) parse_txt matches the writer scope_functions.c:2046-2075 exactly: 'label=value\r\n' lines (acqprobe_line), repeated 'raw14=' collected into a list, 'ringdump_raw14=' as scalar; \r stripped by read_text/splitlines; all six bench acqprobe_*.txt files parse with every field consumed. (b) parse_bin matches the writer's ringdump block at scope_functions.c:2079-2103: 'ACQP' + six LE uint32 (version/timeperdiv/samplerate/rawdump/blocks=5/samples=4608) + 5 command bytes + 5x4608 data - offsets 4/28/33 in the parser line up, and all six bench ringdump_*.bin files load (23073 B, extra=0). (c) Tables: TIME_DIV_TEXTS matches variables.c:518 time_div_texts[35] entry-for-entry (including the duplicated 200ms-20ms long/short seam); SAMPLE_RATES' 18-entry index space matches sample_rate_time_per_div[18] (idx 0 = 200 MSa/s ... 17 = 500 Sa/s), cross-checked on bench data (tdiv 16 -> '5ms', idx 12 -> 20 KSa/s, and time_per_div_sample_rate[16] == 12 in variables.c:421); the p1b clock formula 800/(2*p1b+4) MHz matches clock_synthesizer.c's documented divider math ((P1+512)/128 = 2*p1b+4; 6->50, 5->57.1, 4->66.7, 3->80 MHz). (d) Ring math: build_ring starts at k=1 (skips the documented stale byte), covers all 4096 addresses from a 4608-sample dump (ring[rawdump] filled at k=4096; the 128 fallback is unreachable), and correctly mods raw 0x14 values >= 4096 (bench rawdump 5007 -> ring 911; raw14 lines up to 5086 handled); wrap_test/diff_count windows are exact (overlap len(b)-w); the W=4096 sample-0-only check is sound; longest_flat_run's doubled-array sliding window is a correct O(n) circular scan with the run<=n cap, and its outputs reproduce FPGA_NOTES' closed numbers (gap 2616..1 len 1481, pre 910, post 1705 on the 5ms run). One caveat to carry: build_ring assumes dump byte k sits at ring (raw+k) mod 4096, i.e. the stale byte occupied the ring[raw] slot; if the FPGA pipeline instead delays the whole stream by one clock, every absolute ring figure (trigger 911, fresh start ~1, window 161..1661) shifts down by exactly 1. Both models fit the observed b[k]==b[k+4096] wrap, and the standard firmware read path (fpga_read_adc_data, fpga_control.c:1078 - no first-byte discard) does not disambiguate. All relative counts (910 pre / 2507-1705 post / gap lengths) are unaffected.

**Evidence:** Writer format at scope_functions.c:2040-2103 (acqprobe_line loop, header[0..5], acqprobe_adc_commands, acqprobe_ring) vs parse_txt/parse_bin (analyzer lines 57-90). Bench cross-checks: acqprobe_tt5ms.txt 'timeperdiv=16 / samplerate_idx=12 / clock_p1b=6'; analyzer output '5ms/div (idx 16), 20 KSa/s (idx 12) ... 50.0 MHz (p1b=6)'; variables.c:421-434 time_per_div_sample_rate[16]=12; variables.c:518-533 time_div_texts; clock_synthesizer.c:110-118 divider comment. Ring: run output 'raw 0x14 trigger at ring 911', 'unwritten gap = ring[2616..1] length 1481', 'blocks 0 and 4 ... byte-identical over 4608 samples', 'W=4096 mismatches sit at sample 0 only'.

**Suggested action:** No code change needed for this part. Optionally add a one-line comment to build_ring recording the +/-1 absolute-anchor assumption (stale byte = ring[raw] slot) so future absolute-position claims (e.g. 'writer starts at ring ~0') carry the uncertainty.

**Verifier:** Positive closure spot-checked broadly and everything held: (a) writer scope_functions.c:2048-2075 emits 'label=value' lines incl. repeated raw14= and scalar ringdump_raw14=, matching parse_txt; (b) ringdump writer (2079-2100) = 'ACQP' + six LE uint32 + 5 command bytes + 5*ACQPROBE_RING_SAMPLES(=4608, scope_functions.c:1872) = 23073 B, matching parse_bin's offsets 4/28/33 and the bench files; (c) TIME_DIV_TEXTS matches variables.c time_div_texts[35] entry-for-entry incl. the duplicated 200ms-20ms seam; SAMPLE_RATES[12]=20e3 and time_per_div_sample_rate[16]=12 (variables.c:421-434) match the bench 5ms run; p1b formula 800/(2*p1b+4) matches clock_synthesizer.c:110-116's divider comment (6->50, 5->57.1, 4->66.7, 3->80); (d) build_ring starts k=1, covers all 4096 addresses from 4608 samples (128 fallback unreachable), mods rawdump 5007 -> ring 911; live analyzer run reproduces the closed numbers (blocks 0/4 byte-identical, W=4096 mismatches at sample 0 only, pre 910 / post 1705 / gap 1481 on the noisy 5ms run) and fpga_read_adc_data (fpga_control.c:1060-1090) indeed has no first-byte discard, so the +/-1 anchor caveat is fairly stated.

**Coverage notes:** All four assigned closures completed with hands-on verification; no caps hit. Task 1: full flat disassembly of bootloader_base.bin (65852 lines), literal-pool search for 0x1432 (exactly 2 hits), routine-body reads at 0x1340/0x125dc, exhaustive branch-target greps over the whole image for both routines and their helper (0x1320), fall-through inspection, and an aligned-word pointer scan under plausible load bases - claim CONFIRMED; residual limitation: -marm disassembly cannot see hypothetical Thumb callers (judged negligible: uniform ARM GCC code, pointer scan negative). Task 2: md5/cmp of the committed 1014D loader vs the vendor working-tree rebuild (identical) and vs pecostm32's git-HEAD-committed binary via git show (14335/27136 bytes differ); also re-verified the live fpga_check_ready call site (bl 0x1330 at 0x4bf4) and its vendored source. A rebuild was not attempted (out of scope per task; would need his exact toolchain to be meaningful). Task 3: scripted raw and non-blank diffs of all six section-1 import-fidelity files vs their named P14 origins, plus git log attribution of every divergence to documented commits; the seam-integrity/shared-core bullets of section 1 were NOT re-diffed (they claim guardedness, not byte-identity - outside the task's 'byte-diff list'). Task 4: line-by-line review of tools/acqprobe_analyze.py (383 lines) against the writer scope_do_acquisition_probe/fpga_dump_ring, live runs on both bench run-dirs (all 6 txt+bin pairs), firmware-table cross-checks (time_div_texts, time_per_div_sample_rate, sample_rate_time_per_div, p1b divider math), synthetic-input tests of seam_scan, and reproduction of the still-uncorrected units prints; confirmed f0561ee's analyzer hunk is the CH1-gating note only. Not run: make (per policy), the on-scope probe (no hardware). The fw1 '+3345 vs -750' branch asymmetry (implies a mod-4095 wrap while reads behave mod-4096) was noted but not pursued - FPGA_NOTES already flags it as 'a wrap at 4095+1' and it is a firmware/reference question, not an analyzer defect. Vendored reference trees were used read-only for comparison; no findings reported on them.
